# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Fixed

- **Legacy/observe GPU preferences can no longer strand a server.** A GPU disabled under Scheduler V2 can be persistently re-enabled while legacy, observe, or maintenance dispatch is active, with an explicit restart-required state that survives device polling and clears when the next process creates the owner. Repeated recovery requests are idempotent, fresh one-GPU installs still default enabled, and web, desktop, iPhone, TUI, and CLI now gate live lifecycle controls on authoritative V2 while offering only the supported **Enable on restart** action elsewhere.
- **Scheduler V2 now closes the remaining forced-local and churn gaps.** Equivalent feasible GPUs prefer greater sampled free VRAM with stable-ID ties; forced-local chains use the same frozen execution-plan and owner-thread construction/drop contract as batches. Partial dependency materialization failures are retried with bounded backoff, while auto encoder variants re-resolve only after a material sustained capacity change. Exact pre-grant telemetry churn yields after three replans instead of spinning inside one reducer turn. Forced-local owner pools always signal and join on invariant errors, and partial batch successes are saved with their exact per-item prompt and seed provenance before the aggregate error is reported. LTX-2 Gemma preflight uses the assigned worker ordinal rather than GPU 0.
- **GPU owner threads now shut down transactionally.** Any server initialization failure after GPU workers start requests typed shutdown and joins every owner before returning, while normal shutdown reports owner-thread join panics instead of discarding them. Panics anywhere in the owner entrypoint—including idle eviction and device-backed drops outside generation—now quarantine the context and store a fail-closed process-restart notification.
- **LTX-2 Gemma no longer allocates on an unleased sibling GPU.** Automatic prompt-encoder placement now uses only the GPU assigned to the stage when it fits, otherwise CPU. Previously it walked every visible GPU and could double-book a sibling owned by another scheduler worker; explicit `gpu` still means the assigned GPU and never silently moves.
- **The iPhone Models family filter no longer stays stuck on "All families".** The family taxonomy was fetched once when the Models tab first mounted, so a host that was unreachable — or whose API key was still coming out of the Keychain — left the picker with its single placeholder option for the rest of the session, and a failed catalog search was never retried either. Mold now re-drives the taxonomy and a failed search whenever the browsed host's address, key, or reachability changes, keeps the last taxonomy that did load instead of blanking it on a failed reload, and falls back to a session-sticky set of the families seen in catalog results and installed host inventories when the taxonomy endpoint cannot be read at all — accumulated rather than derived from the rows on screen, which would collapse the picker to the family already filtered on.
- **Desktop Library now uses native save language for gallery media.** The print-details action says **Save image** or **Save video** instead of the browser-oriented **Download**, while preserving the existing user-chosen file-save behavior.
- **New video sequences now start at a GPU-safe clip duration.** Web,
  desktop, and iPhone use the Plato-verified 25-frame default instead of
  treating the server's 97-frame format recommendation as a memory guarantee.
  iPhone memory failures now explain how to recover instead of repeating a raw
  CUDA driver error. LTX-Video sequences correctly use zero context overlap
  and say **Join clips**, while LTX-2 hides clip durations that cannot exceed
  its 17-frame motion tail. iPhone relaunch recovery also fails closed when a
  saved server identity can no longer be verified.
- **Desktop status now follows the host selected in Create.** A concrete host pick in the upper-right Create chip is immediately reflected in the sidebar status trigger and popover, including that host's GPU, queue, and loaded models while idle. Auto and Most capable continue to follow the host of the latest live job, then fall back to This device.
- **Expanded prompts no longer leave Generate looking broken after the model or route changes.** Desktop Create now surfaces the stale Batch 1 state immediately with explicit **Re-expand and generate**, **Generate expanded prompt anyway**, and **Restore original** actions; re-expansion starts from the original prompt instead of recursively expanding the rewrite. Generation and expansion errors use larger copy and expose a copy-to-clipboard control. Generation progress/result chrome on desktop, web, iPhone, and TUI resolves opaque `cv:` / `hf:` wire IDs through the installed-model inventory, so people see names such as “Juggernaut XL - Ragnarok” while requests and metadata retain the stable ID.
- **Web sequences now prevent incompatible LTX-2 pipelines from failing after submission.** Entering Sequence filters the installed picker to chain-capable video models and selects a compatible model when available; otherwise the guide links directly to Discover with Video + Models filters. The server exposes and enforces model-specific sequence support before creating a durable job, job/stage and action errors remain visible, active cancellation shows progress and rejects duplicate clicks, and settled cards can be dismissed. Discover no longer offers aggregate Hugging Face repositories such as `Lightricks/LTX-2.3` as runnable downloads, and pull failures from cards or details surface as error toasts instead of rejected promises in the console.
- **Web catalog tokens can now override server defaults.** A Hugging Face or Civitai token saved in web Settings takes precedence over `HF_TOKEN` / `CIVITAI_TOKEN`; clearing it immediately falls back to the environment-provided value. Environment-backed rows stay editable and identify whether the active masked token is the fallback or a saved override. The NixOS module now also accepts `civitaiTokenFile`, matching `hfTokenFile`, and documents both as overridable defaults.
- **CUDA model reclamation is now reset-free.** Normal model swaps, explicit unload, idle eviction, recoverable OOM handling, upscaling, and LTX-2 no longer reset the process-owned CUDA primary context, which could invalidate live Candle/cudarc handles on the same GPU or a sibling worker. Engines and device-backed caches are dropped under their assigned worker, pending work is synchronized through supported context operations, and admission re-samples actual reserve-adjusted free VRAM after the drop. Driver workspaces, fragmentation, retained allocations, and other-process usage remain unavailable pressure instead of being promoted back to nominal total capacity; fatal CUDA faults still quarantine the worker and stop the process for supervised restart.
- **GPU startup selection and legacy status are now fail-closed and driver-free.** `--gpus none` is a real maintenance mode: the server does not construct an ordinal-0 CUDA engine, start an inference queue, or accept generation/admin-load work. GPU builds with visible-but-unusable identity inventory likewise refuse inference instead of silently falling back to GPU 0; only a true CPU-only build retains the CPU correctness path. `metal:default` now round-trips anywhere the device API advertises it. Legacy `/api/status` projects only healthy, enabled workers, derives its memory line from the cached resource/device snapshot, and no longer makes CUDA calls on request; web and desktop routing/queue lanes use authoritative `/api/devices.schedulable` state on current servers and retain the status fallback for older servers.
- **The desktop status popover now shows every GPU on a multi-GPU host.** It previously rendered only the first GPU from the resources stream, so a job denoising on any other ordinal looked like an idle machine (e.g. one L40S at 0.4 GB while another carried the model). The popover lists one VRAM row per GPU (ordinal-labelled when there is more than one) and the sidebar mini-bar aggregates used/total VRAM across all GPUs, turning critical if any single GPU crosses the threshold.
- Made model identity legible across web, desktop, and iPhone: Installed and Discover rows now show a consistent model-type badge, mature entries carry an explicit `18+ NSFW` label, and model details use type-aware weight labels while surfacing available descriptions, tags, license, source, format, and popularity metadata. Civitai descriptions and presentation metadata now survive normalization and installation sidecars instead of disappearing after a pull.
- Aligned desktop and web Settings cards to the full content column, removed narrow-web overflow, and gave All settings groups clearer token-based color hierarchy with shared halide icon headers and summaries.

### Added

- **GPU lifecycle administration is now durable and scheduler-V2-owned.** Authenticated `PATCH /api/devices/:id` and `mold gpu list|enable|disable` expose the same stable-ID lifecycle: disabling removes a device from new scheduling immediately, lets active work finish, then drops device-backed state and joins its owner; desired enablement persists across restarts, and disabling every selected GPU leaves the coordinator and device API available in maintenance mode with zero GPU contexts. Re-enabling starts a fresh owner/context for that device, and lifecycle changes publish server events. Dynamic enable/disable is available only with `MOLD_DISPATCH_MODE=v2`; legacy/observe reject it, and a device excluded by the startup selector still requires a restart.
- **Added the internal Phase F0 batch/cancellation prerequisites; the public raw-batch API remains unchanged until F1.** Every production image and video family explicitly declares singleton native-batch support and safe cooperative checkpoints. Attempt-scoped helpers always clear cancellation before cached-engine reuse, and cancellation never interrupts a CUDA kernel or resets a context.
- **Added an internal, not-yet-routed server-batch transaction protocol for F1.** Its durable parent and publication journals fence stale lease generations, signal active-child cancellation, retry one nonfatal child failure, reserve final names by attempt owner, stage checksummed outputs privately, and publish all metadata in one SQLite transaction behind a gallery barrier. Recovery strictly replays legal state and publication events, rejects semantic journal poison, and accepts an atomic disk snapshot ahead of its journal only for the single-record replace-before-append crash window. Cross-process gallery bookkeeping prevents an ordinary save from losing its reservation or removing the reservation directory while a durable batch begins: recovery waits for a live ordinary publisher, while a process crash releases the OS authority so startup can reclaim the stale name. Clean galleries still shed empty bookkeeping. Startup recovery runs before serving, archived committed manifests remain authoritative without SQLite, and fault-injection tests cover each durable commit boundary. No existing client-generated sibling batch is made atomic by this prerequisite.
- **CUDA distribution now has RTX 3090-class and B200-class targets with fail-closed provenance.** Releases publish checked sm_86 and sm_100 tarballs and containers alongside sm_89, sm_80, sm_90, and sm_120; public binaries/crates wait for the full GHCR matrix and hosted Nix checks. Stable container aliases are create-once and verified, while automatic Lambda, RunPod CLI, and desktop provisioning consumes the exact release/source `@sha256` manifest instead of trusting a mutable tag. Main, source, and Nix clients use the explicit rolling `latest*` channel. The shared table now covers RTX 3050, all named GeForce RTX 50-series, and named RTX PRO Blackwell variants. Installer and updater inspect the complete `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES` fleet in any order, including MIG UUIDs mapped to their parent GPU capability, validate overrides against every visible device, reject duplicate checksum entries, and fail closed for missing assets or unsupported architecture floors. Release verification parses complete embedded PTX modules, ignores unrelated SPA/static target-like text, derives the full module target set, and rejects missing, incomplete, mixed, malformed, or wrong targets. Mixed 8.6/8.9 fleets select sm_86 because PTX may JIT only toward equal-or-higher compute capabilities; sm_89 is never routed backward to a 3090. The deferred two-part 3090 gate requires a hashed exact-sm89-ELF CUDA Driver incompatibility control without treating it as runtime qualification, then binds exact sm_86/sm_89 binaries and archives through release provenance plus `SHA256SUMS`, validates decoded image/video/chained-video output and the exact math-backend log, preserves hashed raw PID/GPU observations, and loads a hashed sm_86 PTX module extracted from the artifact itself. Its hermetic validator walks the checked-in JSON Schema and re-hashes every referenced evidence file. The sm_100 path supports B200/B300 accelerators on x86 hosts and remains built and simulated, not hardware-qualified; no Lambda hardware campaign was run. GH200, GB200, and GB300 Grace systems fail before image resolution because current Linux artifacts are amd64-only.
- **iPhone Create now supports explicit multi-clip video sequences.** The
  touch-native Single / Sequence switch starts with two required clips,
  filters to compatible installed video models, keeps duration and seed tools
  under disclosure, submits a durable job to the exact Keychain-authenticated
  host, and restores or cancels that job after navigation or relaunch.
- **Desktop Sequence is now a guided two-clip workflow.** Create requires a
  description for every clip, filters out incompatible video checkpoints,
  calls transitions **Continue motion**, **Cut**, and **Crossfade**, and keeps
  frame, seed, source, audio, and TOML controls under progressive disclosure.
- **Web Sequence is now a guided two-clip workflow and can render on remote machines.** Create starts explicit sequences with two plain-language clip cards, calls smooth/cut/fade transitions **Continue motion**, **Cut**, and **Crossfade**, requires every clip description, keeps TOML authoring under **Sequence tools**, and shows a clearer duration summary. Durable creation, authenticated event streaming, previews, and job actions now stay on the selected machine instead of silently falling back to the serving host. The reusable capability, duration, validation, and transition contracts live in the shared Studio package for native clients.
- **Web and desktop Library gain a Lightroom-style thumbnail-size slider.** The compact small-to-large control sits in the top toolbar, updates the responsive web grid or desktop's justified contact sheet continuously, supports native keyboard range input, and remembers the chosen pixel size on each surface.
- **Scheduler V2 admission now uses concrete request-aware execution plans.** Each eligible GPU candidate freezes the resolved model artifacts and identity fingerprints, materialized CPU/GPU placement, inferred precision/quantization metadata, planned load/offload mode, sampled free-VRAM peak, host-RAM increment, and an execution fingerprint. Request placement remains authoritative over environment-overlaid persisted defaults; explicit CPU and device choices are hard constraints, unavailable or cross-GPU pins reject, and automatic CPU placement is offered only under memory pressure on implemented engine-family CPU paths. GPU owners validate the exact device and artifact set before touching CUDA and return a typed plan-invalidated error instead of silently choosing a sibling or replacement artifact.
- **GPU dispatch rollout is now a real restart-time switch.** `MOLD_DISPATCH_MODE=legacy` remains the default until the Phase A–E cutover gates pass. Explicit `v2` owns the coordinator, leases, and rendezvous workers; `legacy` restores the prior dispatcher and depth-two worker transport without starting the V2 coordinator; `observe` keeps that same legacy ownership while running the pure V2 planner read-only at accepted legacy dispatch points and recording comparison logs/metrics. Invalid values fail before any GPU owner starts, and changing modes requires a process restart.
- **Rollback dispatch now shares V2's binary GPU exclusion contract.** Legacy/observe queue depth is tracked separately from execution ownership, so generations, utility work, and durable chain stages cannot overlap on one GPU; the owner acquires a wakeable fair claim only after dequeue, with the existing global three-bypass chain bound. Fatal and graceful shutdown stop dispatchers first, reject and settle accepted-but-unstarted work (including depth-two owner buffers), and retain poisoned CUDA caches for process exit. Observe candidates now come from concrete execution plans plus sampled VRAM and host-RAM safety headroom instead of fabricated all-GPU placements. Queue pause gates utility/admin work too, and capability authority reflects the runtime that actually started in CPU/maintenance modes.
- **Forced-local batches now use every admitted selected GPU that has available sibling work.** `mold run --local --batch N` resolves concrete component placement/offload plans against real discovered free VRAM and OS-available host-RAM headroom, assigns immutable scheduler lanes, and runs one owner thread per admitted device with no one/two-GPU ceiling. Oversized work and hard pins remain hard constraints; a single item keeps the existing best-free-GPU path.
- **Component placement can now use durable device identities.** `DeviceRef` adds the additive `{"kind":"device","id":"cuda:…"}` wire form, and configuration/CLI parsers accept exact `cuda:` or `metal:` IDs advertised by `GET /api/devices` in addition to legacy process-local `gpu:N` pins. Server requests normalize placement once before admission with explicit request placement taking precedence over environment and persisted per-model defaults; the worker pool rejects missing identities and cross-worker component pins instead of searching for or silently substituting another GPU.
- **Ordinary multi-GPU generations now use the scheduler V2 runtime coordinator.** Unstarted work stays in one authoritative host queue until an idle worker publishes a versioned Ready generation; the coordinator atomically matches and memory-reserves all immediately runnable work, then issues at most one fenced lease per device. Worker channels no longer buffer follow-up jobs, so two or more queued generations spread across every healthy GPU instead of silently serializing behind one card. Queue cancel, reorder, and GPU-target changes trigger immediate admission plus the 2-second sliding / 5-second maximum-delay replan window.
- **Servers now expose a stable read-only device inventory.** `GET /api/devices` reports every discovered CUDA/MIG or Metal device with opaque identity, process-local ordinal, lifecycle/health/activity state, loaded work, and nullable cached telemetry. CUDA builds enable NVML telemetry automatically and join it to CUDA's runtime-visible inventory by UUID, so numeric reordering and GPU UUID selectors work while cards hidden by `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES` never leak through `/api/resources` or its stream. The `nvidia-smi` fallback uses the same UUID boundary and converts its MiB values to binary bytes; legacy `gpu_info` therefore keeps reporting a real 24 GiB card as 24576 MB. MIG telemetry is accepted only from the matching MIG UUID and never borrowed from the physical parent; parent/profile fields stay null when the available NVML adapter cannot prove them. Legacy `/api/status` keeps its existing `gpu_info`/`gpus` shapes but now derives them from the same registry and 1 Hz sampler instead of spawning `nvidia-smi` per request. Schema v12 adds machine-wide `device_preferences`; unseen devices remain enabled by default and no row is written until a later explicit administrative change.
- **iPhone can automatically save generated images to Photos.** A default-on Settings → Photos option mirrors desktop's automatic remote-output saving without retaining large base64 generation payloads in the WebView: after a still completes, Mold fetches the authenticated saved gallery file and hands it to the native Photos bridge. Post-generation upscales save both the original and upscaled image; videos remain streamable in Mold Library.
- **iPhone image actions and Library parity.** Generated stills now open the full-screen print viewer on tap. The viewer replaces crash-prone image long-press handling with explicit native **Copy image** and **Save photo** actions, using the authenticated full-resolution bytes and add-only Photos permission. Mobile Library now carries desktop's persistent **New** badges. Desktop and iPhone Library tiles/viewers identify post-generation upscaled images with the same **Upscaled** badge, using persisted generation dimensions with a filename fallback for standalone upscales.

- **Create settings gain a one-tap "Reset to model defaults" on every surface.** The desktop inspector and iPhone essentials get a compact ↺ Reset beside the settings header, and web's Create controls get the same on both breakpoints (web's reset is undoable from its toast, and the phone Advanced sheet's own scoped Reset moves from a buried footer to the top of the sheet alongside the previously-dropped active-count badge) — restoring shape, resolution, detail, prompt strength, seed, and the Advanced groups to the selected model's defaults while preserving the prompt, model choice, and any prepared batch. The TUI already had its "↺ Reset to model defaults" row.
- **Live latent previews now develop on the web and iPhone Create canvases**, matching the desktop: during denoising the server's `preview` SSE frames render under the shared Develop grain, the blur tightening and the grain thinning as the print forms, with the develop bed adopting the submitted print's aspect ratio. Web keeps the progress ring + stage line until the first preview arrives and drops the preview the moment a job settles (nothing heavy is ever persisted); the iPhone bed appears above the existing status line only while a preview exists, so WebKit does no extra compositing during model load.
- **Web Create's seed section gains desktop's "lock last (seed)" affordance**: while the seed is Random, one click pins the most recent finished print's seed as a Fixed seed — the value survives reloads via a small dedicated storage key (`mold.web.lastSeed.v1`), and random-seed chain completions (which report no real seed) never feed it.
- **Desktop and iPhone Discover gained kind and sort filters, matching the web catalog.** A chip row narrows results to Models, LoRAs, CLIP, text encoders, VAEs, tokenizers, or ControlNet, and a sort select orders them by downloads, rating, or recency (`sort=` on `GET /api/catalog/search`). A non-checkpoint kind also hides installed and built-in manifest rows client-side so a LoRA search is not padded with checkpoints; entering the iPhone Installed segment clears the kind like the family filter.
- **iPhone model details now expose runnable quantization variants.** Variant chips are derived from the selected host's manifest siblings, show each checkpoint size, and repoint Pull to the exact selected `base:tag` without bypassing the existing duplicate-safe download lifecycle.
- **Desktop Create shows live RunPod spend beside an active pod-routed print.** The activity strip joins the job's snapshotted host URL to a running pod, then reuses the shared accrued-cost meter for a compact current-total and hourly-rate chip; local and ordinary remote jobs remain unchanged.
- **Metadata-DB reads no longer silently miss under concurrent access.** Connections now set a 5-second SQLite busy timeout; previously a reader that landed on another process's write lock or checkpoint (running the TUI beside `mold serve`, both sharing `mold.db`) got an instant `SQLITE_BUSY` that most read paths swallowed as "no value" — surfacing as settings, theme, or host-registry values intermittently reading back empty.
- **The TUI Library now merges prints from all machines**, matching the desktop's unified gallery: the local output directory, the connected server, and every host registered in Machines are scanned concurrently (each with its saved API key — remote gallery listing previously ignored keys entirely), cross-host copies of one print collapse by filename with the local copy preferred, and the header stays honest about sources (`{n} prints`, `· {host}` for a single remote source, `· all machines` once prints span machines, plus a dim `· k hosts offline` when a scan partner didn't answer). An offline host contributes nothing without breaking the merge, and entering the Library rescans automatically when the last scan is stale (>30 s) or a host was connected/forgotten. A new Details side panel on wide terminals shows the selected print's thumbnail, wrapped prompt, and Model / Seed / Size / **Machine** rows with `[r] Recall prompt` / `[u] Upscale` hints (the old bottom Selected + Prompt row is retired; the full-screen detail view is unchanged), and `/` filters by prompt, model, or filename without invalidating thumbnail caches. Recall/upscale/delete work on remote prints — upscale and delete route to the print's owning host, and deleting a print that exists on several machines removes it from all of them, saying so in the confirmation. Server-fetched image/preview caches are now host-scoped (`<host_id>__<filename>`) so same-named files on two machines can't collide.
- **The TUI animates with tachyonfx, behind reduce motion.** Switching workspaces fades the content region in (~160 ms) and a finished print sweeps its caption row in under the image — the only two motions, per the design spec's restraint budget, and both confined to text cells so terminal graphics protocols are never repainted. Setting `tui.reduce_motion` (or `MOLD_TUI_NO_MOTION=1`) disables all motion for byte-identical renders; the TUI remains fully functional without it. This completes design-spec gap G13 — the terminal is now the fifth Mold Studio shell.

- **Web and Tauri now share one frontend workspace.** Repo-root `bun.lock` and `bun.nix` pin the exact Vue 3, Pinia, Vite 7, Tailwind v4, TypeScript, Vitest, and formatting toolchain for `studio/`, `ui/`, `web/`, and `desktop/`. The browser-safe `studio/` package owns explicit-target HTTP contracts, platform adapters, Pinia runtime state, prompt cycling, and source fitting; Tauri-only packages remain in `desktop/`. Web now uses the canonical `/create`, `/library`, `/models`, `/machines`, and `/settings` routes, rejects incompatible current-server shapes with an upgrade error, and no longer accepts retired routes or persisted form/job schemas. Knip and architecture gates prevent dead modules, duplicate locks, and Tauri imports from entering the shared package.
- **The TUI Create workspace is redesigned to Mold Studio.** Parameters are now six essentials — Model (with its description as a dim line underneath), **Size** (`1024 × 1024`, `◀▶` cycles 1:1 / 3:2 / 2:3 / 16:9 / 9:16 presets fitted to the model's default pixel area, Enter types an exact `WxH`), **Detail** (steps as an 8-dot `●●●●○○○○` gauge), **Prompt strength**, **Seed** (mode + pinned value merged into one row: `◀▶` cycles random/fixed/increment, Enter types a value, `^R` kept), and **Batch** — above a collapsed **Advanced accordion** (`A` toggles; Enter/`→` expands one section at a time) with per-section summaries and an active-count badge: Scheduler & sampling (scheduler · expand · offload), Negative prompt (now an inline editor inside the section — `Alt+N` opens, expands, and focuses it in one stroke), Source image, LoRA, **Upscale after generate** (new: pick an upscaler to run on every print via the existing wire field), Output format, and a capability-gated Video section (frames/fps). The accordion state persists (`tui.advanced_open` / `tui.advanced_section`). The right column is the mockup's **Preview** (dim `◇` idle hint → braille spinner + "Developing… 12/28" + 22-cell bar + `pct · it/s · eta` → the print with a `model · seed · time · host` caption) over a 7-row **Timeline** — the glyph-styled session log (`•`/`✓`/`!`/`✗`, `★` for model loads, `✓ Saved <file>` per print, "— idle. no runs this session." when fresh) that keeps download/weight gauges pinned during pulls and honors the `tui.show_timeline` pref (collapsing, never squeezing, on short terminals).

- **The TUI Machines tab is now the full multi-host workspace.** The interim read-only queue snapshot is replaced by the shared Machines surface: a host list with **This Mac** always first and per-row status dots plus hardware detail lines (GPU · VRAM · backend · host:port), a detail pane with live telemetry (VRAM, models-disk storage, loaded models, uptime, version) and per-host queue lanes (`▶` running with elapsed + GPU ordinal, `●` queued with position), and a stepped `c` connect flow (address → optional masked API key → test) that dedupes by server instance ID and backfills the display name from the server hostname. **Enter** pins a machine as the sticky generation target (persisted; Enter again reverts to Auto) — a targeted host that is down fails with an error naming the host instead of silently falling back to local. `d` forgets a host (confirming that its saved API key is deleted too), `r` refreshes, and `x` cancels a queued job behind a confirm, surfacing the server's refusal for running jobs. Hosts persist in the settings DB at `tui.hosts.v1` with per-host keys at `tui.host_key.<id>` (sent as `x-api-key`, never in URLs, removed on forget); offline hosts stay listed and self-heal. The `^K` palette's "Connect a machine…" entry now opens the connect flow directly.
- `MoldClient` gains typed queue access: `list_queue()` wraps `GET /api/queue` and `cancel_queue_job(id)` wraps `DELETE /api/queue/:id`, backed by new `QueueJobEntryWire` / `QueueListingWire` wire types in `mold-ai-core`. The entry `state` stays a plain string and the additive fields (`gpu`, `target_gpu`, `seed_pinned`, `metadata`) default when absent, so older and newer servers interoperate; cancel errors carry the server's response body (e.g. the 409 already-running reason). Purely additive — no behavior change for existing consumers.
- **Web and Tauri now share one frontend workspace.** Repo-root `bun.lock` and `bun.nix` pin the exact Vue 3, Pinia, Vite 7, Tailwind v4, TypeScript, Vitest, and formatting toolchain for `studio/`, `ui/`, `web/`, and `desktop/`. The browser-safe `studio/` package owns explicit-target HTTP contracts, platform adapters, Pinia runtime state, prompt cycling, and source fitting; Tauri-only packages remain in `desktop/`. Web now uses the canonical `/create`, `/library`, `/models`, `/machines`, and `/settings` routes, rejects incompatible current-server shapes with an upgrade error, and no longer accepts retired routes or persisted form/job schemas. Knip and architecture gates prevent dead modules, duplicate locks, and Tauri imports from entering the shared package.
- **The TUI gains a `^K` command palette.** `Ctrl+K` from any workspace or focus opens a filterable launcher — the terminal translation of the GUI surfaces' ⌘K — covering navigation to all five workspaces and the chain composer, actions (toggle Advanced, randomize seed, expand prompt, prompt history search, help, quit), and one-keystroke switching between all eleven theme presets. Typing filters case-insensitively, Enter runs the selection, Esc closes; while open the palette swallows every key, so `q` filters instead of quitting. As part of the rebind, `Ctrl+K` no longer performs kill-to-end-of-line in the prompt fields.
- **The TUI ships the four Mold Studio themes.** Studio Dark, Studio Light, Safelight Dark, and Safelight Light join the seven existing presets in the Appearance picker (now two rows of swatches), each carrying the design system's dual-accent model — safelight for focus/selection/primary action, halide for info/live state — via new `frame`, `surface2`, `faint`, and `info` theme tokens. Selection tints are pre-blended solids derived from the design mockup's CSS rgba values.
- **The TUI Settings workspace is redesigned to Mold Studio.** The Appearance swatch rows become bordered theme cards — three swatch dots (background, accent, info), the preset name, and a short palette descriptor per card — with the selected card marked by a focus-colored border, arrow-key navigation in two dimensions (Left/Right still cycle linearly), immediate live apply, and whole-row scrolling on short terminals. A new DB-backed **Preferences** section tops the Configuration list: default output format (`tui.default_format`, seeds a fresh session's Format parameter; saved sessions and per-model prefs still win), reduce motion (`tui.reduce_motion`), show Timeline on Create (`tui.show_timeline`), and confirm destructive actions (`tui.confirm_destructive` — turned off, deleting a print, removing a model, or deleting a chain stage dispatches immediately instead of opening the Confirm popup). Every toggle persists to `mold.db` the moment it flips.
- **The web Machines workspace can discover Mold servers on the primary server's LAN.** mDNS-enabled `mold serve` processes now keep a DNS-SD peer cache, advertise it as `discovery.can_browse` in `GET /api/capabilities`, and expose the auth-gated `GET /api/discovery/peers` endpoint. The web Add-machine modal enables Local network only when that capability is present, filters remembered instance UUIDs, prompts for each peer's API key when required, and test-connects from the browser directly before saving; older servers remain disabled without probing. `--no-mdns` / `MOLD_MDNS=0` disables both advertising and server-assisted browsing.

### Changed

- **Desktop and web toasts now drop in from the upper right.** Notifications and error messages slide down cleanly below the title bar / app nav — newest on top, older toasts nudged down with a smooth reflow, and a matching exit animation (web previously had none). Motion respects reduced-motion preferences.
- **Merged catalog searches now query Hugging Face and Civitai concurrently** and cache results per source and page (Civitai cursor-chain progress included) instead of caching whole merged result sets — a Discover page is as slow as the slower upstream rather than the sum of both, repeat and back-paging requests hit the page cache without refetching, and a filter or sort change still starts a fresh feed. Error semantics are unchanged: Civitai failures and HF auth failures (401/403) surface for credential retry, while other HF failures degrade the merged feed softly.
- Internal: the Develop grain canvas (`DevelopCanvas.vue` + `grain.ts`) moved from `desktop/src/lib/develop/` into the shared `ui/` design-system package so web and iPhone can reuse it; no behavior change.
- **Web Create sliders now match the desktop inspector's ranges.** Detail tops out at 60 steps (was 100) and Prompt strength at 12 with a finer 0.1 step (was 20 / 0.5), so the same drag lands on the same value on both surfaces; restored metadata beyond a slider's max still shows its true value. The shared Advanced section order (scheduler → negative → source → LoRA → upscale → output → video, plus web's trailing GPU placement) is now pinned by contract tests on both surfaces.
- **Desktop Advanced controls now expand inside the existing Create settings inspector.** The simplified essentials-only view remains the default; its Advanced toggle reveals capability-gated, icon-led sections directly below without dimming or covering the canvas. The nested section accordions are gone—every applicable control is immediately visible—and the web Create surface uses the same always-open icon treatment and explicit Off-or-upscaler picker.
- **Catalog compatibility is now a direct capability instead of an effort-tracking number.** The Rust catalog model, API wire shape, sidecars, CLI/server download gates, and web/desktop clients use `supported: boolean`; the obsolete `engine_phase` field and numbered rollout checks are gone.
- **Mold Remote now publishes and links its privacy policy.** The website has a dedicated policy covering on-device storage, Keychain credentials, user-selected Mold server traffic, third-party model catalogs, TestFlight, and deletion choices; iPhone and desktop Settings → About open it in the external browser for an App Store-compliant in-app disclosure and cross-platform parity.

- **Project attribution now consistently credits both core contributors.** Desktop, web, and iPhone About surfaces; native package metadata; Rust crate authors; website and README ownership copy; and AUR maintainer headers now name James Brink and Jeffrey Dilley, matching the MIT license.

- **TestFlight uploads now support external testers.** The iOS export no longer marks every IPA as internal-only, so the same validated build can stay in `Mold Internal` and be selected by an external group for Beta App Review; the iOS CI gate prevents that restriction from returning.

- **The TUI Create form dropped its Mode and Host rows** — routing is owned by the Machines workspace's sticky generation target (`mold tui --local` still pins the local engine), and the host-input popup is superseded by the Machines connect flow. The **Info sub-panel and Recent strip are retired**: telemetry lives in Machines and the chrome host chip, the model description moved under the Model row, and the Timeline's `✓ Saved <file>` lines absorb Recent. **Unload model** left Create (Models owns `u`), the old always-visible Negative panel (and its `tui.negative_collapsed` flag) gave way to the accordion's inline editor, and Width/Height merged into the Size row (per-model saved dimensions still round-trip unchanged).
- **The TUI chrome is redesigned to Mold Studio.** The tab strip is now two rows — dim workspace numbers, bold labels, and an accent underline beneath the active tab — with a live host chip on the right (`● This Mac · Metal`, status-colored dot for ready/connecting/offline, backend from the server's reported field with GPU-name inference for older servers). A new one-line **activity strip** sits above the status bar carrying the live job state: `developing · model · 12/28 · 2.0 it/s · queue N` while running (braille spinner), `done · 4.0s · saved to ~/…` after a print, the first line of the error when one is showing, and an honest idle line otherwise — unknown values are omitted, never faked. The status bar moves onto the chrome plane with accent-bold keys. Total chrome stays four rows, so per-view content sizes are unchanged; tab mouse hit-testing is now single-sourced with the renderer geometry.
- **The TUI now navigates the five shared Mold Studio workspaces.** Tabs are Create · Library · Models · Machines · Settings on keys `1`–`5` (previously Generate/Gallery/Models/Queue/Settings/Script on `1`–`6`), matching the desktop, web, and iPhone IA. The chain composer is no longer a tab: `c` opens it from Create (mirroring the desktop's `/create/chain` nesting), Esc returns to composing, and a chain in progress survives switching workspaces. `Esc` from any workspace returns to Create, and a new `A` binding toggles the upcoming Advanced disclosure. `scripts/tui-uat.sh view` accepts the new names with the old ones as aliases.
- **The default TUI theme is now Studio Dark** (previously Catppuccin Mocha), matching the desktop, web, and iPhone default look. A persisted `tui.theme` choice is honored unchanged; unknown or missing slugs now resolve to Studio Dark. Mocha and the other six legacy presets remain selectable, and `studio` / `safelight` are accepted as slug aliases for the dark variants.
- **Dracula's focused-panel border is now purple** (its accent hue) instead of cyan; the cyan moved to Dracula's new info role. Every preset now uses one hue for focus, selection, and primary action.

### Fixed

- **Opaque catalog IDs no longer leak through secondary UI surfaces.** Desktop, web, iPhone, and TUI now resolve queue jobs, loaded models, downloads, gallery metadata, templates, recovery prompts, and machine detail rows through the model inventory before rendering; if an older server has no title metadata, `cv:1759168` becomes the readable fallback `Civitai model #1759168` instead of exposing the wire identifier. API calls and persisted identity still use the stable `cv:` / `hf:` value.
- **iPhone tabs no longer inherit one another's scroll position.** Switching between Create, Library, Models, and Machines now starts the destination at the top instead of reusing the shared WebView scroller's previous offset.
- **Desktop's developing-jobs rail now uses the available sidebar height before scrolling.** A short queue no longer gets an unnecessary scrollbar from the old fixed 11rem cap; genuinely long queues still scroll while status and Settings remain pinned.
- **Web generation now works when Mold is opened over plain LAN HTTP**, including remote deployments such as `http://hal9000`: browser code no longer assumes the secure-context-only `crypto.randomUUID()` API exists and instead uses one shared native/Web Crypto-compatible UUID helper for jobs, prepared batches, drafts, and templates. The web Models Discover view also gains the desktop-style List/Grid control through a shared UI component, and filter/family hover text now uses theme tokens so links remain readable in Safelight light mode.
- **Web catalog credentials now belong to the serving Mold host.** Settings writes Hugging Face and Civitai tokens to the owner-only `catalog-credentials.json` under `MOLD_HOME`, reads back masked status only, and honors environment variables first; browser-local tokens and per-request forwarding are retired on web. Catalog lookup, pull, repair, and deferred model installation all use the same resolver. A rejected gated-model pull now validates the primary credential before enqueueing companions, so a 401 cannot leave a misleading partial download running.
- **Discover's "All" source now paginates Civitai results to the end on desktop and iPhone.** Under source=All the server splits each page's row budget across Hugging Face and Civitai, so a merged page legitimately arrives short whenever one source has no rows for the active filters (e.g. ControlNet, which HF never carries). The desktop and iPhone catalog views treated any short page as feed exhaustion (`entries.length === PAGE_SIZE`) and stopped after one page — soloing Civitai showed far more rows than All. Both now trust the wire `total` for `hasMore`, matching the web surface, with the old full-page heuristic kept only as a fallback for older servers that omit `total`.
- **Docker image builds work again.** The release Dockerfile's final stage still copied the web SPA from the pre-workspace `/web/dist` path; the web-builder stage has built into `/frontend/web/dist` since the frontend workspace consolidation, so every `docker` release job failed with `"/web/dist": not found`.
- **Civitai Discover pages past row 100 are reachable again.** The live-catalog proxy now paginates Civitai with its real cursor chain — one `page_size` window per step, resuming from the stored `nextCursor` — instead of the previous `limit = page × page_size` window, whose upstream cap of 100 silently ended the feed at row 100 and made every deeper page cost the whole window again. Locally-filtered rows (NSFW, family/kind drift) are backfilled from the next window so pages stay full-length, and totals keep `hasMore` honest until the feed is genuinely exhausted.
- **Linux desktop scrolling and window controls work again.** The shell no longer applies its WebKit elastic-overscroll suppression to WebKitGTK, where the newly supported `overscroll-behavior: none` trapped wheel input in nested Library, Models, Machines, Settings, and drawer scrollers; macOS keeps the native-app bounce suppression. The native Window menu now supplies working Minimize, Maximize/Restore, and Close Window commands instead of Tauri's Linux-unsupported predefined items rendering an empty submenu.
- **Corrupt gallery metadata databases now self-heal instead of breaking every Library refresh.** On startup mold runs SQLite's `quick_check`; corruption detected there or by an indexed gallery query copies `mold.db` with its WAL/SHM sidecars to `mold.db.corrupt-<timestamp>*`, recreates the live schema through SQLite's coordinated online-backup API, reconciles gallery rows from the files on disk, and retries the query. A cross-process recovery lock prevents the server, CLI, TUI, and Discord bot from racing rebuilds; unrelated lock, permission, and I/O errors still surface normally.
- **The NixOS desktop package now puts the NVIDIA driver RUNPATH on the real CUDA-linked application binary.** The post-fixup discovers every ELF that directly needs `libcuda.so.1` instead of patching the `makeBinaryWrapper` launcher, and both package fixup and the desktop closure check assert that each consumer carries `/run/opengl-driver/lib`. ([#518](https://github.com/utensils/mold/issues/518))
- **Installed Civitai models now show their human-readable names everywhere.** `/api/models` rows for catalog installs carry an additive `display_name` (the sidecar's upstream title, e.g. "Juggernaut XL - Ragnarok" instead of `cv:1759168`), and the desktop, iPhone, and web Create pickers, Installed lists, host detail pages, detail drawers, search filters, and toasts render it — falling back to the description on older servers. Every API call, selection value, and key still uses the raw `name`, and `cv:`/`hf:` ids are no longer mis-split into fake `base:tag` quant chips.
- **Civitai catalog pagination actually advances now.** Civitai's `/api/v1/models` ignores the `page=` parameter (it is cursor-paginated), so every "next page" request returned the same first window and the Discover feed could never grow past its opening results. The live-catalog proxy now rides Civitai's cursor pagination and never sends `page=` upstream.
- **Desktop and iPhone model discovery replace the "Load more" button with infinite scroll.** The Discover list now auto-loads the next page as the end of the list approaches; the manual button — which read as a dead end and looked broken while upstream pagination repeated itself — is gone on both surfaces.
- **Web and desktop Create now enable sequences for installed LTX-2 catalog checkpoints with opaque `cv:` / `hf:` IDs.** Client routing recognizes that catalog checkpoints without bundled spatial upscalers use the chain-capable one-stage pipeline instead of requiring `distilled` in the stable ID, and `GET /api/capabilities/chain-limits` now resolves installed catalog metadata rather than returning 404.
- **iPhone resume reconciliation is hardened against pre-ID races and localized transport errors.** Jobs now carry a structured interruption marker and submission timestamp; pre-ID queue cleanup only joins rows created in that submission window, and pre-ID durable video chains recover through the chain registry. Interrupted streams no longer fire a raw background failure notification before foreground reconciliation establishes the server-side outcome.
- **Web Create now preflights VRAM and keeps long-running work recoverable.** The composer estimates peak GPU memory against the selected machine, LTX-2 exposes the seven camera-motion presets plus custom LoRA paths, generation SSE is capped at four concurrent browser connections, and the last durable sequence job reattaches after a reload.
- **Web Library and shell parity is complete.** Library can filter by owning machine, refreshes itself while visible, and exposes Open / Reuse / Use as source / Delete from each tile's context menu. The app chrome now reports an unreachable serving engine globally, mobile Create controls honor a 44px interaction floor, storage sizes use one decimal-GB formatter, and the expanded command palette ranks prefix and synonym matches across workspace sub-surfaces.
- **The web phone Create flow now follows the approved prototype order exactly:** title, prompt and style, model, core controls, Generate, canvas, then recent prints. The model and controls no longer render in a detached column below the output on narrow screens.
- **Desktop navigation now fully retires the pre-Studio Jobs workspace and hidden shortcut aliases.** Queue controls remain on each machine's detail screen, legacy `/jobs` links redirect to Machines, and the unadvertised `Cmd/Ctrl+5` and `Cmd/Ctrl+6` aliases no longer intercept keys.
- **Web Settings now consumes the complete engine configuration surface.** Searchable, provenance-tagged editors cover storage paths, server port, generation defaults, metadata embedding, encoder variants, and prompt expansion; unknown keys from newer servers remain editable under Advanced. Environment-overridden values are visibly read-only, every writable key can be reset independently, and profiles can be switched or created without leaving the browser.
- **Web Models now stays current through the full pull/repair lifecycle.** Completed downloads refresh the shared Installed shelf instead of fetching and discarding a model list; component rows show ready/missing state and offer the server-advertised exact repair target; and a raced `MODEL_LOADED` delete returns an actionable unload-first message instead of raw `DELETE failed: 409` text.
- **Web Machines now exposes the controls its servers advertise.** Host detail can pause/resume dispatch, confirm and cancel all queued work, rename or forget a saved remote, and move only still-queued jobs between GPU lanes. Telemetry aggregates every GPU and reports real CPU and system-RAM use instead of a permanent `TEMP —` placeholder; shared status/capability types now carry the additive machine fields directly.
- **Web overlays now keep focus and scrolling contained.** The command palette, machine wizard, model details, confirmation dialog, mobile navigation, Advanced sheet, source picker, expansion dialog, and mask editor lock page scrolling, trap keyboard focus, restore the opener, and close on document-level Escape; Templates now dismisses on Escape/outside click, and catalog family filters are real 44px keyboard-operable buttons.
- **Web Library actions now preserve print identity and generation intent.** Reuse restores the selected model's family defaults; a forgotten remote host shows an unavailable-print state instead of loading a same-named origin file; the details panel scrolls and exposes steps, guidance, scheduler, LoRAs, negative/original prompts, format, size, timestamp, plus prompt/seed copy; and Create's lightbox **Upscale...** action enables the installed default upscaler instead of behaving exactly like **Use as source**.
- **Models → Discover now paginates the live catalog correctly.** `page` and `page_size` select a bounded, distinct upstream window, the response `total` describes the filtered feed instead of the current page, and the web client sends only the eight query fields the server actually accepts; Image/Video remains an explicit local filter rather than a silently discarded parameter.
- **Web Create restores the full model-specific input contract.** Qwen-Image-Edit exposes its required target/reference attachment path; the real GPU Placement panel is rendered and tested; SDXL guidance reaches 20; source fitting offers Contain, Cover, Pad + repaint, Lanczos, and Upscale + fit; ControlNet suggests installed checkpoints while preserving custom input; and still-image requests no longer leak source strength or video-only fields.
- **Web Create now owns remote work end to end.** Cancel sends `DELETE /api/queue/:id` to the job's snapshotted host instead of only closing the browser stream, queue reconciliation checks each machine's own queue, running work has a Cancel control, generation failures stay visible in the canvas, and source preprocessing plus prompt expansion use the selected route. Batch expansion now requests the exact number of prompts from `/api/expand`, preserves reviewed work when its prompt/model/family/host inputs become stale, and records one shared `batch_id` with one-based sibling indexes and the original prompt.
- `mold tui` / `mold serve` builds no longer fail with a vite `Plugin<any> is not assignable to PluginOption` type explosion on checkouts that predate the unified frontend workspace: `scripts/ensure-web-dist.sh` now removes a leftover per-package `web/node_modules` (detected by it containing its own vite copy) before building, so TypeScript resolves exactly one vite/rollup toolchain from the repo-root workspace.

- **Metadata-DB access is now safe under concurrent processes.** Connections set a 5-second SQLite busy timeout (previously an instant `SQLITE_BUSY` that most read paths swallowed as "no value"), and schema migrations now run under an immediate transaction that re-reads `user_version` inside the write lock. Before this, `mold tui` racing its auto-spawned `mold serve` over a fresh or not-yet-migrated `mold.db` could double-apply an `ALTER TABLE` — corrupting the file so every later open failed with "duplicate column" — or intermittently read settings back empty.

- **The web Library grid no longer changes size as lazy thumbnails load while scrolling.** Responsive tracks now ignore media intrinsic widths, and every print carries the same host badge plus hover/focus model and seed edge label as desktop Library.
- **Video-only LTX-2 catalog checkpoints no longer fail after denoising while trying to build an audio track.** Mold inspects installed safetensors for both audio-VAE statistics and vocoder weights, advertises the result as additive `supports_audio` model metadata, and disables generated audio in web, desktop, and iPhone Create when those assets are absent. Web rechecks that checkpoint-specific capability at the final request boundary so stale persisted audio preferences cannot re-enable it, and generation failures remain visible in Activity until dismissed instead of silently disappearing. Direct API requests are rejected with an actionable error before prompt encoding or denoising. Installed `cv:` / `hf:` component status now resolves through the catalog sidecar instead of incorrectly reporting a known model as unknown. If the LTX-2 Gemma prompt encoder exhausts VRAM, the retry now moves only Gemma to CPU while preserving the transformer and video VAE on CUDA; the previous fallback silently rebuilt the entire runtime on CPU.
- **Web and desktop model selectors now show human-readable catalog names.** Civitai and Hugging Face models keep their stable `cv:` / `hf:` identifiers for generation and routing, while Create displays the catalog-provided model name instead of the opaque id.
- **Transformer-only LTX-2 and LTX-2.3 catalog checkpoints now load separate version-matched runtime assets, including every stage of a multi-prompt chain.** Combined checkpoints continue to use their embedded `vae.*` and text-projection weights; catalog pulls add `ltx2-vae` or `ltx2.3-vae`, plus the standalone LTX-2.3 Gemma hidden-state projection required by diffusion-only/quantized exports. ConvRot W4A4 transformers are unpacked, unrotated, and full-streamed automatically so packed byte sizes cannot overcommit reconstructed BF16 residency. Native multi-prompt chains now accept one-stage checkpoints as well as distilled presets, while multi-pass/specialized pipelines retain their explicit rejection. Missing companions fail with a direct repair instruction.
- **The desktop Create settings inspector is resizable again and no longer wraps its shape controls by default.** Its left-edge divider supports pointer and keyboard resizing from 280–480 px, committed widths persist across launches, and double-click resets to the new 340 px default that keeps all five ratios on one row.
- **Repeated source-fit generations now reuse preprocessed images.** Desktop, web, and iPhone keep per-Create-form caches for the expensive Upscale then fit pass and the final canvas fit/mask. Prompt, seed, and generation-parameter iterations no longer rerun or retransmit unchanged source preprocessing; changing dimensions only invalidates the fit layer, while source, upscaler, fit-policy, and mask changes invalidate the layers they affect. Concurrent Batch siblings share an in-flight pass, and processed bytes stay request-local so the editable source remains intact.
- Keep the desktop Advanced LoRA picker inside its always-open icon section and ensure modal drawers cover floating workbench controls such as Templates.

## [0.20.2] - 2026-07-21

### Fixed

- **Models → Installed no longer lists models you don't have.** The shelf rendered every entry the server's manifest knows about — 97 rows on a machine with two models on disk — as though each were installed. It now shows only downloaded generation models, which also fixes the landing tab: a user with nothing installed was always sent to a fake Installed shelf instead of Discover.
- **The mobile navigation menu is usable again.** On phones the nav sheet rendered as a 52-pixel sliver at the top of the screen, hiding every destination behind it.

## [0.20.1] - 2026-07-21

### Fixed

- **Docker release images include the shared Mold Studio UI kit.** The container web-builder now copies repo-root `ui/` beside `/web` before running the SPA build, so `@ui/*` aliases resolve in the release-only Docker context instead of failing TypeScript compilation after otherwise-green web, desktop, and Nix checks.

## [0.20.0] - 2026-07-21

### Added

- `POST /api/expand` accepts an additive optional `style` field naming a visual style for the expansion. The server passes it to the expander as a natural-language system directive so style cues are woven into the expanded prompt rather than suffixed to it; requests without the field behave exactly as before.
- Queue reordering via `PATCH /api/queue/:id` `position` field (used by the Mold Studio activity strip and Machines queues). The additive field moves a still-queued job to the given 0-based index among the queued jobs (clamped into range); running jobs return `409`, and `GET /api/capabilities` advertises the control as `queue.can_reorder`. The reorder changes real dispatch order — the single- and multi-GPU dispatch loops align their lookahead buffer to the registry order each iteration — not just the `GET /api/queue` snapshot; the model-swap picker still runs on top of the reordered sequence within its starvation budget.
- **The web app gains a Machines workspace for multi-host monitoring.** A new top-level Machines view lists this server plus every remembered remote host, each with a status dot, live GPU / CPU / RAM telemetry, models-disk usage, and queue depth; a connect flow adds a host by LAN / IP / DNS address with an optional API key and a test-connection check. Clicking a host opens a per-host detail page with its full telemetry, queue, and installed models. API keys are attached as request headers, never placed in URLs, and hosts dedupe by the server's reported instance id so one machine reached several ways collapses to a single row.
- **Web downloads now open in a top-right popover.** The Downloads button opens an anchored panel (a bottom sheet on phones) with a Downloading section — model name, percentage, a progress bar, and a live transfer-rate and ETA line — plus Queued rows and a Recently installed list with per-model size. Active and queued pulls can be cancelled and failed pulls retried in place; ⌘K and the nav button share one open channel.
- **The web app raises cross-workspace notifications.** A finished generation shows a success toast (except while you are already on Create, where the canvas already shows it) and lights an accent dot on the Gallery nav pill until you visit the gallery; a model pull finishing or failing raises a toast; and a registered remote host going unreachable raises a sticky error toast once per offline transition and lights a stop-tinted dot on the Machines pill while any host is down.
- **The web Create canvas guides a first pull when no models are installed.** With nothing installed to generate with, the empty develop bed becomes a cold-start guide — a headline, a one-line explanation that models run locally on this machine's GPU, and a short list of recommended starter models, each with a one-tap Pull that shows its download progress inline and hands the canvas back to the normal empty state once the model lands.
- **Desktop RunPod pods now confirm before spending and show a live running cost.** Launching an instance or resuming a stopped pod opens a blocking confirm with the GPU, disk, and "billing begins immediately" warning (the resume confirm also names the pod's hourly rate) — nothing provisions on a stray click. Once a pod is running, a running-cost meter on the Machines overview and the RunPod view ticks accrued cost from the pod's uptime and hourly rate every 30 seconds, with Stop always beside it.
- **The desktop Create canvas guides a first pull when no models are installed.** When the merged model union across This device and every connected host is empty, the empty canvas becomes a cold-start guide — "Develop your first print." plus a short list of small starter models (a recommended one first) with one-tap Pull, inline download progress on the pulling card, and an automatic return to the normal canvas once a model lands. While the union is still loading, the existing loading state shows instead of the guide.
- **The desktop app raises cross-workspace notifications.** A finished generation shows a "Generated — saved to Library" toast that jumps to Library when you are anywhere other than Create (the native notification covers the backgrounded case); a model pull finishing or failing raises a toast; and a connected host going unreachable raises a sticky error toast once per offline transition. The Library nav item badges the number of prints developed since your last visit, and the Machines nav item shows a stop dot while any host is offline.
- **Desktop destructive actions now share one confirm-or-undo pattern.** Deleting a single print removes it from the grid immediately and holds it under a 6-second Undo toast before the real delete runs — undo restores it with no server call. Forgetting a machine confirms with blunt copy ("Forget studio? Its API key is discarded."), unloading a model takes a confirming second click, cancelling a job stays immediate with an info toast, and bulk delete keeps its existing typed confirm.

### Changed

- **New web visitors now start on the Safelight theme family**, matching desktop and iPhone defaults; a saved Mold choice is preserved, and the pre-paint bootstrap and browser theme-color follow the Safelight desk tone.
- **The iPhone Resolution tier is now a segmented control.** The native dropdown (two taps, options hidden in a picker) is replaced by the shared kit segmented control matching web and desktop: one segment per family-aware tier showing megapixels with the tier name beneath, a mono pixel-dimensions line under the control, 44pt touch targets, and the same Custom size flow.
- The `ios-dev` devshell command now defaults to booting an iPhone simulator (preferring `iPhone 17 Pro`, overridable with `MOLD_IOS_DEVICE`) instead of letting the Tauri CLI grab a plugged-in iPhone for a provisioning device build; pass a device name to target hardware.
- **The web interface is rebuilt on the shared Mold Studio design system.** Gallery, Create, Models, Machines, and Settings are restyled on the shared `ui/` tokens and primitives across every breakpoint, with the two Mold and Safelight theme families in dark and light. The always-on bottom resource tray is retired — GPU / CPU / RAM telemetry now lives in the Machines workspace and host detail pages — and the Create controls' run-on row reflects the chosen generation target and opens Machines, staying honest that generation runs on the serving host for now. The last legacy pre-Mold-Studio utility aliases and glass panels were removed and every remaining view migrated to the token utilities.

### Fixed

- **Catalog sort is now honored end-to-end.** `GET /api/catalog/search` previously ignored its `sort` parameter and always returned most-downloaded ordering regardless of the client's selection. The server now maps `sort=downloads` (default) / `recent` / `rating` to each upstream's native ordering (Civitai `Most Downloaded` / `Newest` / `Highest Rated`; Hugging Face `downloads` / `lastModified` / `likes`), keys the 5-minute in-process cache on the sort so two orderings never share cached results, rejects unknown sorts — including the retired `name`, which no upstream supports — with a 422 naming the allowed values instead of silently ignoring them, and advertises the vocabulary additively as `catalog.sort` in `GET /api/capabilities` so clients can feature-detect against older servers.
- **Desktop prompt expansion is now style-aware, and prepared batches no longer drop the style.** Expand and Batch preparation send the active style chip to the server as a natural-language directive so the rewrite absorbs the look instead of losing it (the literal preset suffix is never sent). A quick Batch 1 apply bakes the style into the rewritten prompt and clears the chip — undo restores it — while prepared batches keep the chip as the frozen-style indicator and name a style change as stale work. Reviewed prepared prompts still ship verbatim; the preset's negative fragments now merge into the outgoing request (after the user's own) for families that accept a negative prompt.
- **The iPhone app now recovers a generation interrupted by leaving the app.** iOS tears down sockets while backgrounded; on return the app re-queries the exact host the print was submitted to and settles the truth: a print that finished server-side renders in the result canvas as if the completion had arrived (with its seed and Library refresh), a still-developing print re-attaches with live progress, an orphaned queued row is cleared with a plain explanation, and durable video chains resume through their chain record. The reconciliation retries briefly while iPhone Wi-Fi re-associates instead of mislabeling a finished print as failed, and joins gallery rows by seed, model, prompt, dimensions, and steps so a fixed-seed re-run can never resurrect an older print.
- **The iPhone app no longer shows raw transport errors like "Load failed".** WebKit fetch failures, dead SSE sockets, empty HTTP error bodies, and auth rejections all map to directed human copy naming the host ("Couldn't reach Studio. Check the connection and try again."), background model-refresh failures stay in their banner instead of overwriting the Develop status line, and a host coming back online re-loads its models automatically.

## [0.19.0] - 2026-07-21

### Added

- **Desktop Batch now prepares reviewable prompt variations before generation.** Batch 1 keeps the quick Expand and undo flow while freezing its resolved host through Generate. Batch 2 or greater resolves the selected Generate host once, prepares exactly that many non-empty prompts on it, and opens an inline workspace for editing, intentional removal, regenerate-all, discard, and explicit generation. The same frozen host receives every sibling with its expanded prompt, shared source-prompt provenance, and durable batch ID plus one-based sibling position/count visible in Gallery. Source, model, host, or count changes preserve the reviewed set but name it as out of date and block submission until refresh or discard; missing expansion models pull on the directed host without fallback, show Connecting, Starting, Queued, byte/file/ETA progress, and terminal recovery inline without hiding the prompt work, and partial sibling failures keep successful prints visible.
- **Mold for iPhone.** A first-class Tauri iOS companion connects only to remote Mold hosts and provides Generate, merged Gallery, Catalog, and Hosts views. It reuses the desktop wire types, capability-aware request builder, and SSE client; supports saved hosts, Keychain-backed API keys, Tailscale MagicDNS names, and native Bonjour discovery of `_mold._tcp`; and ships with simulator CI plus a rolling internal TestFlight pipeline after eligible successful `main` builds.
- **iPhone Generate now exposes the desktop's capability-driven request surface.** Prompt expansion supports Batch-aware preparation, quick Batch 1 rewrites with undo, and remote-host history; mobile-local templates can be saved, searched, sorted, renamed, loaded, and deleted. Model-aware controls cover batch queueing, source images and fit policies, Qwen edit targets/references, inpainting masks, ControlNet, LoRA stacks and trigger words, schedulers, CFG++, post-generation upscaling, and target-host estimates. Video models gain validated frames/FPS, audio, chain-routing guidance, camera motion, and the full LTX-2 pipeline, spatial/temporal upscale, retake, source-media, and keyframe controls.
- **iPhone Batch now prepares reviewable prompt variations on the selected host.** Batch 1 keeps quick Expand/undo and freezes the host endpoint, Keychain credential, and server instance through Develop. Batch 2 or greater requires exactly N non-empty prompts before opening a touch-native edit/remove/regenerate/refresh/discard workspace; upstream changes preserve specifically named stale work, and reducing two prompts to one requires confirmation. Missing expansion models keep an immutable input/route record while each pull attempt temporarily leases that exact route, joins an already-starting compatible Catalog request, and releases back to Catalog on every success, failure, cancellation, stale input, or supersession; Retry reacquires the frozen route. Editing or removing reviewed work cancels a pending replacement instead of overwriting it. Approved siblings retain deterministic seeds, independent cancellation, source-prompt provenance, durable batch position, and long-video chain propagation; partial outcomes name every failed variation and reviewed prompt alongside any unconfirmed-cancellation caveat while keeping successful prints, and Gallery retains batch provenance.
- **The iPhone gallery now opens prints in a full-screen viewer.** Photos are shown uncropped, videos stream with native inline playback controls, and clearly labeled **Use as prompt** and **Use as source** actions carry settings or the original still into Generate instead of making a gallery tile tap silently replace the composer. Authenticated hosts issue short-lived read-only media tickets so videos can seek without exposing API keys or buffering entire files in phone memory.
- **iPhone Settings adds first-class themes and appearance.** A header Settings screen now offers Mold or Safelight color families with System, Dark, or Light appearance, a remote-host management shortcut, and version/update details. Preferences are validated and persisted locally, while a serialized native bridge keeps UIKit traits and status-bar glyphs synchronized through rapid changes.

### Changed

- **New iPhone installs now start with the Safelight theme family.** System appearance remains the default, and existing users keep their saved Safelight or Mold choice.
- **iPhone resolution and seed controls are more direct.** The redundant selected-resolution summary card is gone: orientation, proportionally drawn aspect-ratio tiles, the resolution tier, and custom-size fields now serve as both the choice and its confirmation. Seed uses clear Random and Fixed modes with last-seed reuse, one-tap replacement, and validation that prevents invalid fixed values from silently becoming random requests.
- **iPhone Catalog Pull actions now show the real download lifecycle.** Cards, model details, and the host picker move immediately through Connecting, Starting, Queued, and Pulling percentage states, prevent duplicate requests, reconcile the opening SSE snapshot before POST, retain job identity across Hugging Face id/name variants, recover from a missing job id, and reset cleanly for retry or terminal outcomes.

### Fixed

- **Desktop image picking now uses the native file chooser.** The shared source-image picker says **Choose file** instead of web-style upload language, opens the OS picker without the WebView file-control delay, limits its visible choices to PNG/JPEG, and still validates dropped and selected files before attaching them.
- **Fatal CUDA faults no longer cause poisoned-worker retry loops.** Illegal-address, uncorrectable-ECC, launch-failure, and related context-killing errors permanently quarantine the affected worker, discard its cached engines instead of restoring them into a dead context, and stop the server with an error so service supervision can restart the process; the embedded desktop engine relaunches the whole app rather than only its server thread. Quantized Qwen-Image-Edit always uses split CFG to avoid the unsafe doubled packed-edit sequence, while `/api/status` overlays physical NVML/nvidia-smi VRAM usage so retained memory remains visible after a driver fault (#457).
- **Desktop Gallery deletion now works from the keyboard and context menu.** Delete/Backspace claims WebKit's history-back shortcut when a print is selected and uses a visible two-step confirmation before removing it; the tile context menu shares the same confirmation path instead of issuing a silent fire-and-forget delete. Text fields retain native editing behavior, and delete failures surface as errors.
- **Desktop image drops now work from Finder anywhere in Generate.** Native Tauri file-drop events bridge PNG/JPEG paths into the source controls, reject files larger than 64 MiB before ingestion, and restore prompt, model, seed, dimensions, and other settings from embedded Mold metadata before attaching the dropped still as the family-appropriate source or Qwen edit target. Loaded previews, gallery actions, and strength controls remain vertically separated inside the narrow inspector instead of overlapping.
- **Desktop scrolling stays anchored at its limits.** The app shell disables WebKit elastic overscroll so trackpad scrolling no longer rubber-bands the desktop interface past its edges.
- **Desktop prompt recall now includes every connected host.** Up/Down in the Generate composer merges prompt history from This device and all ready remote hosts, and a newly submitted prompt is available immediately while its owning host persists it.
- **Remote catalog pulls no longer succeed without starting.** Live Hugging Face LoRA entries now enqueue their normalized recipe on the selected host; unsupported entries return an explicit error, and the desktop rejects a `202` response that contains no primary or companion job instead of claiming the pull started. Hugging Face LoRA repositories with multiple mutually exclusive variants now select one runnable 4-step BF16 payload rather than reporting and downloading the sum of every adapter and fused checkpoint.
- **macOS notifications now inherit Mold's release-bundle icon.** Release alerts use Apple's current UserNotifications framework and signed bundle identity instead of the deprecated private identity-image path that modern macOS ignored.
- **Desktop no longer keeps running from its disk image, which broke notification icons.** macOS resolves a notification's app icon by bundle identifier through Launch Services, so launching Mold straight from the mounted `.dmg` (or a Gatekeeper translocation path) registered a transient bundle that went stale on eject and left notifications with a generic placeholder — even after a copy was dragged into Applications. Mold now detects a disk-image or translocated launch on macOS and offers to move itself to /Applications and relaunch, preventing the stale registration.
- **iPhone builds now launch the bundled mobile UI and show Mold's real icon.** The mobile Vite build emits the `index.html` entry Tauri boots, every Apple icon size is generated from the Mold bowl on an opaque app-theme background, and CI rejects archives with a missing entry point, stale frontend embed, or stock Tauri icon.
- **The iPhone interface no longer magnifies or rubber-bands unexpectedly.** Editable controls retain a 16px minimum, the mobile viewport disables focus, double-tap, pinch, and other document zoom, and the app shell suppresses overscroll bounce. The full-screen gallery keeps its intentionally scoped horizontal swipe navigation; iOS system-level accessibility Zoom remains available outside WebView page scaling.
- **Simulator builds can exercise Keychain-backed remote hosts.** The local iPhone bundle now keeps Xcode's complete ad-hoc bundle signature, so saving and restoring API keys works during simulator UAT instead of failing with a missing-entitlement error.

## [0.18.0] - 2026-07-19

### Added

- **Desktop chains can now start from an image.** The Chains builder gains a chain-level **Start image** well in the header and an attach / replace / clear source-image well on every stage card, both fed by the shared image picker (upload a PNG/JPEG or choose from any connected host's gallery) — making image→video chains possible on desktop. The start image conditions stage 1 (it rides on `stages[0].source_image`; a stage's own image takes precedence), TOML **Export** now inlines images as `source_image_b64` per stage, and **Open** reads them back, so image chains round-trip between the desktop composer and `mold run --script`.

- **Desktop: catalog cards open an in-app detail drawer.** Clicking a catalog card (title or body — the external Hugging Face / Civitai link remains as a secondary icon) now opens a detail panel with the model's description, license, tags, modality and file format, rating, and an itemized list of everything a pull will download: the primary weights files plus each shared companion (VAE / text-encoder / CLIP) with per-file sizes and a computed total. The detail is fetched from the same host the catalog view is browsing, every descriptive field is optional so older servers simply show less, and installed entries swap the Pull action for **Repair**, which re-fetches only missing or incomplete files.
- **Desktop: repair a model with missing components without re-pulling it.** The Installed list's Info expansion already showed per-component present/missing dots; each missing component now carries a **Repair** button that enqueues a targeted catalog download on the host that owns the model — the server skips files already on disk, so only what's missing is fetched. Previously the only recovery was deleting the model and pulling it again.

- **Desktop downloads tray shows real progress detail and failed downloads can be retried.** Every download row now carries a status chip, and active rows add the file currently transferring, files done/total, and a live ETA derived from a sliding transfer-rate window (stalled downloads honestly show no ETA instead of a bogus one). Finished, cancelled, and failed downloads no longer vanish: they collect in a collapsed History section under the tray, failed entries keep their error message, and a Retry button re-starts the download on the same host it originally targeted.
