Gallery cards on Generate include a Recreate action that restores the form from the saved generation metadata.
- **Generate Studio follow-ups.** The Generate tab now filters its model picker to downloaded standalone generation models, groups them by collapsible family, supports compact boolean filtering and multi-sort controls, removes content hiding controls, adds drag-reorderable LoRA stacks, saves/loads named web-local generation templates, shows per-request memory estimates and component status, exposes model-specific scheduler/control options, uses installed upscaler dropdowns, adds GPU queue lanes with editable queued-job targets, and includes a mask editor for source/gallery/uploaded images.
- **Generate support endpoints.** The server now exposes `POST /api/generate/estimate` for request-sensitive peak-memory estimates, `GET /api/models/:model/components` for model component readiness, and `PATCH /api/queue/:id` for queued-job GPU lane targets. Catalog search now includes NSFW rows by default and the web UI no longer exposes NSFW visibility toggles.
- **Full gallery metadata JSON for Recreate.** The metadata DB now stores the complete `OutputMetadata` JSON alongside existing indexed columns, preserving scheduler, output format, CFG++, LoRA stacks, ControlNet/upscale settings, LTX-2 audio/video pipeline options, and video timing fields for future gallery round-trips.

### Fixed

- **Civitai version and download selection now preserves every public variant and downloads the exact selected file.** Live Civitai search now expands a model's public `modelVersions` into separate catalog rows instead of only showing the first version, while EarlyAccess/paywalled versions stay hidden from search. Recipe URLs now include Civitai's file selectors (`type`, `format`, `size`, `fp`) so ambiguous primary URLs cannot redirect to a different precision than the SHA-256 mold verifies. The catalog detail drawer also lists the primary file plus required companion resources with sizes, and the download drawer groups catalog primary + companion jobs into one model-level progress view.
- **Qwen-Image / Qwen-Image-Edit LoRA catalog compatibility.** Qwen LoRA rows now normalize as installable adapter entries instead of inheriting the old unsupported single-file checkpoint state, Qwen-Image-Edit model filters also show Qwen-Image LoRAs, and unsupported catalog rows now render as "Unsupported by this build" instead of an obsolete rollout badge.

## [0.12.0] - 2026-05-27

### Changed

- **Z-Image VAE loads now reuse the shared CPU tensor cache without bypassing its custom loader.** The cached path feeds the existing Z-Image VAE backend so Civitai/LDM key aliases and 1x1 convolution reshapes stay intact while avoiding repeat safetensors reads across engine loads.
- **LTX-2 two-stage runtime now uses a typed stage render context.** Native two-stage orchestration collects per-stage guidance, sampler mode, sigma schedule, LoRA stack, multimodal guidance, and unconditional-context needs through `prepare_stage_context`, reducing duplicated stage setup without changing generated outputs.
- **LTX-2 attention chunking now accounts for effective attention work.** Native LTX-2 attention chunking decisions include batch size, head count, sequence lengths, dtype width, and device threshold instead of only `q_len * k_len`, so batched or many-head attention chunks before full attention score tensors become too large.
- **LTX-2 multimodal guidance now prebatches static stage contexts.** Native LTX-2 audio/video guidance builds the batched cond/uncond/perturbed/modality context, mask, RoPE, and static transformer inputs once at stage entry, then reuses them across denoise steps while keeping latents, sigma, and timestep dynamic per step.
- **LTX-2 native denoise and VAE paths now cache stable helper tensors.** Timestep inverse-frequency tensors, rotary base-index tensors, and VAE latent-stat broadcast tensors are reused by shape/device/dtype across repeated denoise/decode calls, reducing per-step/per-call allocations without requiring real LTX-2 weights for the cache tests.
- **Qwen-Image hot server prompt-cache misses can keep the Qwen2.5 encoder resident when measured headroom allows.** After encoding a cache miss, CUDA hot-path runs now compare actual free VRAM against denoise plus VAE decode reserves before deciding whether to keep the text encoder on GPU for short-interval follow-up misses; cache hits and pressure cases keep the existing drop/park behavior, with compact progress messages exposing the decision.
- **Qwen-Image CUDA local cold runs now avoid the heavy BF16 Qwen2.5 CPU fallback.** When CUDA `auto` lacks BF16 encoder headroom, local sequential Qwen-Image now falls back to the quantized Q4 GGUF text encoder, using GPU if the quantized encoder fits and CPU otherwise. Explicit `--qwen2-variant bf16` keeps the old BF16 path for comparisons.
- **Qwen-Image CUDA VAE decode now chooses tiled decode proactively under memory pressure.** Native-size CUDA requests use the existing tiled GPU decode path before trying a full-frame VAE decode when free VRAM predicts the full decode would OOM, while smaller requests and CPU VAE placement keep the existing path.
- **LTX-Video now actually reuses the server shared tokenizer pool.** The factory already passed `SharedPool` into `LtxVideoEngine`; its T5-XXL load path now consumes the pooled tokenizer handle so switching back into LTX-Video avoids reparsing the shared T5 tokenizer, matching the FLUX tokenizer-cache behavior.
- **FLUX VAE loads now use a shared CPU tensor cache.** When the server provides `SharedPool`, FLUX loads the shared VAE safetensors into CPU RAM once and rebuilds the VAE from that cached tensor map on later engine loads, avoiding repeat disk reads while still placing tensors on the requested runtime device.
- **FLUX T5 / CLIP safetensors loads now use the shared CPU tensor cache.** The tokenizer pool still handles tokenizer reuse, while safetensors-backed encoder weights can be rebuilt from `SharedPool` CPU tensors across FLUX engine instances; GGUF T5 variants keep the existing quantized load path.
- **`.envrc` now sources `nix-direnv` 3.1.1 for cached devshell loads.** Replaces the stdlib `use flake` (which re-evaluates the flake on every `cd` into the project) with the nix-direnv variant that hashes `flake.nix` + `flake.lock` and caches the resolved environment under `.direnv/`. Warm-path shell entry on this repo drops from ~10.6 s to ~0.3 s (35× faster on macOS). Cold path (first entry, or after `flake.lock` changes) is unchanged. The `.envrc` follows upstream's self-bootstrap pattern: `has nix_direnv_version || source_url …` — contributors who already have nix-direnv installed via home-manager (`programs.direnv.nix-direnv.enable = true;`) skip the network fetch; everyone else gets it pinned by SRI hash on first entry and cached under `~/.config/direnv/`.

### Added

- **Z-Image BF16 block streaming for explicit `--offload` runs.** Z-Image now has a mold-owned streaming transformer that keeps stem/final/RoPE layers resident and streams `context_refiner`, `noise_refiner`, then main `layers` from CPU to the runtime device one block at a time; GGUF and LoRA offload remain explicitly rejected.
- **Flux.2 BF16 block streaming for explicit `--offload` runs.** Flux.2 now keeps embeddings, shared modulation, RoPE, and final layers resident while streaming double-stream then single-stream transformer blocks from CPU to the runtime device one block at a time; GGUF and LoRA offload remain explicitly rejected.
- **SD3 BF16 MMDiT block streaming for explicit `--offload` runs.** SD3.5 now keeps patch/position/timestep/vector/context/final layers resident while rebuilding one CPU-held MMDiT joint block on the runtime device at a time, preserving standard joint-block order and the final context-QKV-only block behavior; GGUF and LoRA offload remain explicitly rejected.
- **Trusted server-local media paths for LTX-2 source inputs.** `audio_file_path` and `source_video_path` let `mold serve` reference large local audio/video files without embedding base64 in JSON; paths are canonicalized against configured `media_roots` / `MOLD_MEDIA_ROOTS`, reject traversal and symlink escapes, and preserve the existing 64 MiB inline media caps.
- **Qwen-Image CUDA black-image diagnostics and smoke checks.** Qwen runtime diagnostics now count NaN and +/-Inf at denoise and VAE decode boundaries, fail fast on non-finite tensors, and flag near-black decoded images with an actionable progress message. `scripts/qwen-2512-benchmark.sh --smoke` enables seeded output sanity checks for local/server Qwen-2512 smoke runs.
- **Qwen-Image-2512 benchmark harness.** `scripts/qwen-2512-benchmark.sh` runs repeatable local-cold, server-load, and warm-server Qwen-2512 timing probes and writes NDJSON metrics including duration, output path/bytes, transformer/text-encoder reload counts, and detected VAE mode. `--dry-run` and `--self-test` let CI or reviewers verify parsing/output formatting without downloading model weights.
- **`mold lambda` Lambda Cloud deployment workflow.** The CLI now has a Docker-first Lambda Cloud path (`doctor`, `availability`, `deploy`, `status`, `logs`, `tunnel`, `ssh`, `filesystems`, `terminate`, `reset --to-zero`) backed by typed Lambda API DTOs in `mold-core`. Deployments stay private by default: cloud-init installs a `mold-lambda.service` that runs `ghcr.io/utensils/mold:<version-suffix>` with `--gpus all`, localhost-only `7680`, and `/data/mold:/workspace`, while the local CLI records `$MOLD_HOME/lambda-state.json` and opens an SSH tunnel. `[lambda]` config keys cover API auth, endpoint, image repository, SSH key material, regional filesystem naming/mounts, hourly confirmation threshold, and local tunnel port; `LAMBDA_API_KEY` overrides config. Regional Lambda filesystems are reused for model/cache/DB/gallery persistence and `reset --to-zero` only targets mold-managed resources after typed confirmation.
- **Hopper Docker image tags.** Release Docker builds now publish native `latest-sm90` and versioned `-sm90` tags for H100/H200/GH-class GPUs, alongside existing default Ada, `sm80`, and `sm120` images.
- **MCP LoRA stacks and installed-LoRA discovery.** `generate_image` and `generate_image_async` now accept a plural `loras` stack whose entries can be catalog ids like `cv:827325`, server-side paths, or `{ id|path, scale }` objects with `scale` defaulting to `1.0`. The MCP bridge exposes `list_loras` so hosts can discover installed LoRA ids and paths before generation. The server adds `GET /api/loras`, returning installed sidecar-backed LoRAs; `?model=<name>` filters the list to LoRAs compatible with that model's family.
- **AUR packaging — `mold-ai-bin`, `mold-ai`, `mold-ai-git` published to the Arch User Repository on every tagged release.** Three PKGBUILDs live in `packaging/aur/`; the `mold-ai-bin` package repackages the upstream `mold-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu-cuda-sm89.tar.gz` (RTX 40-series / Ada Lovelace) for the fast-path install, `mold-ai` builds from the release tarball with `--features cuda,preview,expand,tui,webp,mp4,metrics` and honours `CUDA_COMPUTE_CAP` (set to `120` for RTX 50-series / Blackwell), and `mold-ai-git` tracks `main` HEAD. All three declare `conflicts=('mold')` because the binary path `/usr/bin/mold` collides with the well-known [extra/mold](https://archlinux.org/packages/extra/x86_64/mold/) linker by rui314 — pacman refuses to install both simultaneously. A new `publish-aur` matrix job in `.github/workflows/release.yml` (gated on `AUR_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY`) fires after `release-version` on `v*` tag pushes, runs `scripts/aur/update-pkgbuild.sh` to rewrite `pkgver` + `sha256sums`, then uses `KSXGitHub/github-actions-deploy-aur@v2.7.2` to regenerate `.SRCINFO` in an Arch container and push to `ssh://aur@aur.archlinux.org/<pkgname>.git`. `mold-ai-git` is hand-pushed only since its PKGBUILD only changes when the build recipe changes. `mold update` now classifies `/usr/bin/mold` and `/usr/sbin/mold` (covers Arch's usrmerge) as AUR-managed and exits cleanly with a `paru -Syu mold-ai-bin` hint instead of attempting an in-place swap of a pacman-owned file. A `packaging/aur/test/Dockerfile` + `scripts/aur/test-in-docker.sh` provide a Rosetta-friendly smoke-test loop for macOS contributors. (Closes: AUR distribution gap.)

### Fixed

- **Nix sandbox CUDA builds no longer exec the build-tree `mold` binary during Rust checks.** Crane's Linux check phase now runs binary unit tests only, avoiding integration-test startup failures on `libcuda.so.1` inside hermetic builders where the host NVIDIA driver is intentionally unavailable; exec-based CLI smoke tests remain covered outside the sandbox. ([#332](https://github.com/utensils/mold/issues/332))
- **LTX-2 CUDA unload now runs a reclaim rung after dropping runtime state.** Native LTX-2 unload drops the retained runtime session before resetting the assigned CUDA primary context, giving OOM recovery and explicit model eviction the same hard reclaim path while CPU fallback unloads remain no-ops.
- **LTX-2 / LTX-2.3 frame counts now respect the temporal RoPE budget.** Requests whose stage-1 frame count exceeds 153 frames now fail validation with an explicit "temporal RoPE budget" error before inference starts, preventing over-budget 19B/22B runs from producing rainbow/static noise instead of prompt-relevant video. Long clips can still use `--temporal-upscale x2` when the stage-1 render stays within the budget ([#226](https://github.com/utensils/mold/issues/226)).
- **Server OOM diagnostics are now family-aware.** Image requests no longer get video-only `--frames` advice; SD1.5 1024x1024 failures now call out the requested resolution, the 512x512 default/native target, and the fact that checkpoint size is not peak VRAM because activations, VAE decode workspace, CUDA workspaces, and resident cache also count. Preflight budget rejections likewise suggest lowering `--width`/`--height` and `--batch` before variant/offload advice.

## [0.10.0] - 2026-05-15

_LoRA across every supported family, live HF + Civitai catalog, multi-prompt chain v2 scripts, CFG++ sampler, DB-backed user settings, TUI redesign, and a heavy round of queue + memory-management hardening._

### Added

- **`mold mcp` stdio bridge for LM Studio and other MCP hosts.** The new CLI subcommand speaks newline-delimited JSON-RPC over stdin/stdout and proxies tools to the existing `mold serve` HTTP API via `MOLD_HOST` / `--host`. It exposes `generate_image` (single PNG/JPEG result returned as MCP image content), `generate_image_async` + `generation_status` (short-returning job flow with SSE progress and completed image retrieval, avoiding host-side long tool-call timeouts), `list_gallery` / `get_gallery_image` (search/filter/sort saved gallery items and fetch the latest or named image), `list_models`, and `server_status`, with docs showing the LM Studio `mcp.json` entry.
- **`GET /api/queue` — server-side authoritative job listing for SPA reconciliation.** Closes the long-standing "queue piles up but mold is idle" failure mode where the web UI accumulated zombie `running` cards after dropped SSE streams (network blip, server restart, browser tab suspended past keepalive). The fix is layered: (L1) `web/src/api.ts::generateStream` / `generateChainStream` now track whether a terminal event (`complete` / `error` / HTTP error) fired during the SSE stream — if `streamSse` resolves cleanly without one, they synthesize `onError({kind:"network", message:"stream closed before completion"})` so the per-job card flips out of `running` immediately. (L2) `loadPersistedJobs` converts any `state: "running"` row to `state: "error"` with `"page reloaded — server progress lost"` on SPA rehydrate, since the prior session's SSE controller is unreachable from the new module instance; `Job` now carries a `lastProgressAt: number` updated on every progress event, and `RunningJobCard.vue` renders a `STALE_THRESHOLD_MS = 60_000` heartbeat badge ("No progress for >60s — stream may have dropped") on any running card that hasn't received an event in a minute. (L3) `SseProgressEvent::Queued` gains a server-assigned `id: String` field (`#[serde(default)]` for backwards compat — legacy servers omit it, new clients treat empty as "no id"); `GenerationJob` and `GpuJob` carry that UUIDv4 from `submit()` through the dispatcher to the worker thread; a new `crates/mold-server/src/job_registry.rs` `JobRegistry` (Arc<RwLock<Vec<EntryInternal>>>) is registered against in `routes::generate` / `generate_stream`, flipped `Queued → Running` by the GPU worker on pickup, and removed by an idempotent drop guard on every terminal path (success, error, panic, client-disconnect-before-dispatch, no-worker-available, GPU worker disconnected). The new `GET /api/queue` endpoint returns `{ entries: [{ id, model, state, started_at_unix_ms, position, gpu? }] }` — `gpu` is omitted (not nulled) for queued rows so clients can't infer GPU 0 from absence. The web SPA captures `id` from the first `queued` SSE event into `Job.serverId` and a new `useQueueReconciler` composable (`startQueueReconciler` from `App.vue`) polls `/api/queue` every 30 s; any running card whose `serverId` isn't present in the listing past a 30 s grace window (covers the submit→first-event race) is dead-lettered as `"job not found on server — connection lost"`. Poll failures never dead-letter — SSE remains the canonical signal for "this job died." The web tests grew by 24 cases across `api.test.ts` (silent-close synthesis, complete/error happy paths, HTTP 503 path, abort behavior — single + chain), `useGenerateStream.test.ts` (5 `loadPersistedJobs` dead-letter cases incl. legacy-payload back-compat), `useQueueReconciler.test.ts` (9 cases incl. grace-window race, transient-failure safety, stop()-idempotence); the server grew `job_registry::tests` (5 cases) and `routes_test::tests::queue_*` (2 cases — empty listing + FIFO position + queued-row-omits-gpu wire shape). The `SseProgressEvent::Queued` wire change is opt-in via `#[serde(default)]` on the new `id` field so the TUI, Discord bot, and any cached older clients keep deserializing intact. (Closes the journal-confirmed scenario: 13:26:49 last completion, both GPUs idle, `queue_depth=0`, browser showing 20+ "running" cards from a prior session.)
- **SD3 / SD3.5 LoRA support — `lora` / `loras` request fields now accept SD3 adapters.** SD3.5 Large, Large Turbo, and Medium models (the latter being the MMDiT-X variant with the extra `attn2` self-attention tower) all wire through a new `crates/mold-inference/src/sd3/lora.rs` module that mirrors `flux/lora.rs`: a custom `SimpleBackend` (`LoraBackend`) intercepts every `vb.get()` call during `MMDiT::new`, mmap-loads the base tensor from the stabilityai BF16 safetensors (or city96 GGUF), and merges the `B @ A · scale` delta inline before handing the result back to candle — no pre-materialised HashMap, peak VRAM matches the non-LoRA path. The BF16 path covers the diffusers-canonical key matrix (`transformer.transformer_blocks.{i}.attn.to_q/to_k/to_v` and `add_q_proj/add_k_proj/add_v_proj` fuse-routed to the joint MMDiT's `x_block.attn.qkv.weight` / `context_block.attn.qkv.weight`; `attn.to_out.0` / `attn.to_add_out` direct-routed to the attention projections; `attn2.*` for MMDiT-X / SD3.5-medium's second x*block attention tower; `ff.net.0.proj` / `ff.net.2` / `ff_context.net.0.proj` / `ff_context.net.2` mapped to `mlp.fc1` / `mlp.fc2` on each stream; `norm1.linear` / `norm1_context.linear` mapped to the per-stream `adaLN_modulation.1`) plus the Kohya / sd-scripts `lora_unet_joint_blocks*_` flat-naming convention. The GGUF path (`gguf*lora_var_builder`) selectively dequantizes patched tensors to F32 on CPU, merges, then re-quantizes back to the original GGML dtype on the target device so each patched tensor occupies the same VRAM as its original — no Q8→F16 inflation. Multi-LoRA stacking is supported: passing multiple `LoraWeight`s in `req.loras`accumulates patches additively on shared tensors, with each adapter's delta independently keyed in the cross-rebuild`LoraDeltaCache`by`(tensor, patch-index, lora_path_hash, scale_bits)`. The parsed-adapter `(path, mtime)` cache (capacity 4, FIFO) survives transformer rebuilds so slider scrubbing on a single LoRA hits the cache after the first generate and skips the safetensors parse. Zero-scale entries are dropped from the effective stack (`ZERO_SCALE_EPS = 1e-8`) so a slider pinned to 0.0 doesn't force a rebuild. The validator at `crates/mold-core/src/validation.rs::require_lora_capable_family`now accepts`Some("sd3")`alongside`Some("flux")`and`Some("ltx2")`; the rejection message lists all three so a user denied for SDXL knows their alternatives. 24 unit tests in `sd3::lora::tests` pin the contract: per-leaf assertions for every MMDiT attention/MLP/adaLN leaf (`to_q/to_k/to_v`, `add_q_proj/add_k_proj/add_v_proj`, `attn2.to_q/to_k/to_v`, `to_out.0`, `to_add_out`, `attn2.to_out.0`, `ff.net.0.proj`, `ff.net.2`, `ff_context.net.0.proj`, `ff_context.net.2`, `norm1.linear`, `norm1_context.linear`), the full Kohya flat-key matrix on `joint_blocks*{i}*x_block*_`and`joint*blocks*{i}_context_block_\*`, the diffusers / Kohya / OneTrainer / PEFT-default / Mochi suffix matrix in `classify_lora_key`, synthetic-safetensors round-trip on both naming conventions, `alpha · scale / rank`effective-scale arithmetic, parsed-adapter cache hit + mtime invalidation, multi-adapter stacking on a shared candle tensor, and end-to-end Direct + FusedSlice patch application through the`LoraBackend`on synthetic MMDiT-shaped tensors with the Q/K untouched / V-only-merged invariant. SD1.5 / SDXL / Z-Image / Flux.2 / Qwen-Image are still gated to no-LoRA at the validator. (LoRA bypass mode`MOLD_LORA_BYPASS`, fingerprint-based skip-rebuild, and block-level offloading composability are deferred — SD3 always rebuilds the transformer on each generate when LoRAs are active.)

### Changed

- **SD1.5 / SDXL / SD3 denoise loop now skips the unconditional pass when guidance ≈ 1.0** (LCM, Lightning, SDXL Turbo, SD3 Turbo). Previously `use_cfg = guidance > 1.0` still ran the doubled `[uncond, cond]` forward at exactly `cfg = 1.0` — wasting half the per-step compute even though `apply_cfg(1.0, .)` collapses to `cond`. Replaced with `cfg_active(guidance) = (guidance - 1.0).abs() > 1e-4` (matches ComfyUI's `samplers.py:370` short-circuit). Distilled-CFG workflows now run 1× transformer forwards per step instead of 2×, halving denoise time. New shared predicate in `crates/mold-inference/src/engine.rs::cfg_active` is reused by SDXL `denoise_loop` / `encode_prompt`, SD1.5 `denoise_loop` / `encode_prompt`, and SD3 `euler_sample`. SD3 SLG (Skip Layer Guidance) behavior is preserved: when active, the path keeps the conditional pred around for the SLG offset. FLUX / Flux.2 / Z-Image / Qwen-Image are guidance-distilled (single-pass already) and unchanged.
- **Activation-aware memory budgets replace the fixed 3 GB / 5 GB / 2 GB headroom heuristics** across `should_offload`, `preflight_memory_check`, the FLUX pre-VAE-decode `KEEP_TRANSFORMER` force-drop, and the server-side `preflight_memory_guard`. New `mold_inference::device::activation_bytes(width, height, batch, dtype_bytes, family)` mirrors ComfyUI's `model_base.memory_required(input_shape)` shape: peak activation memory scales as `area × dtype_bytes × batch × per_arch_factor` with a 256 MB floor for kernel workspaces. Per-family factors are calibrated empirically (anchor: FLUX dit 1024² bf16 cfg=1 ≈ 273 MB; 2048² ≈ 1.09 GB) — `FluxDit/Flux2Dit/ZImageDit` 130, `Sd3Mmdit` 156, `SdxlUnet/QwenImageDit` 173, `Wuerstchen` 217, `SmallTransformer` 87. The server's `preflight_memory_guard` now accepts an `ActivationHint { width, height, batch, dtype_bytes, family }` plumbed through `ensure_model_ready` / `ensure_model_ready_sync` / `run_chain_blocking`; the family is resolved via `family_for_model` (manifest first, catalog config fallback) so `cv:*`/`hf:*` IDs get the right factor. End-to-end impact: a 768² FLUX generation no longer false-offloads on a 16 GB card, while a 2048² generation gets a 4× larger budget — catching the OOM the previous fixed 5 GB headroom only approximated.
- Catalog `/api/catalog/families` response: dropped vestigial `foundation`/`finetune` count fields (always 0 since the bulk scrape was removed). The sidebar no longer shows the counts line.

### Removed

- **Bulk-scrape catalog DB, scanner, refresh endpoints, embedded shards, and `mold catalog` CLI subcommand.** Every read path is now live HF + Civitai with sidecars next to each install for "what's downloaded." The previous walker hit Civitai page-by-page until rate-limited, persisted 1000+ rows the user never saw, and required a 30–60 min `mold catalog refresh` before `mold run cv:<id>` would work. Removed in this release: the `catalog` and `catalog_fts` SQLite tables (DB migration v9 drops them forward-only), the `mold-catalog::scanner` / `sink` / `shards` / `stages` / `filter` modules, the embedded JSON shards under `mold-catalog/data/catalog/`, the `mold-db::catalog` repo, the entire `mold catalog` CLI subcommand tree (`list`, `show`, `refresh`, `where`), the server's `POST/GET /api/catalog/refresh*` endpoints, the `RefreshTracker` queue and scan-driver task, and the `CatalogRefreshPanel` web component plus all `useCatalog.refreshStatus` plumbing. **New live additions** that replace what was removed: `mold_catalog::live::fetch_civitai_version(id)` and `fetch_hf_repo(repo_id)` for single-id resolution (used by `mold run cv:<id>` / `hf:<repo>`, the SPA's drawer fetch, and `POST /api/catalog/:id/download`); `live::search` now branches on `source` (Civitai-only, HF-only, or both — HF errors don't nuke Civitai results); HF live search proxies `https://huggingface.co/api/models?search=…` and drops repos that don't map to a supported mold family via a substring + tag heuristic. The DB-backed `/api/catalog` list endpoint is gone — the SPA was already using `/api/catalog/search` (live) on its read path. Operators upgrading get a one-shot v9 migration that drops the catalog tables and reclaims the space; the next CLI / server boot reads from live.

### Fixed

- **`cv:*` Z-Image checkpoints now resolve the right runtime files.** The catalog already marked single-file Z-Image entries such as `cv:2442439` as requiring `z-image-te`, but `mold-core` had no hidden companion manifest for that name, so companion auto-pull skipped it and server generation failed with `missing required component 'z-image-te'`. Added the `z-image-te` manifest (three Tongyi-MAI/Z-Image-Turbo Qwen3 shards, tokenizer, and VAE) routed under `shared/z-image`. The Civitai normalizer now chooses the `type=Model` safetensors when a version lists Text Encoder / VAE / Model files (fixes `cv:2442439` selecting `zImageTurbo_turbo_txt.safetensors`), downloads recipe-provided `type=Text Encoder` files for Z-Image when present, and resolves those per-version text encoders ahead of the shared companion shards while keeping the known-good shared Z-Image VAE. Recipe-provided text encoders default to BF16 (CPU if VRAM is tight) instead of silently falling back to a generic quantized Qwen3. The Z-Image safetensors loader also accepts the Civitai/ComfyUI `model.diffusion_model.` transformer prefix.
- **Z-Image server decode no longer falls back to the flat-gray CPU VAE path when GPU decode is available.** Eager loading now places the VAE on GPU when there is enough room for the VAE weights, rather than requiring the full 1024px decode workspace before denoising. The transformer is already dropped before VAE decode, so the larger workspace is available at the point it is actually needed.
- **Z-Image no longer renders cross-hatch / tiled-noise outputs.** The local Z-Image transformer wrapper now follows the reference pad-token protocol: Qwen caption tokens and image patch tokens are padded to 32-token boundaries with the checkpoint's learned `cap_pad_token` / `x_pad_token`, caption tokens are refined before image tokens, and the main transformer sees the reference basic-mode `[image, caption]` ordering with image RoPE offsets based on the padded caption length. The previous path loaded those pad-token tensors but never used them, and an intermediate wrapper version extracted image tokens from the wrong side of the unified sequence, so denoise ran with token layout drift from the checkpoint's training/runtime convention. The fix applies to BF16 safetensors, GGUF dense fallback, and LoRA-wrapped loads because they all construct the same `MoldZImageTransformer2DModel`. New unit tests pin 32-token rounding, learned-token append behavior, zero position IDs for image pad tokens, and basic-mode unified token ordering.
- **LTX-2 LoRA requests no longer rejected with a 422.** The validator at `mold-core/validation.rs::require_lora_capable_family` was FLUX-only despite `ltx2/lora.rs` having full LoRA engine support. Extended the gate to accept `"ltx2"` alongside `"flux"`; SDXL / SD3 / SD1.5 / Z-Image / Flux.2 / Qwen-Image still correctly reject.
- **Preflight rejection message now reports the actual budget cap instead of raw free VRAM.** When `POST /api/generate/stream` rejected a model for exceeding the 90% VRAM budget, the error read e.g. "estimated peak ~24.4 GB exceeds available ~25.3 GB" — which looks false to the user (24.4 < 25.3). The comparison was against `available × 0.9 = 22.8 GB`, not the raw available figure; the message was printing the wrong number as the "exceeds" target. Fixed: `check_model_memory_budget` in `crates/mold-server/src/model_manager.rs` now prints the budget cap directly — e.g. "estimated peak ~24.4 GB exceeds the per-load budget cap ~22.8 GB (90% of 25.3 GB free, with 2 GB activation headroom built into peak estimate)". Two new tests pin the contract: `preflight_error_message_states_correct_budget_cap` asserts the cap value appears in the message for the exact user-reported numbers; `preflight_error_message_does_not_imply_peak_less_than_available` sweeps five (peak, free) rejection scenarios and asserts every message says "budget cap" with the correct sub-peak number. A third test (`memory_budget_headroom_is_2gb` in `mold-ai-inference`) pins the 2 GB headroom constant so a future change to the constant forces an update to the message text.
- **LTX-Video runtime OOM hygiene + preflight peak estimate corrected.** Two bugs compounded to produce an opaque `CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` crash for `ltx-video-0.9.8-13b-dev:bf16` at 768×512×25 on a 24 GB card. (1) **Preflight was wrong**: `activation_family_for("ltx-video")` returned `Ltx2Video`, which triggered `streaming_transformer()=true` and routed the preflight through the 6 GB streaming-cap path designed for the 22B LTX-2 block-streaming transformer. LTX-Video loads the _entire_ transformer into VRAM at generate time (it is not streamed); the 13B BF16 checkpoint is ~26 GB and the 6 GB cap let an OOM-destined load through the preflight unchecked. Fix: split `ActivationFamily::LtxVideo` (non-streaming, full file-size estimate) from `ActivationFamily::Ltx2Video` (streaming, 6 GB cap) and map `"ltx-video"` to the former. The preflight's Sequential peak for 13B BF16 is now ~28 GB which correctly rejects on a 24 GB card. (2) **Runtime OOM error was opaque and left dirty state**: when `load_transformer` OOM'd, the raw `DriverError(CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY, "out of memory")` reached the SSE event stream unchanged. Now `gpu_worker::is_cuda_oom(e)` detects the pattern in the anyhow chain, calls `device::try_synchronize_device(ordinal)` to drain pending CUDA work before reporting, and substitutes `gpu_worker::oom_user_message(model)` — an actionable message that says reduce `--frames`, lower `--width`/`--height`, use a quantized variant, or close other GPU apps. The rejection message for LTX-Video preflight failures now also uses this wording (instead of `--offload`, which is a FLUX-only flag). Cache hygiene: `insert_loaded` is only called after `engine.load()` succeeds so a failed load never inserts a phantom cache entry — confirmed by the new `failed_load_does_not_leak_into_model_cache` test. Four new regression tests: `preflight_rejects_ltx_video_13b_at_768x512x25_on_24gb_card`, `preflight_estimate_for_ltx_video_13b_within_expected_range`, `activation_family_for_ltx_video_is_non_streaming`, `preflight_rejection_message_for_ltx_video_suggests_frames_or_resolution`; three OOM hygiene unit tests: `is_cuda_oom_detects_driver_error_string`, `runtime_oom_message_suggests_offload_and_smaller_frames`, `failed_load_does_not_leak_into_model_cache`.
- **`POST /api/generate/stream` with a video model and no `output_format` field no longer returns 422.** Previously `GenerateRequest::output_format` was a non-optional field with `#[serde(default)]` that deserialized to `OutputFormat::Png` when omitted. The `ltx2` validation gate then rejected `Png` with `"LTX-2 outputs must use mp4, gif, apng, or webp"`, making every default-config LTX-2 HTTP request fail. The fix changes the wire field to `Option<OutputFormat>` (with `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]`) and adds `GenerateRequest::normalise_output_format(family)`, which fills in `Mp4` for `ltx2` and `ltx-video` families and `Png` for all image families when the caller did not supply the field. The server's `prepare_generation` calls `normalise_output_format` — after family resolution but before validation — so the validator always sees a concrete format. Explicit caller choices (including explicit `"png"` on a video model) are preserved and still produce the existing 422 error. The `resolved_output_format()` convenience method on `GenerateRequest` returns the normalised value (or `Png` as a last-resort fallback) so inference engine code does not need to unwrap the Option. Five unit tests cover the normalisation contract.
- **`flux2-klein:bf16` block-pattern artifacts (16–32 px grid, cyan/magenta/yellow tints) — `LastLayer` shift/scale swap.** Commit `c0c2b80` introduced `Flux2Linear` for BFL-native NVFP4 single-file checkpoints and simultaneously swapped the `LastLayer::forward` AdaLayerNorm chunk assignment from diffusers ordering `(scale, shift) = (chunks[0], chunks[1])` to BFL-native ordering `(shift, scale) = (chunks[0], chunks[1])`. BFL-native (`model.diffusion_model.*`) checkpoints do store `(shift, scale)` in `final_layer.adaLN_modulation.1.weight`; however, the standard HF diffusers `flux2-klein-bf16` checkpoint already has the two halves **swapped by the diffusers converter** (`swap_scale_shift` in the Python conversion script) so it is already in `(scale, shift)` order — the same as what `AdaLayerNormContinuous` and `LastLayer::forward` expect. The `c0c2b80` swap broke the diffusers checkpoint path: the final layer was applying the scale multiplier where it should add the shift offset and vice versa, producing the characteristic 2×2-patch-granular color-separated block artifact at every 16–32 px. Fix: revert `LastLayer::forward` to use `(scale, shift) = (chunks[0], chunks[1])` (the correct diffusers convention) and move the BFL-native half-swap into `SingleFileBackend` as a new `BackendEntry::SwapHalves` entry for `norm_out.linear.weight`. At load time, `SwapHalves` concatenates the two half-tensors in reverse order so the weight always arrives at `LastLayer` in diffusers `(scale, shift)` order regardless of the source checkpoint format. The GGUF quantized transformer path (`quantized_transformer.rs`) is unaffected — it has its own `QLastLayer` that never shared this code path. Two new regression tests pin the fix: `last_layer_forward_uses_diffusers_scale_then_shift_ordering` (unit test verifying `norm(xs=0) * (1+scale) + shift = shift` and failing if the ordering is swapped) and `flux2_singlefile_backend_swaps_ada_ln_halves_for_diffusers_ordering` (fixture test verifying the `SwapHalves` entry exchanges the two halves of the BFL-native weight on lookup).
- **SD1.5 prompt cache: migrated to `CfgPromptCacheKey` (silent-wrong-output fix carried over from SD3 / SDXL).** SD1.5's prompt cache was still keyed on `(prompt, guidance)` only, but stored the doubled `(uncond, cond)` tensor when CFG is active — same shape as SDXL. Changing only the negative prompt at the same positive prompt + guidance returned a stale `(uncond_old, cond)` pair; the user got plausible but not-what-they-asked-for output with no error. Migrated the cache key + both call sites (`encode_prompt` for the eager path, sequential first-cache-check for the load-use-drop path) to `cfg_prompt_cache_key(prompt, negative_prompt, guidance)`. New regression test `sd15_prompt_cache_distinguishes_negative_prompt_changes` mirrors the SDXL / SD3 tests. With this, every CFG-capable family in the repo (SD1.5, SDXL, SD3) is on the same correct cache shape — the legacy `PromptCacheKey` / `prompt_cache_key` are deleted (no remaining consumers; FLUX / Z-Image / Flux.2 are guidance-distilled and don't use a CFG cache).
- **LTX-2 / LTX-Video preflight no longer false-rejects 22B checkpoints on 24 GB cards.** The server's `preflight_memory_guard_with_available` budgets transformer GPU residency at the on-disk file size, but LTX-2 always loads via `Ltx2AvTransformer3DModel::new_streaming` (`crates/mold-inference/src/ltx2/model/video_transformer.rs:1781`) — only `streaming_prefetch_count` (default 2) transformer blocks are co-resident at any time, plus a few hundred MB of non-block weights (`proj_in`, `proj_out`, `time_embed`, `caption_projection`, `scale_shift_table`, RMS norms). The 22B Lightricks LTX-2 v2.3 checkpoint (e.g. `cv:2752735` `ltx23_full.safetensors`) is 46 GB on disk at BF16, so the file-size estimate produced peak ~48 GB and rejected on a 3090 even though only ~2 GB of transformer weights are GPU-resident at peak inference. Two-part fix: (1) New `ActivationFamily::Ltx2Video` variant in `crates/mold-inference/src/device.rs` plus an `ActivationFamily::streaming_transformer()` predicate that returns `true` for it; `activation_family_for` now maps `"ltx2"` / `"ltx-video"` / `"ltx-2"` / `"ltx-2.3"` to `Ltx2Video`. (2) New `streaming_transformer_peak(paths)` in `crates/mold-server/src/model_manager.rs` replaces the `transformer_size + vae_size` term with a 6 GB cap (covers two prefetched 22B blocks ≈ 1.83 GB + non-block fragments + VAE + buffer) when the activation hint marks the family as streaming. Encoder phase still pays full encoder_total because Gemma / T5 / CLIP load whole — so a 25 GB Gemma TE on a 24 GB card is still correctly rejected. Three new tests pin the contract: `preflight_accepts_ltx2_22b_on_24gb_card_via_streaming_peak` (the cv:2752735 case), `preflight_rejects_ltx2_22b_when_hint_marks_non_streaming` (anchors the file-size fallback for non-streaming families), and `preflight_rejects_ltx2_when_encoder_phase_exceeds_card` (the encoder-phase cap is preserved).
- **Release workflow no longer hard-fails when the GitHub Actions cache backend is unhealthy.** All three native release jobs (`build-macos`, `build-linux-sm89`, `build-linux-sm120`) set `RUSTC_WRAPPER: sccache` at the job-env level, so when the GHA cache backend returns the HTML outage page (`<h2>Our services aren't available right now</h2>` bubbling out of sccache as `cache storage failed to read`), cargo's very first `sccache rustc -vV` exits 101 and the whole release build dies — happened on the `d4c6899` push to main (run 25609490104). Mirrors the existing `ci.yml` probe pattern: a new `Probe sccache (disable on cache outage)` step compiles a one-line Rust program through sccache to exercise the cache backend (the local-daemon-only `sccache --start-server` doesn't catch a backend outage; the failure only surfaces on first read). On non-zero exit, the step emits a `::warning::` annotation, sets `RUSTC_WRAPPER=` (cargo treats empty as unset), and stops the failed daemon — the build then falls through to direct rustc (slower, no caching) instead of crashing. Steady-state behavior is unchanged because the probe succeeds in <1 s when the backend is healthy.
- **Truncated single-file safetensors (e.g. cv:2739091) now fail with an actionable error before mmap.** A partial download — file on disk shorter than the safetensors header declares — used to bubble up from `MmapedSafetensors::new` as an opaque safetensors `InvalidData`, and the only thing the user saw on the wire was `model load error: mmap single-file checkpoint at <path>` (the outer `with_context` swallowed the chain). Two-part fix: (1) `crates/mold-inference/src/loader/single_file_backend.rs::check_safetensors_not_truncated` does a header-only peek, computes `max(data_offsets[1])`, and rejects with `"checkpoint at <path> is truncated: file is X bytes but the safetensors header declares tensor data ending at Y bytes (Z bytes missing). The download is incomplete — re-fetch the model."` Called from `from_entries` so every Civitai single-file entry point (SD1.5, SDXL, Flux.2 BFL-native, NVFP4) is covered uniformly. (2) `crates/mold-server/src/queue.rs::clean_error_message` now renders the full anyhow chain (`{e:#}` instead of `{e}`) so wrappers like `validate single-file checkpoint at …` carry their root cause through to the user instead of clipping at the topmost context. Three new tests pin both halves: `check_safetensors_not_truncated_passes_for_intact_file` / `_flags_short_file` (synthetic safetensors + `set_len`), `from_flux2_singlefile_surfaces_truncation_clearly` (end-to-end on the public Flux.2 entry point — chained message contains both the `validate single-file checkpoint` outer wrapper and the `truncated` root), and `clean_error_message_renders_full_anyhow_chain` (regression guard on the formatter switch).
- **`cv:*` / `hf:*` first-fetch hardening — partial config can no longer be sealed under stale disk assumptions.** Two stacked bugs hit the catalog bridge whenever a generate request preceded a finished download: (1) `synthesize_catalog_config` ran the FLUX bundled-VAE probe at `model_manager.rs:553-562` only when `primary_path.exists()`. For transformer-only checkpoints (e.g. `cv:994561` `*Unet.safetensors`) requested before download completion, the probe was skipped and the `else` arm sealed `cfg.vae = primary` — so engine load later crashed with `cannot find tensor encoder.conv_in.weight` even though the file IS on disk by then; only the per-file content introspection never re-ran. (2) `install_catalog_model` returned `bool`, collapsing every failure mode (Civitai outage, 404, malformed recipe) into the same generic "not installed" 404 — a Civitai network error and a typo'd ID looked identical to the user. Two-part fix: (a) Lifted the pure portion of synthesis into `mold_catalog::synthesis::synthesize_intent` (single source of truth shared by `mold-cli` and `mold-server`), which produces a `CatalogModelIntent` with no disk reads; and (b) deferred all disk-dependent decisions (FLUX VAE probe, companion-path lookup) into a new `model_manager::resolve_intent_to_paths` that runs at engine-load time on every request, against fresh disk state. The intent is cached on `AppState.catalog_intents`; the `ModelConfig` is rebuilt lazily. `install_catalog_model` now returns `Result<(), InstallError>` with `Network` (502), `NotFound` (404), and `RecipeMalformed` (500) variants mapped per-status by `routes::generate`. Companion resolution now hard-errors with the specific missing component name (`flux-vae`, `t5-v1_1-xxl`, ...) instead of silently `tracing::warn`-ing through partial state — and distinguishes "manifest entry not in Config" (config bug) from "file not on disk yet" (transient — run `mold pull <id>`). New tests: 5 in `mold_catalog::synthesis::tests` (parity, disk-independence, error variants), 7 in `model_manager::tests` (intent consistency, transformer-only resolution, hard companion errors, file-arrives-after-request, error mapping), 3 in `catalog_live_test` (network unreachable / 404 / 5xx via wiremock). Closes the cv:994561 transformer-only-FLUX scenario and the Civitai-down opaque-error scenario.
- **VAE tiled-decode OOM fallback no longer no-ops at 1024×1024.** The default `TileConfig::tile_size` was 128 latent-px, but a 1024² generation produces a 128×128 latent — so `axis_starts` returned `vec![0]` and the "tiled retry" emitted a single tile equal to the full latent, repeating the exact allocation that just OOM'd. With `MOLD_FLUX_KEEP_TRANSFORMER=1` keeping the transformer resident through VAE decode (which leaves only ~3 GB free on a 24 GB card), this surfaced as `DriverError(CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY)` reaching the user even though the fallback was firing. Two-part fix in `crates/mold-inference/src/vae_tiling.rs`: (1) Default `tile_size` lowered to 64 latent (overlap 16) — matches ComfyUI's actual `VAE.decode_tiled` default in `comfy/sd.py` (the prior docstring claimed it mirrored ComfyUI but the values didn't). At 1024² this produces a 3×3 grid per offset pass, the smallest grid that genuinely subdivides the conv2d workspace. (2) New `shrink_tile_for_latent(cfg, lat_h, lat_w)` helper invoked from `decode_with_oom_fallback`: when `tile_size ≥ min(lat_h, lat_w)` (e.g. a hand-tuned config or future default drift), shrinks `tile_size` to half the smaller axis (rounded to a multiple of 8, floored at 32) and proportionally reduces `overlap`. This guarantees the OOM fallback path always uses strictly less peak VRAM than the full decode that just failed. Five new tests pin both halves: `test_default_tile_size_subdivides_1024_latent` (regression: default must be < 128); `test_shrink_tile_no_op_when_default_already_subdivides_1024`; `test_shrink_tile_subdivides_when_tile_ge_latent`; `test_shrink_tile_no_op_when_latent_below_floor`; `test_shrink_tile_uses_smaller_axis`. Existing `test_tile_config_default` updated to the new (64, 16, 3) constants.
- **FLUX transformer-only single-files (e.g. `cv:994561` `realHornyProV3_realHornyProV3Unet.safetensors`) now load via the catalog bridge.** The bridge in `crates/mold-server/src/model_manager.rs::synthesize_catalog_config` and `crates/mold-cli/src/catalog_bridge.rs::synthesize_model_config` unconditionally set `cfg.vae = primary_path` for every FLUX `Bundling::SingleFile`, then `copy_catalog_companion`'s `flux-vae` arm was a no-op — so any Civitai FLUX fine-tune that strips the VAE (`*Unet.safetensors`) crashed at engine load with `cannot find tensor encoder.conv_in.weight`. New `mold_inference::loader::flux_single_file_bundles_vae` peeks the safetensors header (8-byte length + JSON, no tensor data) and looks for VAE-encoder markers (`encoder.conv_in*`, `first_stage_model.encoder.*`, `vae.encoder.*`); when absent, the bridge defers `cfg.vae` and the `flux-vae` companion's resolved path populates it. Bundled-VAE checkpoints keep current behavior (cfg.vae == primary, companion is a no-op). Plus: the gpu-worker / `check_model_available` "Run: mold pull <id>" message now says "Re-pull the entry from the catalog so its companions … are fetched" for `cv:` / `hf:` IDs whose synthesized config is missing required components — was misleading because the primary checkpoint IS on disk; only the companion isn't. Plus: `is_degraded()` lazy-resets the `consecutive_failures` counter when the cooldown timestamp has expired so a single post-cooldown failure no longer immediately re-degrades the GPU. New tests: 5 in `loader::single_file::tests` (probe true/false/A1111/pruner/missing-file), 4 in `model_manager::tests` (companion populates / does not override / synth bundle vs. transformer-only), 4 in `catalog_bridge::tests` (mirror), 2 in `gpu_pool::tests` (cooldown lazy-reset).
- **FLUX LoRA loader now accepts Kohya / sd-scripts naming.** The loader at `crates/mold-inference/src/flux/lora.rs` was matching only the diffusers/PEFT canonical suffixes (`.lora_A.weight`/`.lora_B.weight`) and the dot-separated module-path keys produced by the diffusers training stack — so any Civitai LoRA exported by `kohya-ss/sd-scripts` (the most common FLUX training tool, used by e.g. cv:1301668 _aidmaRealisticSkin-FLUX-v0.1_) silently skipped every layer and bailed with `no LoRA A/B pairs found`. Two-part fix: (1) `LoraAdapter::load` now also strips `.lora_down.weight` / `.lora_up.weight` (Kohya's A/B suffix convention; `lora_down` is `(rank, in)` ≡ A, `lora_up` is `(out, rank)` ≡ B) so the pair-grouping picks them up. (2) `map_lora_key` recognises the `lora_unet_*` prefix and dispatches into a new `map_kohya_unet_key` helper that walks the flat `double_blocks_<i>_<leaf>` / `single_blocks_<i>_<leaf>` namespace and produces candle targets directly. Kohya stores the **fused** Q/K/V output as a single `lora_unet_double_blocks_<i>_img_attn_qkv` (and `txt_attn_qkv`, and `single_blocks_<i>_linear1`) entry with a B@A delta whose shape matches the candle `img_attn.qkv.weight` / `txt_attn.qkv.weight` / `single_blocks.<i>.linear1.weight` 1:1, so every Kohya key is a `LoraTarget::Direct` — no FusedSlice splitting needed. New unit tests cover all 13 distinct Kohya leaf forms (10 double-block + 3 single-block) and a synthetic safetensors round-trip that proves `LoraAdapter::load` accepts a Kohya-shaped fixture and pairs `lora_down`/`lora_up`/`alpha` into a single layer.
- **Web catalog: FLUX (and any other empty-DB family) now appears in the sidebar, and clicking a card actually opens the detail drawer.** Two regressions stacked on top of the live-search migration. (1) `GET /api/catalog/families` queried only the bulk-scrape DB, so a family whose shard had landed empty (e.g. FLUX after a partial scan rate-limited around Civitai page 11) was silently dropped from the sidebar — even though `/api/catalog/search?family=flux` returned plenty of rows. The endpoint now merges DB counts with the static `Family::ALL_FAMILIES` list so every supported family surfaces with `foundation/finetune` defaulting to 0 when absent. (2) `useCatalog.openDetail(id)` re-fetched `/api/catalog/:id` (DB-only); live-search rows aren't in the DB so the call 404'd and the `void cat.openDetail(...)` site silently swallowed the rejection, leaving `detail.value` null and the drawer unmounted. `openDetail` now uses the entry already in the in-memory list when present (instant open, no extra round-trip), falls back to the DB endpoint only for ids the user pages to without a list view, and clears `detail` on fallback failure instead of throwing. New `families_endpoint_includes_zero_count_families` integration test pins the merge contract; new `useCatalog` vitest cases cover the in-list short-circuit, the fallback-fetch path, and the 404-leaves-detail-null path.
- **LTX-2 single-file Civitai catalog entries (e.g. `cv:2752735`) now generate end-to-end.** Five stacked bugs each broke a different stage; `cv:2752735` produces a 1.17 MB / 768×512 / 25-frame MP4 in ~115 s on a 3090 after this fix. Walking through them: (1) **Header validator was checking the wrong key prefix.** `crates/mold-inference/src/ltx2/single_file.rs` looked for `blocks.*` / `model.diffusion_model.blocks.*`, but Lightricks LTX-2 v2.3 checkpoints use `model.diffusion_model.transformer_blocks.*` (the same prefix LTX-Video uses, and the prefix the runtime's `remap_ltx2_transformer_key` already targets via `vb.pp("transformer_blocks")`). The two single*file modules now share the same prefix; family disambiguation moves entirely to the call site. (2) **`enable_audio` validation triggered on `Some(false)`.** `validate_generate_request` gated on `is_some()` instead of `== Some(true)`, so an explicitly-off audio toggle from the web form tripped `unknown model family; enable_audio is only supported for LTX-2 / LTX-2.3 models` for any catalog model. New `validate_generate_request_with_family(req, family_hint)` accepts a family hint, and the server's `prepare_generation` now calls `model_manager::catalog_family_for(state, model)` to look up the catalog DB's family for `cv:*` / `hf:*` IDs (which the static manifest can't see) before validation. (3) **LTX-2 catalog companions list pulled T5 instead of Gemma.** `companions_for(Family::Ltx2, SingleFile, Checkpoint)` returned `["t5-v1_1-xxl"]`, but the LTX-2 runtime requires the Gemma 3 12B encoder at `paths.text_encoder_files`; T5 is unused (~9.5 GB wasted download AND the runtime still errors). Added a new `ltx2-te` companion (gated `google/gemma-3-12b-it-qat-q4_0-unquantized`, mirroring the `shared_ltx2_files()` set the manifest LTX-2 models pull) and swapped it in. `discovered_manifest_paths` gained an `<models_dir>/.hf-cache/` fallback (companion manifests only — non-companion manifests still require canonical clean-path with the size guard) so a single Gemma TE on disk satisfies both the manifest LTX-2 model AND the catalog `ltx2-te` companion. (4) **`Ltx2Engine::from_single_file` discarded `text_encoder_files`.** The original constructor built a fresh `ModelPaths` with `text_encoder_files: Vec::new()`, throwing away the Gemma path the catalog bridge had resolved. Now takes the full `paths` and only overrides `transformer` / `vae`. (5) **`preset_for_model` couldn't pick a preset for `cv:*` IDs.** `cv:2752735` doesn't contain `ltx-2.3` / `ltx-2` substrings. Extended `single_file::load` to surface `__metadata__.model_version` from the safetensors header, plumbed it through `Ltx2Engine.preset_hint`, and added `preset_for_model_with_hint` so a `"2.3.0"` metadata stamp deterministically selects the 22B preset. (5b) *_TwoStage default required a spatial upsampler asset cv:_ checkpoints don't ship.\__ `select_pipeline` defaulted to TwoStage (which needs `ltx-2.3-spatial-upscaler-x2-1.0.safetensors`) for non-distilled models; now falls back to OneStage when the upsampler isn't on disk. (6) **NVFP4 LTX-2 checkpoints now fail fast with an actionable error.** The LTX-2 runtime has no NVFP4 path (Flux.2 does — `Flux2Linear::Nvfp4Streaming` in `crates/mold-inference/src/flux2/transformer.rs`). cv:2781713 (`ltx23FP4_ltx23OfficialDev.safetensors`) used to surface a deep `expected: [4096, 4096], got: [4096, 2048]` mid-generation; `single_file::load` now detects `_.weight_scale_2`markers and returns`LoadError::Nvfp4Unsupported` with a message pointing at the missing runtime support and naming the marker keys to grep for. New tests across all six layers pin the regressions.
- **Server: model load preflight no longer false-rejects a swap on a 24 GB GPU when an existing model is resident.** Two compounded over-conservatisms were producing `INSUFFICIENT_MEMORY` errors for loads that would actually fit after the active model is evicted: (1) `model_manager::preflight_memory_guard_with_available` estimated peak under `LoadStrategy::Eager`, which sums every component file (`transformer + vae + every encoder + 2 GB headroom`), but every diffusion family in this repo (FLUX, SD3, Z-Image, Flux.2, Qwen-Image, LTX) drop-and-reloads text encoders — real peak is `max(encoders, transformer + vae) + headroom`. For FLUX-dev `:q8` on a 24 GB card, Eager peak ≈ 24 GB tripped the 90 % hard limit; Sequential peak ≈ 14 GB clears it. (2) `preflight_memory_guard` budgeted available memory as `free_vram_bytes(ordinal) + recorded active_vram_bytes`, but the load path runs `cuDevicePrimaryCtxReset_v2` after `unload_active()`, which releases the _entire_ device — not just the recorded delta. A new `device::total_vram_bytes(ordinal)` helper returns the device total; on CUDA the preflight now switches to `total_vram` as the budget whenever an active model exists (since the reset will reclaim everything). macOS unified-memory math is unchanged — Metal has no equivalent context reset, so the additive `available + active_vram` budget stays correct there. Two new regression tests in `model_manager.rs::tests` lock both halves of the fix: `preflight_passes_for_quantized_flux_on_24gb_card_with_swap` and `eager_strategy_would_have_rejected_quantized_flux_on_24gb`.

### Added

- **Flux.2 LoRA support — diffusers + Kohya/sd-scripts naming, BF16 + GGUF + BFL-native single-file paths.** New `crates/mold-inference/src/flux2/lora.rs` mirrors the FLUX adapter pattern (`crate::flux::lora::LoraAdapter` is reused for adapter parsing + `LoraDeltaCache` + `get_or_load_adapter`, so flux2 inherits the parsed-LoRA cache and the multi-suffix safetensors classifier — Diffusers / PEFT canonical / Kohya / OneTrainer / Mochi). Flux.2 LoRAs in the wild (Civitai cv:2682864 / cv:2742432) target **BFL-native module names** with either the PEFT canonical suffix (`diffusion_model.double_blocks.{i}.img_attn.qkv.lora_A.weight`) or the Kohya prefix (`lora_unet_double_blocks_{i}_img_attn_qkv.lora_down.weight`) — both shapes route through a single `map_flux2_lora_key(stem, space)` that produces target descriptors in either the **Diffusers** key space (used by `Flux2Transformer::new` over sharded HF or `SingleFileBackend`-remapped BFL-native single-file checkpoints) or the **BFL** key space (used by `QuantizedFlux2Transformer` for GGUF). Critical asymmetry: Flux.2 BF16 splits double-block QKV into separate `to_q` / `to_k` / `to_v` tensors, but BFL/GGUF keep them fused as `img_attn.qkv` — so a single fused-QKV Kohya LoRA emits **three** `Splat` patches (each occupying one third of `B`) in the Diffusers space and a single `Direct` patch in the BFL space. Single-block fused weight (`to_qkv_mlp_proj` BF16 ≡ `linear1` BFL) has identical `[Q, K, V, MLP_gate, MLP_val]` byte layout in both spaces — a `linear1` Kohya LoRA is one `Direct` patch regardless. Modulation leaves (`*_mod_lin`) skipped silently because Flux.2 modulations are shared across blocks (no per-block target). Pipeline integration: new `pending_loras: Vec<LoraWeight>` on `Flux2Engine` (per-request, mirrors `pending_placement`); `effective_flux2_loras(req)` filters zero-scale entries and routes `loras` (plural) over `lora` (singular); `load_transformer` branches on path × has_lora × lora-aware var-builder construction. New `wrap_backend_with_lora(inner_backend, specs, space, …) -> Box<dyn SimpleBackend>` composes over any inner `SimpleBackend` (mmap'd safetensors directly, or `SingleFileBackend`); new `gguf_lora_var_builder_flux2` mirrors `flux::lora::gguf_lora_var_builder` (dequant → merge → requant per LoRA-affected GGUF tensor, non-LoRA tensors stay quantised). Validator at `mold-core::validation.rs::require_lora_capable_family` extended to accept `flux2`; rejection message updated to list FLUX, Flux.2, and LTX-2; two new positive tests (`lora_on_flux2_accepted`, `loras_plural_on_flux2_accepted`). **Kohya leaves covered**: `img_attn_qkv`, `img_attn_proj`, `img_mlp_0`, `img_mlp_2`, `txt_attn_qkv`, `txt_attn_proj`, `txt_mlp_0`, `txt_mlp_2` (double-block); `linear1`, `linear2` (single-block); `img_mod_lin` / `txt_mod_lin` / `modulation_lin` skipped silently. **Diffusers leaves covered**: `attn.to_q`, `attn.to_k`, `attn.to_v`, `attn.add_q_proj`, `attn.add_k_proj`, `attn.add_v_proj`, `attn.to_out.0`, `attn.to_add_out`, `ff.linear_in`, `ff.linear_out`, `ff_context.linear_in`, `ff_context.linear_out`, `attn.to_qkv_mlp_proj`, `attn.to_out` — every one with both BFL and Diffusers candle-key targets pinned. 21 new unit tests in `flux2::lora::tests`: per-leaf table tests across both key spaces, splat-into-three-targets coverage, alpha-normalises-scale, two-spec stacking on the same target, end-to-end `wrap_backend_with_lora` against synthetic safetensors fixtures (BF16 splat path AND BFL Direct path), `resolve_rows` thirds-split sentinel, classify-key contract for both Kohya and PEFT canonical suffixes, deterministic `lora_path_hash`. Performance optimisation deferred — the FLUX `LoraDeltaCache` and `LoraFingerprint` are accepted but not yet plumbed through Flux.2 (correctness first).
- **SD1.5 LoRA support (BF16/FP16 path, diffusers + Kohya/sd-scripts naming).** The third image-family LoRA path after FLUX and LTX-2 — Civitai's giant SD1.5 LoRA ecosystem now lands directly. New `crates/mold-inference/src/sd15/lora.rs` mirrors the SDXL LoRA pattern (lifted on 2026-05-10): `map_sd15_lora_key(stem)` parses both `lora_unet_*` (Kohya) and `(unet|transformer|diffusion_model.)*` (diffusers / PEFT canonical) shapes onto candle UNet keys; `Sd15LoraBackend` is a `SimpleBackend` that intercepts `vb.get()` and merges `W' = W + scale·(B @ A)` in F32 before casting back to model dtype, so `UNet2DConditionModel::new` loads merged weights without knowing the LoRA exists. Wraps both the diffusers-layout safetensors path and the single-file `SingleFileBackend` (Civitai checkpoints), so cv:_ / hf:_ SD1.5 base models accept LoRA stacks too. Eager-mode UNet is dropped + rebuilt when the requested LoRA stack fingerprint changes (path + scale, ordered) — mirrors the SDXL `active_lora_fingerprint` shape so slider scrubbing and LoRA-swap UX work out of the box. SD1.5 has 4 down/up blocks (vs. SDXL's 3), with attention in 0..=2 / 1..=3 respectively, and one transformer-block layer per attention; the SDXL key helpers parse block indices dynamically so the same routing logic covers both families. Multi-LoRA via `loras: [...]` (plural wins over the legacy singular `lora` field, zero-scale entries are filtered, ordered as supplied — same precedence as FLUX/LTX-2/SDXL). Supported leaves: `attn1.{to_q,to_k,to_v,to_out.0}`, `attn2.{to_q,to_k,to_v,to_out.0}`, `ff.net.{0.proj,2}`, `proj_in`/`proj_out` (spatial transformer), `time_emb_proj` + `conv1`/`conv2`/`conv_shortcut` (resnet leaves observed in full-fine-tune extractions), and `downsamplers.0.conv` / `upsamplers.0.conv`. Text-encoder LoRAs (`lora_te_*`) are skipped silently with a `tracing::warn` per leaf — SD1.5 has only one CLIP-L (no CLIP-G) so the same single-encoder skip rule the validator already applies covers them. Validator extension in `crates/mold-core/src/validation.rs::require_lora_capable_family` accepts `Some("sd15")` alongside `Some("flux")` / `Some("ltx2")` so the HTTP gate produces a clear 200 instead of the prior 422 reject.
- **Z-Image LoRA support (`--lora` / `--loras` on `z-image-turbo:{bf16,q8,q6,q4}` and any `cv:` Z-Image checkpoint).** Z-Image grew the same LoRA wiring FLUX, LTX-2, and Flux.2 already have. New module `crates/mold-inference/src/zimage/lora.rs` (~580 lines + tests) implements a `ZImageLoraBackend` `SimpleBackend` wrapper that intercepts `vb.get()` during `ZImageTransformer2DModel::new` construction and applies `W' = W + scale·(B @ A)` inline. Both the BF16 sharded-safetensors path and the GGUF path (which already dequantises into a dense `HashMap<String, Tensor>` via `gguf_dense.rs` before building the model) flow through the same wrapper — the GGUF route gains a public `dequantize_gguf_dense_tensors` entry point and the LoRA wrapper attaches to the dequantised `HashMap` directly. **BF16 ↔ GGUF asymmetry is handled at the LoRA key-mapping layer, not the model layer**: Civitai Z-Image LoRAs (cv:2904324) target the **fused** `diffusion_model.layers.{i}.attention.qkv` (B is `[3·dim, rank]`), but candle's `ZImageTransformer2DModel` only ever sees split `attention.to_q`/`to_k`/`to_v` — so a fused-QKV LoRA emits **three `Splat` patches**, each carrying the same `B @ A` but slicing rows `[0..h]` → Q, `[h..2h]` → K, `[2h..3h]` → V at apply time. Pre-split LoRAs (`attention_to_q` / `_to_k` / `_to_v`) resolve to `Direct` patches. Recognised LoRA-key shapes: Kohya/sd-scripts (`lora_unet_layers_0_attention_qkv.lora_down.weight`), PEFT canonical with `diffusion_model.` prefix (cv:2904324's actual form: `diffusion_model.layers.0.attention.qkv.lora_A.weight`), root-relative PEFT, OneTrainer, and PEFT default-adapter — all via shared `crate::flux::lora::classify_lora_key` suffix matrix. Covered leaves: `attention_qkv` (splat 3), `attention_to_q`/`_to_k`/`_to_v` (direct), `attention_out` / `attention_to_out_0` (direct on `attention.to_out.0`), `feed_forward.w{1,2,3}` (direct), `adaLN_modulation.0` (direct). Refiner blocks (`noise_refiner.{i}.*`, `context_refiner.{i}.*`) are recognised alongside `layers.{i}.*` for trainers that fine-tune them. Validation: `require_lora_capable_family` in `mold-core/src/validation.rs` extends `flux` / `ltx2` → `flux` / `ltx2` / `z-image`. 23 new unit tests in `zimage::lora::tests` pin the contract — leaf-name coverage for every cv:2904324 Kohya leaf, fused-QKV splat ordering, pre-split direct mapping, PEFT canonical prefix-stripping (`model.` / `diffusion_model.` / `transformer.`), refiner-block recognition, `apply_patch_splat_uses_correct_third_of_delta` (the load-bearing math test), end-to-end mmap + `wrap_backend_with_lora` round-trip in both Splat and Direct paths, alpha-normalises-scale, multi-adapter stacking, unrelated-tensor pass-through. Plus 2 validation tests (`lora_on_zimage_accepted` / `loras_plural_on_zimage_accepted`).
- **SDXL LoRA support — Kohya + diffusers/PEFT key conventions.** New `crates/mold-inference/src/sdxl/lora.rs` ports the FLUX `LoraBackend` pattern to SDXL's UNet (`candle_transformers::stable_diffusion::unet_2d::UNet2DConditionModel`). Civitai SDXL LoRAs ship two key conventions and both are recognised: **Kohya / sd-scripts** (`lora_unet_down_blocks_2_attentions_1_transformer_blocks_0_attn1_to_q.lora_down.weight` — the majority of Civitai SDXL LoRAs, e.g. `cv:244808`, `cv:383563`) and **diffusers / PEFT canonical** (`down_blocks.2.attentions.1.transformer_blocks.0.attn1.to_q.lora_A.weight`, often with a leading `unet.` / `transformer.` / `diffusion_model.` prefix). Both shapes route through `map_sdxl_lora_key(stem)` and resolve to candle UNet candle keys for every observed leaf: self-attention (`attn1.{to_q,to_k,to_v,to_out.0}`), cross-attention (`attn2.{to_q,to_k,to_v,to_out.0}`), feed-forward (`ff.net.0.proj`, `ff.net.2`), SpatialTransformer pre/post (`proj_in`, `proj_out`), and the resnet `time_emb_proj` / `conv1` / `conv2` / `conv_shortcut` leaves observed in full-fine-tune extractions. Adapter parsing reuses `flux::lora::LoraAdapter` so SDXL inherits the multi-suffix safetensors classifier (Diffusers / PEFT canonical / Kohya / OneTrainer / Mochi / PEFT default-adapter) and the FIFO parsed-LoRA cache for free. Pipeline integration: new `SDXLEngine::pending_loras: Vec<LoraWeight>` (per-request, mirroring `pending_placement`) feeds `build_unet_for_strategy` which wraps the UNet's inner `SimpleBackend` (mmap'd diffusers safetensors **or** the single-file `SingleFileBackend` for Civitai checkpoints like Juggernaut/Pony) with `SdxlLoraBackend` — the wrapper intercepts every `vb.get()` and merges `W' = W + scale·(B @ A)` in F32 before casting to the model dtype. Eager-mode UNet invalidation: `SDXLEngine::active_lora_fingerprint: Vec<(String, u64)>` (path + scale.to_bits()) tracks the LoRA stack currently merged into the loaded UNet — on any change (swap, scale, add, remove, reorder) the UNet is dropped so the next `reload_unet_if_needed` rebuilds it with the new wrapper. Plural `loras` wins over singular `lora`; zero-scale entries are filtered (a slider pinned to zero is a no-op patch and shouldn't trigger a rebuild). No GGUF/quantized transformer support yet — SDXL only has the BF16/F16 path in mold. Validator (`mold-core::validation::require_lora_capable_family`) extended to accept `sdxl`; the rejection message now lists FLUX, LTX-2, SDXL. Tests (+20): per-leaf table tests for the full Kohya leaf set across down_blocks (10 leaves), up_blocks (4 leaves), mid_block (3 leaves), per-attention proj_in/proj_out, and per-resnet time_emb_proj; diffusers-canonical leaf coverage with `unet.` / `transformer.` / `diffusion_model.` / `model.diffusion_model.` prefix-strip; end-to-end `wrap_backend_with_lora` against a synthetic safetensors fixture verifying `W' = W + scale·(B @ A)` for both Kohya and diffusers key shapes; alpha normalises scale (`user × alpha / rank`); two specs stacking on same target keep distinct `lora_path_hash`; request normalisation (plural-wins-over-singular, legacy-singular-falls-through, drop-zero-scale); deterministic `lora_path_hash`; `lora_stack_fingerprint` drives UNet drop on stack-change. SD1.5 LoRA support is on the roadmap and will lift this module's helpers — the public-crate surface is structured so SD1.5 can absorb it.
- **LoRA support for Qwen-Image (and Qwen-Image-Edit).** Adds `--lora` / `--loras` to every Qwen-Image-2512 / Qwen-Image-Edit-2511 checkpoint (BF16 sharded safetensors, FP8 single-file, GGUF quantized). New `crates/mold-inference/src/qwen_image/lora.rs` ports the FLUX `LoraBackend` pattern to the dual-stream Qwen-Image transformer: a `SimpleBackend` wrapper merges `W' = W + scale·(B @ A)` inline as `QwenImageTransformer2DModel::new` consumes its `VarBuilder`. The candle module exposes split Q/K/V (`attn.{to_q,to_k,to_v}` for the image stream and `attn.{add_q_proj,add_k_proj,add_v_proj}` for the text stream) on every load path, so split-form Civitai / PEFT trainers map to `Direct` patches, and the rare fused-form trainers (`attn.qkv` / `attn.add_qkv_proj`) splat across three slices via the same row-third sentinel `flux2`/`z-image` introduced. Two on-disk naming asymmetries — BF16 official (`ff.net.*`, `norm1.linear`) vs ComfyUI/GGUF (`img_mlp.net.*`, `img_mod.1`) — are handled by emitting **both** candidate candle keys per LoRA leaf; the wrapper's `get()` only fires whichever key the inner backend serves, so the same LoRA file works against any checkpoint format without a probe step. GGUF transformers route through a sibling `gguf_lora_var_builder` that selectively dequantises every patched tensor to F32 on CPU, merges, and re-quantises back to the original GGML dtype. The block-offload path is intentionally left out of scope for V1 and emits a clear actionable error when LoRAs are combined with `--offload` / `MOLD_OFFLOAD=1`. Validator at `mold_core::validation::require_lora_capable_family` extended to accept `Some("qwen-image")` and `Some("qwen-image-edit")` alongside `Some("flux")` and `Some("ltx2")`; the rejection message now lists FLUX, LTX-2, and Qwen-Image so denied SD1.5 / SDXL / SD3 / Z-Image / Flux.2 users see their alternatives. 30 unit tests in `qwen_image::lora::tests` pin the contract — every recognised PEFT / Kohya leaf for both streams (split + fused), the dual-emit FF / AdaLN coverage, the `Splat` thirds-split math (`apply_patch_splat_uses_correct_third_of_delta`), end-to-end mmap-backend round-trips, and a VAE-tiling pass-through sanity check that confirms `MOLD_VAE_TILED=1` is a no-op for LoRA (the wrapper targets the transformer only).
- **`MOLD_LTX2_GEMMA_VARIANT={auto,q4,bf16}` — Q4 GGUF variant of the LTX-2 Gemma 3 12B prompt encoder.** The historical default loads ~23 GB of BF16 safetensors from `google/gemma-3-12b-it-qat-q4_0-unquantized`, which doesn't fit on a 24 GB GPU alongside the streaming 22B LTX-2 transformer's preflight cap and falls through to CPU under `MOLD_LTX2_GEMMA_DEVICE=auto` (paying a ~30–60 s encode tax on cv:2752735). New `Q4Gguf` backend loads `google/gemma-3-12b-it-qat-q4_0-gguf` (~7 GB on disk and on GPU) — fits comfortably alongside the streaming transformer on a single 24 GB card so auto placement keeps Gemma GPU-resident. New file `crates/mold-inference/src/ltx2/text/gemma3_gguf.rs` implements `GgufGemmaEncoder` against the llama.cpp `gemma3-text` arch (verified tensor names: `token_embd`, `output_norm`, `blk.{i}.{attn_norm,post_attention_norm,ffn_norm,post_ffw_norm,attn_q,attn_k,attn_v,attn_output,attn_q_norm,attn_k_norm,ffn_gate,ffn_up,ffn_down}`); architecture matches the BF16 path exactly — 48 layers, 16 Q heads, 8 KV heads, head_dim 256, hidden 3840, **four norms per block** (Gemma 3 quirk vs. Qwen3 — `input_layernorm` + `post_attention_norm` + `pre_feedforward_layernorm` + `post_feedforward_layernorm`), `xs * sqrt(hidden_size)` embed scaling, **RMSNorm with `(weight + 1.0)`**, sliding-window attention every layer where `(i + 1) % 6 != 0` with `rope_local_base_freq=10000` vs. global RoPE with `rope_freq_base × scaling_factor`. `GgufGemmaConfig` reads block_count, embedding_length, attention head/KV/key counts, sliding window, RMS-norm eps, and RoPE freq/scaling from the GGUF metadata (`gemma3.*`); `sliding_window_pattern=6` and `rope_local_base_freq=10000` are not in metadata and stay hardcoded to match the Gemma 3 reference. New `GemmaHiddenStateBackend` enum in `prompt_encoder.rs` plugs the GGUF encoder behind the same `Vec<Tensor>` hidden-states contract so the downstream `EmbeddingsProcessor` (caption-projection + connectors) is variant-agnostic — `NativePromptEncoder::backend_variant()` exposes the loaded variant for the runtime placement logger. New `GemmaAssets::{gguf_path, has_bf16_weights}` + `discover_gguf` + `resolve_gemma_variant(assets) -> Result<GemmaVariant>` in `crates/mold-inference/src/ltx2/text/gemma.rs` choose the variant from `MOLD_LTX2_GEMMA_VARIANT` (case-insensitive `q4`/`bf16`/`auto`/unset; aliases: `gguf` / `safetensors`) with auto-detection from disk: only-GGUF → Q4, only-BF16 → BF16, both present → BF16 (preserve existing installs; opt into Q4 explicitly), neither → error. The server-side preflight `streaming_transformer_peak` is unchanged — `text_encoder_files` size shrinks naturally from ~23 GB → ~7 GB when users point the catalog at the GGUF, and the existing arithmetic does the right thing. Twenty-eight new unit tests pin the contract: GGUF dump test (gated `#[ignore]` to skip CI), embedding-only forward with `√hidden` scaling, multi-block residual identity with zero weights (proves the four-norm block compose), sliding-window assignment vs. BF16 reference, GGUF discovery (lexically-first determinism), eight resolver-precedence cases (q4-with-and-without-gguf, bf16-with-and-without-safetensors, auto-prefers-bf16-when-both, auto-picks-gguf-when-only-gguf, unset/empty/garbage env), and `backend_variant_reports_safetensors_for_new_constructor`. **Manifest support deferred** — users place the `.gguf` file in their gemma_root manually for V1; auto-fetch is cleaner alongside the catalog repair button work.
- **`MOLD_LTX2_GEMMA_DEVICE={auto,cpu,gpu}` — auto CPU/GPU placement for the LTX-2 Gemma 3 12B prompt encoder.** The LTX-2 transformer streams blocks (preflight peak ~6 GB on a 24 GB card after `0c62bc3`), but the encoder phase still loads Gemma whole (~23 GB BF16 from `google/gemma-3-12b-it-qat-q4_0-unquantized`). On a single 3090 the user previously had to set `MOLD_LTX2_DEBUG_FORCE_CPU_PROMPT_ENCODER=1` to avoid OOM, and on a 2× 3090 host the encoder didn't try the second card even when it had room. New auto placement walks the active GPU first → other GPUs in ordinal order → CPU based on `usable_free_vram_bytes()` against a 24 GB threshold (`LTX2_GEMMA_VRAM_THRESHOLD`); cross-device tensor copy at the end of encode runs through the existing `move_prompt_encoding_to_device` path, ~50–100 ms for the typical conditioning shape. `MOLD_LTX2_GEMMA_DEVICE=cpu` / `=gpu` pins behavior for users who want to opt into CPU offload on a 4090 or pin GPU for a CI run; `=auto` (default) and unset both run the resolver. `MOLD_LTX2_DEBUG_FORCE_CPU_PROMPT_ENCODER=1` continues to work as a deprecated alias and emits a one-shot `tracing::warn!` pointing at the new name. The server-side preflight (`streaming_transformer_peak`) now consults the same resolver and drops `encoder_total` from the GPU peak when the encoder will be CPU-resident, so cv:2752735 admits on a 24 GB 3090 with a 25 GB Gemma TE instead of false-rejecting at the 90 % cap. New helpers in `crates/mold-inference/src/device.rs`: `LtxGemmaPlacement`, `select_ltx2_gemma_device`, `resolve_ltx2_gemma_device_override`, `resolve_ltx2_gemma_placement`. Runtime helper `resolve_prompt_encoder_device` lives in `crates/mold-inference/src/ltx2/pipeline.rs` and is called from `load_runtime_session_on_device`. Twelve new unit tests pin the contract: single-GPU room/no-room, sibling-GPU walk, three-GPU ordinal-order traversal, no-GPUs-discovered, threshold-byte pin, env override matrix (`cpu` / `gpu` / `auto` / case-insensitive / garbage / legacy alias / new-beats-legacy), CPU transformer short-circuit, env-pinned-CPU resolver path, CPU↔CPU `move_prompt_encoding` round-trip, and the preflight admit/reject pair (`preflight_admits_ltx2_22b_with_25gb_gemma_when_resolver_picks_cpu`, `preflight_rejects_ltx2_when_encoder_phase_exceeds_card`).
- **`MOLD_VAE_DTYPE={auto,bf16,fp16,fp32}` — force VAE precision to fix banding on FLUX/SD3-finetuned VAEs.** A long-standing class of artifacts on Civitai FLUX fine-tunes (and occasionally SD3, SDXL) shows up as faint diagonal banding in smooth gradients (skies, skin, bokeh) — the result of VAE conv weights rounding badly at BF16/F16 when the fine-tune drifts away from the calibration the original VAE was trained against. New `crate::device::resolve_vae_dtype(default)` reads `MOLD_VAE_DTYPE` and overrides the per-pipeline historical default; `auto` (or unset) preserves current behaviour bit-for-bit, `fp32` forces full precision, `bf16`/`fp16` are also accepted (case-insensitive, also `f32`/`f16`). Wired into FLUX, FLUX2, SD3, SDXL, and SD1.5 pipelines — both at VAE _load_ time (so the weight tensors arrive at the resolved precision) and at the latent-cast site immediately before `vae.decode()` / `vae.encode()`. The encoded latent is cast back to the engine compute dtype before re-entering the denoise loop, so the rest of the pipeline keeps its natural precision and the only slowdown is the VAE pass itself. **Sequential** load-strategy paths (CLI one-shot, FLUX KEEP*TE_RAM=0) re-resolve per request — env changes take effect on the next `generate()`. **Eager** (server, default) captures the resolved dtype at engine load and persists it on the loaded-state struct (`LoadedFlux::vae_dtype` etc.), so changing the env without `systemctl --user restart mold-server` won't take effect until the next model swap. Behaviour is consistent across Civitai single-file checkpoints and HF diffusers layouts (both share the `build_vae*\*`helpers underneath). VRAM trade-off: forcing fp32 roughly doubles peak decode VRAM (~7 GB → ~14 GB at FLUX 1024²); the existing`crate::vae_tiling::decode_with_oom_fallback`absorbs the increase by retrying with tiles when full-tensor decode OOMs, so 24 GB cards stay safe. **Out of scope**: Z-Image's CPU VAE branch is always F32 (already optimal — the env knob is a no-op there); Wuerstchen's VQ-GAN is hardcoded F32 by design (its decoder operates in pixel space, not latent space, and never had the banding mode); Qwen-Image already forces F32 per-tile in its tiled VAE path so the env knob would be redundant there. New helper + 6 unit tests in`crate::device` (`resolve_vae_dtype_unset_returns_default`, `\_auto_returns_default`, `\_fp32_forces_f32_regardless_of_default`, `\_bf16_forces_bf16_even_when_default_is_f32`, `\_fp16_alias_recognised`, `\_invalid_value_falls_back_to_default`) using a serialized `vae_env_lock` static mutex.
- **CFG++ sampler for SDXL and SD1.5 (DDIM scheduler only).** Extends the SD3 CFG++ landed in #277 to the UNet families. Both pipelines now honour `--cfg-plus` / `MOLD_CFG_PLUS=1` when paired with `--scheduler ddim` (the default for both); `EulerAncestral` and `UniPC` are not supported and emit a one-shot `tracing::warn` plus a fall-through to standard CFG when combined with the flag, so misconfiguration surfaces instead of silently no-op'ing. The DDIM math (Epsilon prediction, eta=0): `x_{t-1} = sqrt(α_{t-1}) · x_0(ε̂_guided) + sqrt(1-α_{t-1}) · ε_uncond` where `x_0(ε̂) = (x_t - sqrt(1-α_t) · ε̂) / sqrt(α_t)`. Standard CFG uses `ε̂_guided` in _both_ the x_0 estimate and the renoise term; CFG++ keeps it for the x_0 estimate and swaps the unconditional row in for the renoise direction — the same manifold-projection trick the SD3 sampler uses, adapted for the DDIM closed form. Why DDIM-only: the candle scheduler trait is opaque (`step()` is the only entry point and `alphas_cumprod` is a private field), so we cannot read α values per-step from outside the scheduler.
