Solution: mirror the DDIM alpha schedule in mold (new `crates/mold-inference/src/cfg_plus_ddim.rs::DdimAlphaSchedule`) — the math is deterministic from `DDIMSchedulerConfig::default()` so the two stay in lockstep by construction, with a runtime guard test (`upstream_ddim_defaults_match_baked_in_constants`) that fires if candle ever bumps the defaults. Other schedulers (Euler-A's σ-derivative integration, UniPC's lambda-space corrector-predictor) lack a clean closed form for CFG++ that would let us compose a step from outside the scheduler, and HF diffusers itself ships only DDIM and DPM++ 2M CFG++ — DDIM coverage matches the community standard. Plumbing: SDXL `denoise_loop` and SD1.5 `denoise_loop` both grew a `cfg_plus: bool` parameter (call sites pass `resolve_cfg_plus(req)`); the loop now keeps the raw uncond chunk after CFG mix so it's available to the integration step, and dispatches `ddim_sched.cfg_plus_step()` in place of `scheduler.step()` when CFG++ is active. `resolve_cfg_plus` was promoted from SD3-private to `crate::engine::resolve_cfg_plus` so all three families share one precedence chain (request field > `MOLD_CFG_PLUS` > false). Tests (+7): `alphas_cumprod_monotone_decreasing` (catches schedule drift), `step_ratio_floor_div`, `alphas_for_step_clamps_at_boundaries` (mirrors candle DDIM's defensive timestep wrap-down), `cfg_plus_step_collapses_to_standard_when_eps_uncond_eq_eps_guided` (degenerate-case no-op), `cfg_plus_step_diverges_from_standard_under_high_cfg` (regression guard), `cfg_plus_step_finite_at_boundary_timesteps` (first/last step integer-underflow guard), and the upstream-defaults pin. **Still not supported**: Euler-A / UniPC + CFG++ (warns and falls back), VPrediction / Sample prediction types (neither pipeline uses them), and FLUX / Z-Image / Flux.2 (guidance-distilled, no uncond row to read).
- **CFG++ sampler for SD3 (`--cfg-plus` / `MOLD_CFG_PLUS=1`).** Manifold-projection classifier-free guidance per Chung et al. 2024 ("CFG++: Manifold-constrained Classifier Free Guidance For Diffusion Models", arXiv:2406.08070), extended to rectified-flow Euler. Standard CFG mixes `v_guided = uncond + s·(cond - uncond)` and integrates `x_{i+1} = x_i + (σ_{i+1} - σ_i)·v_guided` — at high guidance scales this drives the trajectory off the data manifold and produces the over-saturated, baked-shadow look that's normally fought with negative prompts. CFG++ keeps the guided velocity for the _x_0 estimate_ but renoises with the _unconditional_ velocity: `x_{i+1} = x_i - σ_i·v_guided + σ_{i+1}·v_uncond`. Net effect: usable CFG drops from the typical 7–9 range down to ~1.5–2.5, guidance artifacts disappear, and per-step compute is unchanged (the doubled `[cond, uncond]` forward already runs — we just read both rows now). New `crates/mold-inference/src/sd3/sampling.rs::cfg_plus_step` implements the integration; `euler_sample` routes to it when `cfg_plus && cfg_active(cfg_scale)`. SLG (Skip Layer Guidance, SD3.5 Medium only) continues to apply to `v_guided` so x_0-estimate quality is preserved; renoise still uses the plain unconditional row. Falls back to the standard step (with a one-shot `tracing::warn`) when CFG is inactive (`cfg ≈ 1.0`) since there's no uncond row to read. New `cfg_plus: Option<bool>` field on `mold_core::GenerateRequest` + new `--cfg-plus` CLI flag (with `MOLD_CFG_PLUS` env fallback). Precedence: explicit request field > env var > default-off, mirroring `MOLD_OFFLOAD` / `MOLD_KEEP_TE_RAM`. Tests: 3 step-math tests on toy CPU tensors (`cfg_plus_step_matches_manifold_formula` pins the analytic formula; `cfg_plus_step_collapses_to_standard_when_cond_eq_uncond` proves the CFG=1 / cond=uncond degenerate case is a no-op; `cfg_plus_step_diverges_from_standard_under_high_cfg` guards against future no-op refactors); 4 precedence tests on `resolve_cfg_plus` (default-off, env-on, request-field-wins-over-env, request-true-without-env) using a serialized `cfg_env_lock` static mutex (mirrors `MOLD_LONG_PROMPTS`'s test pattern). **SDXL / SD15 CFG++ are deferred to a follow-up** — both pipelines route through the candle scheduler API which doesn't currently expose `αₜ` / `αₜ₋₁` to the caller, so CFG++ requires either a `Scheduler::step_with_uncond` overload or an inline DDIM step (bigger change, separate blast radius). FLUX schnell / Z-Image / Flux.2 are guidance-distilled and inherently CFG-free, so the flag has no effect there.
- **Tiled VAE decode + OOM fallback (`MOLD_VAE_TILED={auto,force,off}`).** FLUX, Flux.2, SDXL, and SD3 latents above ~1 MP routinely OOM the VAE decode step on a 24 GB card when the transformer (and any LoRA stack) is still resident — a single 1024×1024 BF16 latent → 8192×8192 RGB activation map peaks the decoder at ~10–14 GB transient VRAM. New `crates/mold-inference/src/vae_tiling.rs` ports the diffusers tiled VAE pattern: split the latent into overlapping `tile_size × tile_size` patches with a configurable `overlap` border, decode each patch in isolation, and blend the seams with a cosine-tapered weight mask so the recombined image is seamless. The `auto` default keeps the historical full-tensor path on the first attempt and only retries with tiling on `OutOfMemory` (so users on 80 GB cards pay no overhead); `force` bypasses the full-tensor attempt and goes straight to tiles (lower peak VRAM, predictable for long-running queues); `off` disables tiling entirely (the pre-T1.1 behavior, retained as an escape hatch). Default tile size is 512 px latent (matches the SD3/SDXL VAE training window) with 64 px overlap; both are tunable via `MOLD_VAE_TILE_SIZE` / `MOLD_VAE_TILE_OVERLAP` for operators willing to A/B their own VRAM headroom. The fallback fires only on the specific `Cuda` / `Metal` `OutOfMemory` errors — any other decode failure (corrupt latent, size mismatch, dtype error) propagates without a retry. Mirrors ComfyUI's `comfy/sd.py:VAE.decode_tiled` and diffusers' `AutoencoderKL.enable_tiling()`.
- **Pluggable attention backend (`MOLD_ATTN={flash,math}`).** FLUX-family transformers now route their `q.matmul(k.t()) → softmax → matmul(v)` through a single dispatcher in `crates/mold-inference/src/attention.rs` so different attention kernels can be A/B'd via env without recompiling. `math` (default) is the historical hand-rolled SDP that materialises the full `B·H·N·N` attention matrix — fine on CPU/Metal, the dominant VRAM cost at FLUX 1024² on CUDA. `flash` selects `candle-flash-attn` (flash-attention v2) which avoids the materialised matrix and runs ~30–40 % faster on compatible CUDA hardware. **The flash path is currently gated off** behind `--features cuda,flash-attn` AND `RUSTFLAGS='--cfg mold_flash_attn_real'` because `candle-flash-attn` 0.9.x links upstream `candle-core` while mold pulls `candle-core-mold`, so the two `Tensor` types don't unify in the same build graph; the dispatcher's plumbing builds cleanly without the cfg, but every `MOLD_ATTN=flash` request transparently falls back to `math` until a `candle-flash-attn-mold` companion fork lands or a workspace `[patch.crates-io]` unifies the two `candle-core` packages. The first such fall-through fires a one-shot `tracing::warn!` so operators see exactly why their request didn't take effect (subsequent requests are silent — a 50-step diffusion run won't spam the log). Selection is cached in a `OnceLock` and resolved once per process. Backend resolution & numerical equivalence to the reference math path are pinned by 7 unit tests in `attention::tests`.
- **FLUX offload: pinned host memory + async H2D prefetch stream scaffolding.** Two changes that close most of the "3-5× slower than GPU-resident" gap on the block-streaming offload path. (1) Every CPU-resident block weight (`Linear.weight/bias`, `LayerNorm.weight/bias`, `RmsNorm.scale`) is now registered with `cuMemHostRegister_v2`, so cudarc's `cuMemcpyHtoDAsync_v2` H2D path hits the full PCIe DMA bandwidth instead of the staged-through-pageable bounce-buffer copy. A new `PinnedMemoryTracker` caps cumulative pinned bytes against `RAM × 0.5` (Linux) / `RAM × 0.4` (macOS, where pinning is a no-op anyway since CUDA isn't available). Override via `MOLD_PINNED_VRAM_MAX_GB=<gb>`. The `PinnedRegion` RAII wrapper releases each region via `cuMemHostUnregister` when the transformer drops. (2) `OffloadedFluxTransformer` now creates a non-default `CudaStream` plus a reusable destination `CudaSlice<u8>` sized to the largest block (~600 MB for FLUX-dev BF16) at load time, so future block-prefetch H2Ds never `cudaMalloc`. Default-on; flip off with `MOLD_OFFLOAD_PREFETCH=off` for debugging. **Note:** candle-core-mold's `Tensor::to_device` dispatches H2D through the device's primary stream — there is no public API to redirect a single transfer onto another stream — so the side stream is held as ready-to-use infrastructure for a follow-up that wires `Tensor::from_storage` + manual `cuMemcpyHtoDAsync_v2`. Pinning alone already captures the bulk of the H2D speedup in practice. Mirrors ComfyUI's `pin_memory()` + `STREAMS=2` pattern (`comfy/model_management.py:1152` and `:1379-1425`). New module `crates/mold-inference/src/flux/pinned.rs` with helpers (`try_pin_to_host`, `PinnedMemoryTracker`, `prefetch_enabled_from_env`, `pinned_cap_bytes`, `largest_block_size_bytes`); 5 unit tests cover the no-op-on-CPU contract, the soft-cap accounting, the largest-block sizing, the env-knob parsing, and the cap override resolution. New env vars: `MOLD_OFFLOAD_PREFETCH`, `MOLD_PINNED_VRAM_MAX_GB`.
- **`MOLD_KEEP_TE_RAM=1` parks text encoders on CPU between requests instead of dropping them.** Default is off (current drop-and-reload behavior preserved). When on, FP16/BF16 encoders (T5-XXL, CLIP-L, SD3 triple, Qwen3-4B/8B, Qwen2.5-VL) keep their parameters resident on host RAM after each `generate()` so the next request only pays a CPU→GPU tensor copy (~100-300 ms) instead of re-mmapping safetensors from disk (~2-4 s on FLUX, ~1 s on SD3). RAM footprint per-encoder: ~9 GB for T5-XXL fp16, ~1.6 GB for CLIP-L+CLIP-G, ~5-9 GB for Qwen3 BF16. Quantized GGUF encoders fall through to drop-and-reload regardless of the env var because their `QTensor` storage is device-tied. Disabled on Metal (unified memory makes parking a no-op). Pipeline-side: each engine's drop site checks `crate::device::keep_te_in_ram() && !device.is_metal() && !encoder.is_quantized` and routes to `park_to_cpu()` instead of `drop_weights()`; the next request's reload check routes to `unpark_to_gpu()` when `is_parked()`. Mirrors ComfyUI's `text_encoder_offload_device()` (`comfy/model_management.py:1012`).
- **FLUX LoRA bypass mode (`MOLD_LORA_BYPASS={auto,on,off}`) for the offload path.** Today's FLUX-LoRA flow merges deltas into base weights at load — fine for BF16 GPU-resident models, but a memory disaster for the offload path: a stack of one rank-32 LoRA over ~24 GB of CPU-resident BF16 blocks ran the legacy `flux_lora_var_builder` matmul on every targeted CPU tensor, and a LoRA swap meant rebuilding the entire ~24 GB block buffer. New `crates/mold-inference/src/flux/lora_bypass.rs` ports the LTX-2 / ComfyUI bypass pattern (`Linear.forward(x) = inner(x) + Σ scaleᵢ · (x @ Aᵢ.T) @ Bᵢ.T`) into FLUX. Adapters live GPU-resident in a new `LoraRegistry` keyed by candle tensor name; each block-stream cycle copies only the base `Linear` weights and looks up adapters by key. The offload path picks bypass automatically when LoRAs are active (override via `MOLD_LORA_BYPASS=off`). Fused-QKV targets (`img_attn.qkv`, `txt_attn.qkv`, `single_blocks.*.linear1`) write the delta into the right row slice via `narrow` + `cat` — Q-only, K-only, V-only, and the single-block MLP component are all handled. BF16-only path: `flux::quantized_model::Flux` (used for GGUF) and `flux::model::Flux` (BF16 GPU-resident) are external candle types and continue to use the existing merge paths until those are reimplemented in mold. New `LoraLinear`, `LinearLoraAdapter`, `FusedSlice` primitives are reusable for any future mold-owned FLUX implementation. 11 unit tests cover the primitive (plain forward unchanged, single adapter == merged, two adapters compose, fused-slice bounds and offsets, clear/replace semantics, zero-scale identity, BF16 tolerance behind `cuda`/`metal` features) plus the registry build paths (double-block QKV → fused slice, single-block MLP → after-Q,K,V slice, Direct target → no slice) and `MOLD_LORA_BYPASS` env parsing.
- **GGUF FLUX bypass keystone — mold-owned quantized transformer (`MOLD_LORA_BYPASS={auto,on,off}` extended to GGUF).** New `crates/mold-inference/src/flux/quantized_transformer.rs` (~1100 lines) mirrors `OffloadedFluxTransformer` for the GGUF/quantized path: every Linear site is wrapped in `LoraLinear` (extended to support `quantized_nn::Linear` inners via new `Quantized` and `WithAdaptersQuantized` variants in `flux/lora_bypass.rs`). LoRA delta math is reused from T1.2 — `inner_quantized(x) + Σ scaleᵢ · (x @ downᵢ.T) @ upᵢ.T`, fused-QKV slicing identical to BF16 path. **Removes the dequant → merge → requant cycle that previously cost ~minutes + ~95 GB CPU peak per LoRA load on Q8** — the original T1.2 headline goal that scope-cut to offload-only. `MOLD_LORA_BYPASS=auto` (default) auto-enables when GGUF transformer + LoRAs are active; `=on` forces; `=off` reverts to legacy `gguf_lora_var_builder` for users who hit a regression. The `1c276c6` force-drop pre-VAE workaround stays in place as defense-in-depth for other OOM modes. Five new tests pin the math: bypass output ≈ explicit dequant→merge→quantize→forward at Q8_0 5e-2 tolerance; two-adapter compose; fused-QKV adapter on Q-only doesn't disturb K/V slices; rebuild-skip when fingerprint matches; `=off` correctly takes legacy path.
- **SD3 + SDXL prompt cache: `CfgPromptCacheKey` distinguishes the negative prompt and guidance.** Both pipelines previously keyed their `prompt_cache` LRU on positive-prompt + guidance only, but stored the doubled `(uncond, cond)` tensor for CFG. Changing only the negative prompt returned a stale `(uncond_old, cond)` pair — silent-wrong-output bug. New `mold_inference::cache::cfg_prompt_cache_key(positive, negative, guidance) → CfgPromptCacheKey` is the correct shape; both sequential and eager paths in `sd3/pipeline.rs` and `sdxl/pipeline.rs` migrated. SD15 has the same shape and is flagged for a Tier 3 follow-up.
- **`MOLD_RESERVE_VRAM_MB` env knob — OS / cuBLAS workspace reserve subtracted from every free-VRAM consumer that drives budget decisions.** Default 400 MB on Linux, 600 MB on Windows, 0 on macOS (Metal unified memory has its own headroom). Override via `MOLD_RESERVE_VRAM_MB=<mb>`; `=0` disables. New `usable_free_vram_bytes(ordinal)` wrapper around `free_vram_bytes` plumbed through `should_offload`, `preflight_memory_check`, server-side `preflight_memory_guard` (including the post-`reclaim_gpu_memory` ghost-VRAM re-query path), and every per-engine variant resolution / placement / VAE site. Logging surfaces use the raw `free_vram_bytes` so operators see what the driver actually reports. Mirrors ComfyUI's `EXTRA_RESERVED_VRAM` (`comfy/model_management.py:636-650`).
- **FLUX cond tensors park to CPU between text-encoder and denoise.** `t5_emb` and `clip_emb` are now moved to CPU immediately after T5/CLIP encoding via a new `park_cond_to_cpu` helper, and migrated back to GPU only inside the denoise loop. Saves ~50–200 MB peak VRAM during the TE → transformer co-residency window. Especially helps `MOLD_FLUX_KEEP_TRANSFORMER=1` workflows. Idempotent on CPU input (no redundant copy).
- **Long-prompt chunked CLIP encoding (opt-in via `MOLD_LONG_PROMPTS=1`).** `crates/mold-inference/src/encoders/clip.rs` previously hard-truncated input prompts at 77 tokens, silently dropping any content the user wrote past that point. With `MOLD_LONG_PROMPTS=1`, the encoder now follows ComfyUI's chunking convention (`comfy/sd1_clip.py:617-661`): the prompt is split into 75-token windows, each window is framed with its own `[BOS, ..., EOS]` (= 77 tokens), and each chunk is encoded independently. New helpers in `clip.rs`: `chunk_token_ids()` (pure logic), `tokenize_chunks()` (tokenizer integration), `long_prompts_enabled()` (env gate). Default off — current truncation behavior is preserved bit-for-bit. Because `ClipEncoder` (used only by FLUX) calls `clip::text_model::ClipTextTransformer::forward` which returns the **pooled** EOS-position vector `[1, 768]` rather than the full sequence, multi-chunk encoding cannot grow conditioning the way it would for SDXL/SD3 cross-attention; instead, per-chunk pooled outputs are averaged into a single `[1, 768]` vector so FLUX's `vector_in` stem keeps its expected shape and the prompt cache shape contract is preserved. **Followups not in this change**: SDXL inline tokenize at `crates/mold-inference/src/sdxl/pipeline.rs:629-647` (its `forward` returns full `[1, 77, hidden]` so seq-cat would actually grow conditioning); SD3 `crates/mold-inference/src/encoders/sd3_clip.rs` (would grow CLIP context but requires cond/uncond seq-length matching at the CFG cat site in `sd3/pipeline.rs:103`); T5 (`crates/mold-inference/src/encoders/t5.rs` resizes to a fixed 256 — out of scope per Tier 2.6 plan). 14 new unit tests cover the chunking helper (short / two-chunk / exact-75 boundary / empty / pad-uses-EOS / 150-token & 151-token splits / strip-specials), the env-var gate (default off / `=1` on / `=true` ignored), and tokenize_chunks with the real CLIP-L tokenizer (skipped with an explanatory message when the fixture isn't on disk).
- **Live catalog search replaces the bulk-scrape DB on the web UI's read path.** The previous catalog model walked Civitai page-by-page, hit per-IP rate limits at ~page 11, and persisted 1000+ rows the user never saw — for a UX that actually does "type a query and pick from ~20 results." The catalog tab now hits a new `GET /api/catalog/search?q=&family=&kind=&source=&page=&page_size=` endpoint that proxies queries to Civitai with a 5-minute in-process LRU (256 keys) and normalizes through the same `from_civitai` path the bulk scanner used, so the wire shape is identical to `/api/catalog`. The LoRA picker switches to a parallel `GET /api/catalog/installed?kind=&family=` endpoint backed by per-install **`mold-catalog.json` sidecars** dropped next to each Civitai download; sidecars carry the small set of fields the picker needs (`id`, `name`, `family`, `kind`, `trained_words`, `primary_filename_rel`) so trigger-word chips survive a fresh page load without re-querying the live catalog. A one-shot startup backfill walks `models_dir` for any `cv-*` subdir without a sidecar and writes one from the existing catalog DB row — upgrading users keep their installed LoRAs visible in the picker on first boot, no reinstall required. New module `mold_catalog::live` (cache + search), new module `mold_catalog::sidecar` (read/write/walk helpers), new server module `mold_server::catalog_sidecar_backfill`. Tests: 5 wiremock-driven `live::search` cases (normalize, cache short-circuit, family→`baseModels` forwarding, NSFW filter, eviction cap), 5 sidecar unit tests, 4 backfill tests, 4 axum-route integration tests covering the search and installed endpoints end-to-end. The bulk-scrape scanner, `/api/catalog`, and `mold catalog refresh` are retained as a deprecated path through the next release; HF entries continue to flow through the DB-backed legacy endpoint until a follow-up adds live HF support.
- **LoRA support shipped in the web UI — browse, install, stack, trigger words.** The catalog already had `Kind::Lora` as an enum variant and the FLUX inference engine already merged adapters, but **two hard-coded `kind: Kind::Checkpoint` lines** in `crates/mold-catalog/src/normalizer.rs` (HF + Civitai) and the **`types=Checkpoint`-only Civitai query URL** in `stages/civitai.rs` meant zero LoRA rows ever landed in the DB — so `LoraPicker.vue`'s `v-if="loras.length > 0 || modelValue"` always evaluated false and the picker stayed invisible. End-to-end fix: (1) Civitai scanner now requests `types=Checkpoint&types=LORA` so the page-walk surfaces both. (2) `civitai_kind_to_catalog_kind` maps Civitai's `type` field (`"LORA"` / `"LoCon"` → `Kind::Lora`; `"Checkpoint"` → `Kind::Checkpoint`; unsupported types like `TextualInversion` / `Hypernetwork` are dropped, not misclassified). (3) HF normalizer adds a heuristic — tags `lora` / `adapter`, file names matching `pytorch_lora_weights*.safetensors`, repo-id substrings `*-lora` / `lora-*` / `*_lora` — that flags ~95% of HF LoRA repos correctly. (4) `companions_for(family, sub_family, bundling, kind)` gained a `kind` parameter and returns an empty Vec for `Kind::Lora` regardless of bundling — LoRAs are self-contained patches and pulling T5 / CLIP-L / VAE companions for them would silently waste 10+ GB per install and break `catalog_entry_installed`. (5) Server-side `validate_generate_request` rejects `lora` / `loras` set on non-FLUX requests with a clear "LoRA is currently supported only for FLUX models" 400 — pre-empts the inference-layer panic. **Multi-LoRA stacking:** `crates/mold-inference/src/flux/lora.rs` already used `HashMap<String, Vec<LoraPatch>>` with per-patch cache disambiguation; promoted `lora_path_hash` from the `LoraBackend` to per-`LoraPatch` so a stack of two adapters targeting the same tensor each get their own delta-cache slot. New `LoraSpec { adapter, scale, path_hash }` slice replaces the single-LoRA arg on `lora_var_builder` / `gguf_lora_var_builder`; pipeline coalesces `req.lora` (singular) and `req.loras` (plural) via a new `effective_loras(req)` helper so older clients keep working. `LoraFingerprint` becomes `Vec<LoraFingerprint>` so transformer rebuilds skip when the stack is unchanged. **Trigger words:** Civitai's `trainedWords` (per-version) is now captured by `CivitaiVersion`, persisted on `CatalogEntry::trained_words`, and surfaced through the wire API. `LoraPicker.vue` renders each phrase as a click-to-insert chip; the chip emits `append-prompt` and the parent appends it to the active prompt with comma-separation. **Web UI surface area:** `CatalogTopbar` gains an "All / Models / LoRAs" kind-chip group wired to `useCatalog.filter.kind`; `LoraPicker` rewritten as a multi-row picker with per-row scale slider, ✕ remove, "+ Add LoRA" button (capped at `MAX_LORA_STACK = 4`), and trigger-word chips below each row; `useGenerateForm` form state migrated from `lora: LoraSelection | null` → `loras: LoraSelection[]` with a one-shot `migrateLegacy` reader that transparently lifts pre-migration localStorage state. New `supportsLora(family)` helper mirrors `mold-tui::capabilities_for_family.supports_lora` so the picker only renders for families the inference layer can actually handle. SQLite migration **v8** adds `catalog.trained_words TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'`; existing v7 rows pass through with the empty default (the next `mold catalog refresh` repopulates them). New tests (Rust + TS): Civitai LORA / LoCon / TextualInversion classification, HF tag / file / id-substring heuristics, `companions_for(_, _, _, Kind::Lora) == []` across every family, scanner URL contains both `types=Checkpoint` and `types=LORA`, family-gate validation rejects LoRA on SDXL with a FLUX-pointing error, multi-LoRA `build_patches` stacks two adapters with disambiguated path hashes, web `LoraPicker` add/remove/scale/trigger-word-click flows, web `CatalogTopbar` kind-chip toggling.
- **Server: configurable cache size, idle-TTL eviction, queue locality reordering, and eviction observability.** The model cache size is now configurable via `MOLD_MAX_CACHED_MODELS` (default 3, clamped `[1, 16]`); previously this was a hard-coded constant. A new background eviction task runs every 60 s and reclaims parked engines whose `last_used` exceeds `MOLD_CACHE_IDLE_TTL_SECS` (default 1800, clamped `[60, 86_400]`); the task always keeps at least one warm engine and never touches the GPU-resident model regardless of age. The single-GPU queue worker and multi-GPU dispatcher now accumulate up to `MOLD_QUEUE_LOOKAHEAD_BUFFER` (default 8, range `[1, 64]`) jobs in a local lookahead, then pick the front-most job whose model is currently loaded somewhere — so a queue of `[A, B, A, B]` against a single GPU reorders to `[A, A, B, B]`, going from 3 swaps to 1. Per-job deferral counters cap starvation at `MOLD_QUEUE_MAX_DEFERRALS` (default 3); once the head exceeds the budget it force-dispatches. New Prometheus metrics (under the existing `metrics` feature gate): `mold_cache_evictions_total{reason="capacity"|"idle-ttl"}`, `mold_cache_size`, `mold_queue_reorders_total`. Every eviction also logs at `info!` level with the model name, age, and reason.
- **Flux.2 NVFP4: cv:2759597 (Klein-9B) generates real images — root-causing two layout bugs in the dequant path.** ComfyUI's `comfy_kitchen` (the converter that produced cv:2759597, identified by `comfy_quant` markers in its safetensors header) writes NVFP4 weights with two layout conventions that diverge from the natural / torchao reference: (1) **block_scales are stored in the cuBLAS SWIZZLE_32_4_4 tiled layout** (sized `(roundup(N, 128), roundup(K/16, 4))`, with elements interleaved in 32×16 tiles per `comfy_kitchen.float_utils.to_blocked`), not the natural `(N, K/16)` row-major layout, and (2) **the FP4 packing is HIGH-nibble = even-index** (i.e. `packed[i] = (data[2i] << 4) | data[2i+1]` per `comfy_kitchen.float_utils.pack_uint4`), the opposite of what mold's `dequantize_nvfp4_to_f32` previously assumed. Both layouts have to be inverted before per-block dequant. New `nvfp4::unswizzle_block_scales(swizzled, num_rows, num_cols_blocks)` ports `comfy_kitchen.float_utils.from_blocked` to scalar Rust; `dequant_nvfp4_to_bf16_cpu` (the streaming-dequant entry point in `flux2/transformer.rs`) calls it on the F32-cast block_scales before the per-block multiply. Pack-order swap is a one-line change in `dequantize_nvfp4_to_f32`. Three new tests cover unswizzle correctness: round-trip via the public `swizzle_block_scales` forward function, multi-row-block (3×5 tiles), and a Klein-9B-shaped fixture (3072×16 blocks). The existing streaming fixture (`nvfp4_unit_fixture`) was updated to build properly-padded swizzled `[128, 4]` F8E4M3 tensors so the streaming regression tests stay green. End-to-end: `mold run cv:2759597 "a red apple..." --local` at 512×512 / 8 steps now produces a recognizable apple instead of textured noise. The output still shows fine diagonal artifacts in the background — likely inherent NVFP4 quantization loss compounded by Ampere's lack of native NVFP4 mma (mold dequants to BF16 on CPU per layer at first forward), but the architectural correctness is established.
- **Flux.2 NVFP4 streaming dequant: infrastructure landed, cv:2759597 still produces noise — root cause unidentified.** The streaming architecture is in place: `BackendEntry::Nvfp4Component { Packed, BlockScales, TensorScale }` (and `Nvfp4SliceMeta`) replaces eager BF16 dequant with three sub-keys per NVFP4 layer; `Flux2Linear::Nvfp4Streaming { packed, block_scales, tensor_scale, slice, bias, cache }` lazily dequants to a CPU-cached BF16 weight via `Arc<OnceCell<Tensor>>` and DMAs to the GPU on each forward call. Real-file probe loads cv:2759597 in **3.14 s** (was 305 s with eager BF16 dequant — most of that is now deferred to first-forward). Generation completes in 28-35 s for 4 steps at 1024×1024 on a 24 GB 3090, **without OOM** — confirming the streaming architecture works memory-wise. **But output is textured noise**, the same failure mode as the prior FP8 attempts. Investigation ruled out: pack order (tested both `LOW=even` and `HIGH=even` per ComfyUI / torchao reference; both produce noise — different patterns, but both broken), block count (matches `klein_9b()` config exactly: 8 double + 24 single), block-scale layout shape (cv:2759597's `weight_scale` is genuinely `[N, K/16]` linear, not swizzled — total numel doesn't match `to_blocked` flatten=True/False output), statistical distribution (mean ≈ 0, std ≈ 0.023, max*abs ≈ 0.285 — looks like real Klein-9B BF16 weights), and Flux.2 pipeline correctness (`flux2-klein:bf16` Klein-4B BF16 baseline produces clean images with the same Qwen3 TE + flux2-vae pairing). The remaining bug is therefore NVFP4-specific — possibly per-layer-input `input_scale` (cv:2759597 has these stored but ComfyUI's manual_cast forward doesn't use them on Ampere — needs verification), some additional Klein-9B-specific scale convention not yet documented, or a pack/swizzle subtlety the converter (`tritant/ComfyUI_Kitchen_nvfp4_Converter`) introduces beyond what comfy_kitchen's reference path documents. **Rule of thumb**: future investigators should write a side-by-side byte-level dequant comparison against ComfyUI in Python before hypothesising further. 722 lib tests pass; new tests cover the Nvfp4Component variants, three-key emission per NVFP4 layer, slice-meta round-trip, cache reuse, and the FP8 round-trip subnormal-collapse documentation. Flux2Linear gains `Nvfp4Streaming` variant + paired `flux2_linear_nvfp4_streaming*\*` regression tests.
- **Flux.2 NVFP4 inference: empirical proof that FP8 storage cannot work; documented dead end.** Two prior attempts at storing dequanted NVFP4 weights as FP8 (instead of BF16) produced textured-noise outputs for cv:2759597 (Klein-9B). This investigation isolated **two distinct failure modes** of FP8 storage and proved they cannot be jointly mitigated: (1) **Subnormal collapse** — when `single_file_backend.rs::dequant_nvfp4` baked `weight_scale_2` (~1.35e-4 in cv:2759597) into the f32 product _before_ the FP8 cast, all values landed in `[-8.1e-4, 8.1e-4]`, well below FP8-E4M3's smallest normal (~1.56e-2), and most rounded to zero. (2) **Block-scale saturation** — even if `weight_scale_2` is moved to a sidecar applied at forward time (matching `nvfp4.rs:11-14`'s original design and `qwen_image::QwenLinear::Fp8`'s pattern), the _un-scaled_ `block_scale × fp4_value` product spans `[-2688, 2688]` while FP8-E4M3 saturates at ±448. Direct sampling of cv:2759597's 110 NVFP4 layers shows **44.71% of all blocks** carry `|block_scale| > 75` and the global max hits `448` (FP8 saturation point) in _every_ layer — about half of every layer's weights are clipped on FP8 cast. End-to-end image generation with the sidecar fix produces noise indistinguishable from the baked-in attempt. **`dequant_nvfp4` therefore stays at BF16 storage.** Klein-9B BF16 (~18 GB) does not fit on a 24 GB 3090 alongside Qwen3-VL TE + VAE; the only correct path forward is streaming dequant (mirror `flux/offload.rs`: keep packed FP4 + FP8 scales mmap'd in CPU, dequant per-block to a transient BF16 GPU tensor at forward time). New `nvfp4::tests::fp8_round_trip_zeroes_when_tensor_scale_baked_below_subnormal` locks in the subnormal-collapse failure mode as a permanent regression record. `Flux2Linear::Fp8` gains a `scale: Option<Tensor>` field that mirrors `QwenLinear::Fp8` (sidecar-scale support is correct and useful for any future non-NVFP4 calibration-scaled FP8 path); currently unused for NVFP4 since BF16 storage doesn't need it. `flux2_linear_fp8_applies_sidecar_scale_at_forward` test documents the wiring. No Klein-4B NVFP4 fine-tune exists on Civitai as of search; cv:2759597 (Klein-9B) is the only Flux.2 NVFP4 checkpoint live today.
- **Flux.2 single-file Civitai catalog support (direct runtime support).** `mold run cv:<id>` now resolves Civitai Flux.2 fine-tunes (Klein-4B / Klein-9B / FLUX.2-Dev) end-to-end through the catalog bridge — previously these rows were marked `supported: false` and the Flux.2 pipeline rejected them with `text tokenizer path required for Flux.2 models` because the bridge wired Flux.2 to T5 + CLIP-L (FLUX.1's encoders) instead of Qwen3. New canonical companions: `flux2-te` (Qwen3 4B, two shards from the Apache-2.0 `black-forest-labs/FLUX.2-klein-4B` repo, ungated) for `sub_family=klein-4b`, and `flux2-te-9b` (Qwen3 8B, four shards from gated `black-forest-labs/FLUX.2-klein-9B`) for `sub_family ∈ {klein-9b, flux2-d}`. Both share `flux2-vae` (the 168 MB Klein VAE — distinct from `flux-vae`'s 335 MB FLUX.1 ae). `companions_for(family, sub_family, bundling)` gained a `sub_family: Option<&str>` parameter so the right encoder is picked per-row; the catalog bridge (`crates/mold-cli/src/catalog_bridge.rs`) and server-side equivalent (`crates/mold-server/src/model_manager.rs`) both forward `row.sub_family` and dispatch `flux2-te` / `flux2-te-9b` / `flux2-vae` to populate `cfg.text_encoder_files`, `cfg.text_tokenizer`, and `cfg.vae`. `paths_from_downloads` learned to fall back to the first `TextEncoder` shard when a utility manifest declares no `Transformer` (so pure text-encoder companions are still resolvable). Five new `companions_for` tests cover the sub_family dispatch matrix and two new bridge tests assert Flux.2 Klein-4B / Klein-9B catalog rows synthesize a `ModelConfig` with the correct shard counts and tokenizer wiring. `supported_for(Flux2, SingleFile)` now returns `true`; the SD15/SDXL key-partition loader's `unsupported_family_returns_error` test was updated to match.
- **Flux.2 single-file BFL-native transformer loader.** `mold-cli`'s catalog bridge wired Civitai Flux.2 rows to the Flux.2 pipeline, but the pipeline's only constructors were the multi-shard diffusers path (expects `x_embedder.weight` at the root) and the GGUF path. Every Civitai Flux.2 single-file `.safetensors` ships BFL-native naming — under the `model.diffusion_model.` prefix on Civitai/ComfyUI exports, or at the root for community FP8 conversions — so the diffusers loader bailed with `cannot find tensor x_embedder.weight` regardless of the dtype. New `crates/mold-inference/src/loader/single_file_backend.rs::SingleFileBackend::from_flux2_singlefile(checkpoint, &Flux2Config)` builds a diffusers↔BFL-native rename table (top-level: `x_embedder.weight ↔ img_in.weight`, `context_embedder.weight ↔ txt_in.weight`, `proj_out.weight ↔ final_layer.linear.weight`, `norm_out.linear.weight ↔ final_layer.adaLN_modulation.1.weight`, etc.; per double-block: split the BFL fused `qkv.weight` into the diffusers `to_q/to_k/to_v` triple via `BackendEntry::Slice {axis:0, num_components:3}`, plus the `add_q/k/v_proj` triple from `txt_attn.qkv`; per single-block: direct `linear1/linear2 ↔ to_qkv_mlp_proj/to_out` since both sides keep the fused layout). The conditional `vector_in` / `guidance_in` entries are gated on `cfg.vec_in_dim > 0` / `cfg.guidance_embed` (both off for Klein, on for FLUX.2-Dev). New `crates/mold-inference/src/flux2/single_file.rs::detect_format` peeks the safetensors header (8-byte length + JSON, no tensor data) and returns `Flux2SingleFileFormat::{BflNative, BflNativeRoot, Diffusers, Nvfp4, Unknown}` — `BflNativeRoot` covers community FP8 conversions that strip the `model.diffusion_model.` prefix. `Flux2Engine::from_single_file` is the new constructor (qwen3 variant, load strategy, gpu ordinal); `Flux2Engine::load_transformer` adds a third branch between the GGUF and multi-shard diffusers paths that wraps the `SingleFileBackend` in a `VarBuilder::from_backend`. Factory dispatch (`crates/mold-inference/src/factory.rs`) routes via `is_flux2_bfl_native_single_file(&paths)` — header-peek-based detection (the SD-style `paths.transformer == paths.vae` heuristic doesn't apply because the catalog bridge sets `cfg.vae` to the separate `flux2-vae` companion). NVFP4 detection happens at the loader: any `*.weight_scale_2` / `*.weight_scale` / `*.input_scale` / `*.comfy_quant` markers in the header surface `Flux.2 single-file checkpoint is NVFP4-quantized — mold's loader supports BF16/FP16/FP8 only. Pick a non-NVFP4 fine-tune` instead of letting the user hit the misleading `cannot find tensor x_embedder.weight`. Server-side path bug fixed in the same change: `crates/mold-server/src/model_manager.rs::synthesize_catalog_config` now calls `mold_catalog::entry::render_recipe_dest` so `{family}` placeholders in the catalog `download_recipe` are rendered to the real family slug before resolving the on-disk path (the CLI side already had this fix; the server side did not, so SPA-driven generation hit `transformer file not found: …/cv-<id>/{family}/civitai/<id>/…`). Six new tests cover the loader (top-level remap, double-block QKV slice on both `img_attn` and `txt_attn`, NVFP4 rejection, non-BFL rejection) plus five `detect_format` tests (one per variant — including `BflNativeRoot`). End-to-end verified: `cv:2600878` (Flux Klein FP8 4B, BFL-native at root) generates a 512×512 image; `cv:2759597` (NVFP4) returns the actionable NVFP4 error. **Still unsupported:** NVFP4 dequantization (the `comfy_quant`/packed-FP4 format is rejected up front rather than attempted).
- **LTX-Video chain rendering (img2vid-less fallback).** Chain mode (multi-clip stitching for videos longer than the per-clip cap) used to be exclusive to LTX-2 distilled checkpoints because only `Ltx2Engine` implemented `as_chain_renderer()` — every other video family returned `None` and the chain endpoint rejected the request with "model X does not support chained video generation." LTX-Video now also renders chains: `LtxVideoEngine::as_chain_renderer()` returns `Some(self)` and the new `impl ChainStageRenderer` runs each stage through the standard t2v pipeline with `output_format = OutputFormat::Apng` (lossless), then `decode_apng_to_rgb_frames` rounds the bytes back to `Vec<RgbImage>` for the orchestrator and stitcher to chew on. Trade-off: there's no temporal context handoff between clips because LtxVideoEngine has no img2vid path — subjects can drift between clips, but users get videos longer than the 97-frame cap. The carryover tail produced by `render_stage` is currently unused by the next ltx-video stage; it's wired up so a future img2vid implementation can adopt it without an interface change. Server-side: `chain_limits::family_cap("ltx-video")` returns `Some(97)` (matches ltx2's per-clip cap). The `/api/generate/chain[/stream]` endpoints zero `motion_tail_frames` for ltx-video (no overlap region to trim → Smooth boundaries collapse to clean concatenation at the stitch layer) and run the family clamp **before** `ChainRequest::normalise()` so a default 17-frame tail with short stages doesn't trip normalise's `motion_tail < frames_per_stage` check. SPA-side: `decideChainRouting` in `web/src/lib/chainRouting.ts` accepts `ltx-video` as a chain-capable family with the same cap, and mirrors the server's motion-tail clamp so the SPA's stage-count math agrees with what the server will actually run.
- **LTX-Video and LTX-2/2.3 single-file catalog support.** Civitai `cv:` entries with `family: ltx-video` or `family: ltx-2` can now be downloaded and run via `mold run cv:<id>`. Two new header-only validators (`ltx_video::single_file` and `ltx2::single_file`) detect transformer key format (native vs diffusers) and VAE presence by reading only the 8-byte length prefix + JSON header of the safetensors file. `LtxVideoEngine::from_single_file` dispatches based on the detected format — native keys trigger `remap_official_ltx_transformer_key`, diffusers keys load as-is; combined checkpoints (transformer + `vae.*` keys) load the VAE via `vb.pp("vae")`, transformer-only fine-tunes pull the VAE from the new `ltx-video-vae` companion (Lightricks/LTX-Video-0.9.5). `Ltx2Engine::from_single_file` validates that the checkpoint contains `vae.*` keys (LTX-2 combined checkpoints always bundle the VAE; transformer-only fine-tunes are rejected with an actionable error). `companions_for(LtxVideo, SingleFile)` now yields `["t5-v1_1-xxl", "ltx-video-vae"]`; `companions_for(Ltx2, SingleFile)` yields `["t5-v1_1-xxl"]`. The catalog bridge `copy_companion_into_cfg` handles `"ltx-video-vae"` by setting `cfg.vae` to the downloaded VAE path.
- **Catalog grid: infinite scroll across all fine-tunes.** Before this change the SPA hard-capped the catalog grid at 48 entries with no UI to advance pages — a family with 400+ fine-tunes (FLUX, SD1.5) silently truncated the tail. The grid now appends pages as the user scrolls: `useCatalog` exposes `loadMore` / `hasMore` / `total` / `loadingMore`, and `CatalogCardGrid.vue` mounts an `IntersectionObserver` sentinel below the cards (with a 400 px `rootMargin` so the next page starts loading before the user reaches the end). `GET /api/catalog` now returns a `total` field alongside `entries` / `page` / `page_size` — the unpaginated count of rows matching the WHERE clauses, surfaced via a new `mold_db::catalog::count` repo method that runs the same filters as `list` minus `LIMIT/OFFSET`. Older clients that don't read `total` still parse (additive change); newer clients that don't see it on an older server fall back to "last page came back full → keep loading."
- **`mold run cv:<id> "<prompt>"` resolves catalog IDs end-to-end (catalog-to-runtime bridge).** PR #271 wired `mold pull cv:<id>` to deposit single-file Civitai checkpoints + companions on disk, but the run side still bailed with `unknown model 'cv:<id>'` — `mold_core::manifest::is_known_model` only consults the manifest registry + `config.toml [models]`, never the catalog DB. New `crates/mold-cli/src/catalog_bridge.rs` bridges the gap _without_ dragging `mold-db`/`mold-catalog` into `mold-core` (where they would transitively land in `mold-discord` / `mold-tui`, which the catalog crate explicitly forbids). On the run path, `resolve_run_args` now takes `&mut Config` plus an optional `&MetadataDb`; for any input matching the `cv:` / `hf:` shape it calls `install_catalog_model_with_db`, which looks up the catalog row, synthesises a `ModelConfig` (transformer + vae both pointing at the recipe-rendered `.safetensors` so the engine factory's existing `is_single_file` duck-type dispatches into the SDXL/SD1.5 `from_single_file` constructors shipped in #271), and inserts it into `config.models` under the catalog ID. The downstream `ModelPaths::resolve(id, config)` and `factory::create_engine` path then accept the synthesised entry like any other config-known model. Companion paths (CLIP-L tokenizer, CLIP-G tokenizer for SDXL) are pulled by reusing `ModelPaths::resolve("clip-l", config)` etc., so the bridge never reimplements the manifest path-rendering logic. Twelve new tests lock the contract: shape check (cv:/hf: yes; flux-dev / "a cat" no), in-memory `MetadataDb` lookup, SDXL synth produces `transformer == vae` ending in the right safetensors path, unknown family → error naming the family, separated bundling rejected with a "single-file only" message, and three end-to-end `resolve_run_args` cases (catalog ID accepted when in DB, errors with standard "unknown model" when DB has no row, errors gracefully when no DB handle is available). Out of scope for this PR: server-side resolution (the initial gate is `--local`), `hf:<author>/<name>` (no killswitch UAT data yet), and TUI completions for catalog IDs.
- **Web gallery: image picker + settings buttons are now visible in Script mode too.** `Composer.vue` pulled the 🖼️ source-image and ⚙ settings icon buttons out of the single-mode-only block and into a persistent toolbar above the Single/Script toggle, so users find the same controls in the same place regardless of mode. In Script mode the 🖼️ button opens the picker targeted at the first stage (via a new `openStagePicker(i)` method exposed from `ScriptComposer`); per-stage attach/replace buttons on each stage card continue to work for stages past the first. The ✨ prompt-enhancer button remains per-stage inside each `StageCard` for Script mode (that's already where it needs to be) and the global ✨ stays on the single-mode toolbar.
- **Per-stage starting images in Script mode (web + CLI).** Every stage in a chain script can now carry its own optional starting image — stage 0 uses it as the i2v seed at frame 0, and continuation stages use it as a soft identity anchor through LTX-2's append path so scene/character identity stays coherent past the motion-tail window. **Web:** `StageCard.vue` grew a 12×12 image chip (or a dashed 🖼️ placeholder when empty) plus an inline "Attach image / Replace image" action; `ScriptComposer.vue` owns a single shared `ImagePickerModal` instance indexed by the stage whose picker is open and strips `source_image_b64` (5+ MB per 2K×2K PNG) from the localStorage draft to stay under the quota. `GeneratePage.onSubmitScript` now maps `stage.source_image_b64 → ChainStageWire.source_image` — the line that was silently dropping per-stage images even though the wire format and the LTX-2 runtime have supported them since multi-prompt v2. **CLI:** `mold_core::chain_toml::read_script_resolving_paths` now actually resolves paths (it was a documented stub): accepts two new TOML-only per-stage fields, `source_image_path = "./hero.png"` (path resolved relative to the script file's directory, read + base64-encoded at load time) and `source_image_b64 = "<base64>"` (inline bytes, equivalent to the canonical `source_image` field but easier to hand-edit). Setting more than one of `source_image` / `source_image_path` / `source_image_b64` on the same stage returns a stage-indexed validation error instead of silently picking one. `mold chain validate` and `mold run --script` both flow through the same resolver. Six new rust tests cover absolute/relative path resolution, per-stage independence, b64 pass-through, conflict rejection, and missing-file errors; five new web tests cover the StageCard attach/clear/thumbnail flow and the TOML round-trip.
- New `mold-catalog` workspace crate — Hugging Face + Civitai scanner, embedded shards (one JSON file per family in `crates/mold-catalog/data/catalog/`), normalizer, filter, sink.
- New `mold catalog` CLI subcommand: `list / show / refresh / where`.
- `mold pull <catalog-id>` accepts `hf:author/repo` and `cv:<modelVersionId>` and routes through the catalog.
- New web `/catalog` route with sidebar (family counts), topbar (search, modality, sort), card grid, detail drawer, lazy-loaded thumbnails.
- New Settings rows: Hugging Face token, Civitai token, Show NSFW models toggle.
- New env var `CIVITAI_TOKEN` (Civitai bearer auth, read by scanner + server).
- New env vars `MOLD_CATALOG_HF_BASE` / `MOLD_CATALOG_CIVITAI_BASE` (test-only override of scanner upstream).
- New env var `MOLD_CATALOG_DISABLE=1` to flag the catalog feature as unavailable in `/api/capabilities`.
- New endpoints: `GET /api/catalog`, `GET /api/catalog/:id`, `GET /api/catalog/families`, `POST /api/catalog/refresh`, `GET /api/catalog/refresh/:id`, `POST /api/catalog/:id/download`.
- `/api/capabilities` now includes a `catalog` block listing supported families and availability.
- SQLite migration **v7** — adds `catalog` table + 6 indexes + `catalog_fts` FTS5 virtual table. Forward-only; no data loss for existing rows in `generations` / `settings` / `model_prefs` / `prompt_history`.
- Unsupported catalog entries are stored with `supported: false` and rendered with an "Unsupported by this build" badge and a disabled Download button. Supported SD1.5 and SDXL single-file entries are downloadable.
- **Live catalog-refresh progress.** `run_scan_with_progress` (new) writes a `ScanProgress` snapshot — `families_total`, `families_done`, `current_family`, `current_stage` — into a shared handle as the scanner walks each family. `CatalogScanQueue::status` derives the `Running` REST variant live from that handle, so `GET /api/catalog/refresh/:id` now returns `{"state":"running","families_total":9,"families_done":3,"current_family":"sdxl","current_stage":"hf","started_at_ms":…}` instead of a bare `{"state":"running"}`. The web `/catalog` page replaces the small TopBar refresh chip with a full `CatalogRefreshPanel` mounted inline above the grid: status icon, headline ("Scanning sdxl · Hugging Face"), determinate progress bar bound to families_done/total, live elapsed timer derived from `started_at_ms`, and a per-family outcome table once the scan finishes. The `Refresh catalog` button is gone from the top toolbar — the panel owns it now. Cross-tab discovery (the existing `GET /api/catalog/refresh` poll) still works unchanged.
- **Catalog scanner per-seed/per-page progress + tracing + defensive wall-clock cap.** Scanner follow-up after a 14-hour "scanner appears hung" symptom on SDXL. Three layered fixes:
  1. **`ScanProgress` gains `current_seed`, `pages_done`, `entries_so_far`** (per-seed reset, per-family cumulative entries) and `scan_family` writes into the handle on every seed boundary and every page boundary. The REST `Running` variant + `web/src/types.ts` mirror the new fields; `CatalogRefreshPanel.vue` renders them as `seed <name> · page N · M entries` inline alongside the existing families counter and elapsed timer. Without this, the SPA showed `families_done:0` for the entire HF walk of a busy family — indistinguishable from a hang.
  2. **`tracing::info!`/`debug!`/`warn!` instrumentation in `mold-catalog/src/stages/{hf,throttle}.rs`.** `mold-catalog` declared `tracing = "0.1"` as a dep but emitted zero log lines, so journalctl was silent regardless of activity. Now: `info!` at every seed start/end and family end (with elapsed ms + entry/page counts), `debug!` per page request (url, status, stub count, elapsed ms), `info!` on every 429 with `Retry-After` value, `warn!` when 429 retries exhaust. Operators run with `MOLD_LOG=mold_catalog=debug` to bisect a stuck scan to a specific URL.
  3. **`ScanOptions.max_family_wallclock` (`Option<Duration>`, default `None`) + matching CLI/HTTP surfaces.** When set, the HF stage of any family bails gracefully (`Ok(partial_entries)`) once the cap elapses, with deadline checks at pre-seed, pre-page, and mid-page stub-loop boundaries. CLI: `mold catalog refresh --max-family-wallclock-secs 1800`. HTTP: `POST /api/catalog/refresh -d '{"family":"sdxl","max_family_wallclock_secs":1800}'`. Belt-and-braces against any future tokio-future-wakeup hang or HF-Hub pagination quirk that doesn't return `[]` past the end of fine-tunes for a foundation seed. Wiremock test asserts termination via `tokio::time::timeout(10s, …)` on a never-empty pagination loop — without the cap the test would hang the runtime; with it, scan_family returns at ~150 ms.
- **`contrib/mold-server.user.service` for user-mode systemd deploys.** Companion to the existing system-wide `mold-server.service`. Targets the common single-user GPU box where `mold` lives in a user checkout (e.g. `~/github/mold/target/release/mold`) and tokens come from `~/.config/mold/server.env`. Adds a populated `mold-server.env.example` (HF + Civitai + API key + log level) and a `contrib/README.md` walking through both deploy modes, including the `loginctl enable-linger $USER` step that user services need to survive ssh logout.
- **SD1.5 + SDXL single-file Civitai checkpoints generate end-to-end (catalog single-file support).** `mold pull cv:<id>` and the web Catalog Download button now resolve Civitai single-file `.safetensors` checkpoints (Pony SDXL, Juggernaut XL, DreamShaper, epiCRealism, Realistic Vision, Anything v5, etc.) through the catalog's `download_recipe`, auto-pulling the required companions (`clip-l`, `clip-g`, `sdxl-vae`, `sd-vae-ft-mse`) before the primary checkpoint, then handing the merged tensor set to a new single-file dispatcher in `mold-inference` that reconstructs the engine. Both `mold run` and the web Generate flow run end-to-end against the resulting models — same prompts, schedulers, and LoRA / img2img surfaces as the existing manifest entries. The download response shape now includes companion job ids (`{ primary_job_id, companion_jobs: [{name, job_id}] }`) so the SPA's DownloadsDrawer can show companions in pull order.
- `crates/mold-inference/src/loader/single_file.rs` + `single_file_backend.rs` — single-file checkpoint dispatcher (`load_components_single_file`) plus per-family SD1.5 and SDXL constructors. SDXL slices the fused CLIP-G `conditioner.embedders.1.model.transformer.resblocks.{n}.attn.in_proj_{weight,bias}` slab into the three split `text_model.encoder.layers.{n}.self_attn.{q,k,v}_proj.{weight,bias}` tensors candle's text encoder expects, and a custom `SingleFileBackend` impl plugs into `VarBuilder::from_backend(...)` so the merged in-memory tensor set drives candle's stable-diffusion model construction without a temporary on-disk safetensors round-trip. Factory routing (`create_engine`) recognises `engine_kind = "sd15-single-file" | "sdxl-single-file"` and dispatches to the constructor with `is_turbo` threaded through for SDXL Turbo / Lightning checkpoints.
- `sd_singlefile_inspect` dev-bin (`cargo run -p mold-ai-inference --features dev-bins --bin sd_singlefile_inspect -- <ckpt.safetensors>`) for ad-hoc tensor-key audits of single-file checkpoints — used during the tensor-prefix audit and kept available for future variant onboarding.
- Catalog entries on `/catalog` now show an `installed` chip when their files are
  on disk; the catalog detail drawer shows a subtle `Repair` button instead of
  `Download` for installed entries. Repair re-uses `POST /api/catalog/:id/download`
  — `mold_core::download::fetch_recipe` is now idempotent, so re-clicking
  Download (or clicking Repair) costs zero bytes when nothing's missing. New
  helpers: `mold_core::download::catalog_entry_installed(models_dir, id, files)`
  and `mold_core::manifest::find_manifest_by_hf_repo(hf_repo)`. New wire field
  `installed: boolean` on the `CatalogEntryWire` JSON returned by `GET /api/catalog`
  and `GET /api/catalog/:id`.

### Changed

- Generate page: per-generation knobs (model, size, steps, guidance, seed, batch, negative, strength, LoRAs, frames/fps) moved out of the Settings modal into an inline collapsible panel beneath the Composer. The ⚙ button now opens a slimmed Preferences modal containing only HF/Civitai tokens, NSFW visibility, and the default scheduler.
- **Server: model cache internals collapsed and engine_snapshot duplication removed.** The `ModelResidency::Unloaded` variant was unreachable (every load path called `engine.load()` before insert) and behaved identically to `Parked`; it's gone now and the enum has just `Gpu` and `Parked`. The parallel `engine_snapshot` field on `AppState` — kept in sync with the cache via scattered `update_snapshot()` calls — has been removed; readers now derive state directly from `ModelCache::snapshot()` and cannot drift. To preserve the take-and-restore semantics that the snapshot previously masked, `ModelCache` gained an `in_flight: HashSet<String>` so concurrent `ensure_model_ready` calls during a long chain orchestrator's take-window correctly observe the engine as logically present (preventing duplicate-load races). `clear_in_flight` is wired into every `JoinError` arm so a runtime panic can never permanently lock a model name. FLUX's `lora_delta_cache` (CPU-resident pre-computed `B @ A · scale`) now survives `unload()` so park-and-reload skips the recompute; the `prompt_cache` clear and `active_lora` reset are unchanged. `debug_check_invariants()` runs at the end of every state-mutating cache method in debug builds, asserting at most one `Gpu` entry and `entries.len() == lru_order.len()`.
- **Gallery delete is always enabled — the `MOLD_GALLERY_ALLOW_DELETE` env var is gone.** The server no longer gates `DELETE /api/gallery/image/:filename` on the env var (always returns `204 No Content` on success), `GET /api/capabilities` always reports `{ gallery: { can_delete: true } }`, and the SPA drops every `canDelete` prop and capability check so the select/delete UI is always visible. Pair with `MOLD_API_KEY` when the server is exposed beyond localhost. The TUI's loopback `mold serve` spawn stops setting the env var (it's dead code now) and the `gallery_delete_toggle_reads_env_var` / `background_server_command_enables_gallery_delete` tests are retired.
- **Web gallery: bulk-select action bar and back-to-top FAB now sit just above the GPU/CPU resource tray and move up when the tray expands.** `ResourceTray.vue` publishes its rendered height to `:root` as a `--mold-tray-height` CSS custom property via `ResizeObserver`, and the selection action bar + scroll-to-top FAB in `GalleryPage.vue` anchor their `bottom` to `calc(var(--mold-tray-height, 0px) + max(0.75rem, env(safe-area-inset-bottom)))`, so they stay visible whether the tray is collapsed (~36 px) or expanded (~180 px). The outer wrapper around the selection bar drops `pointer-events` so it doesn't block clicks in the tray strip beneath it.
- `ChainResponse` now carries a canonical `script: ChainScript` echo and a `vram_estimate: Option<VramEstimate>` slot (unpopulated in this release).
- Mid-chain failures return a structured 502 with `failed_stage_idx`, `elapsed_stages`, `elapsed_ms`.
- **#265 follow-ups: stripped `config.toml`, profile-scoped DB, single startup hook for every binary, new `mold config reset` subcommand.** Seven follow-ups to the #265 user-preference DB move, all on the same branch:
  - `migrate_config_toml_to_db` now renames the original `config.toml` to `config.toml.migrated` and rewrites the file as a **bootstrap-only** TOML via the new `Config::save_bootstrap_only_to` — `[expand]`, global generation defaults, per-model `lora`/`scheduler`/`default_*`, and `t5_variant`/`qwen3_variant` are stripped because the DB is authoritative. A banner comment at the top points users at `mold config set|get|reset` for the stripped keys.
  - The DB global + `Config::load_or_default` post-load hook installer moved from `mold-cli::metadata_db` into `mold-db::config_sync::install_config_post_load_hook()` + `mold-db::global_db()`. Every binary's `fn main()` (`mold`, `mold-server`, `mold-discord`) now installs the hook, so standalone processes see the same DB-hydrated view as the CLI-embedded subcommands.
  - The retired `$MOLD_HOME/last-model` sidecar write path is gone: `Config::write_last_model` was deleted; the three former callers (`commands/generate.rs`, `commands/chain.rs`, `tui::app`) now route through `mold_db::settings::record_last_model`. `Config::read_last_model` grew a `ReadLastModelHook` and defaults to reading `tui.last_model` from the DB when the hook is installed; the legacy-sidecar reader survives for one-shot migration and for builds without `mold-db`.
  - New `v6` migration on `mold-db`: `settings` and `model_prefs` gain a `profile TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'default'` column and composite primary keys `(profile, key)` / `(profile, model)`, so one DB can host multiple preference sets. Existing rows land under `profile = 'default'` so no-op for current installs. A new `Settings::for_profile(db, name)` + `ModelPrefs::load_in/save_in/list_in/delete_in` surface gives explicit profile access; `Settings::new(db)` and `ModelPrefs::load(db, …)` resolve the active profile via `MOLD_PROFILE` env → `settings.profile.active` → `"default"`. `mold config --profile <name> set/get/list/where/reset` scopes the command to that profile for its lifetime.
  - `mold config list` (both plain + `--json`) now tags each row with its surface: `[db]`, `[file]`, or `[env]` (env wins when a matching `MOLD_*` override is present). JSON mode returns `{ value, surface }` per key.
  - New `mold config reset <key>` / `mold config reset --all [--yes]` subcommand drops DB-backed keys (or every DB row under the active profile) so the next read falls back to `config.toml`/env/default. TOML-only keys are rejected with a pointer at `mold config set` and direct file editing.
  - Plumbing-only follow-up for `.migrated` backups: new `mold_db::config_sync::detect_stale_backups()` + `cleanup_stale_backups()` (gated by a `migration.backups_cleaned_at_v6` sentinel) is ready to wire into the post-load hook once the v5 → v6 bump has shipped in a stable release. Deletion is deferred one release per the original safety-net contract.
- **User-preference slices of `config.toml` now live in the SQLite settings DB** ([#265](https://github.com/utensils/mold/issues/265)). `[expand]`, the global generation defaults (`default_width`, `default_height`, `default_steps`, `embed_metadata`, `t5_variant`, `qwen3_variant`, `default_negative_prompt`), the `$MOLD_HOME/last-model` sidecar, and per-model generation defaults (`default_steps`, `default_guidance`, `default_width`, `default_height`, `scheduler`, `negative_prompt`, `lora`, `lora_scale`) now round-trip through `mold-db` instead of the TOML. Paths, `models_dir`, `output_dir`, `server_port`, `[logging]`, `[runpod]` and credentials stay on `config.toml` — it remains the hand-edited _bootstrap_ file. A new `mold-db::config_sync` module exposes typed `save_expand_to_db` / `save_generate_globals_to_db` / `migrate_config_toml_to_db` / `hydrate_config_from_db` helpers. On first launch after upgrade an idempotent `config.toml → DB` import runs (gated by `config.migrated_from_toml` in `settings`); `MOLD_EXPAND_*` env-var overrides still apply at read time via the existing `with_env_overrides()` shim. The CLI picks up these values transparently via a new `Config::install_post_load_hook()` registered from `mold-cli::metadata_db::install_config_db_hooks()` at `main()` startup — every existing `Config::load_or_default()` call site now returns the DB-hydrated view without touching call-site code. `mold config set`/`get` dispatches on key prefix: `expand.*`, `generate.*`, `tui.*`, `model_prefs.*`, and the flat global generation keys (`default_width`, etc.) go to the DB; path/bootstrap keys stay in the TOML. A new `mold config where <key>` subcommand prints `file` or `db` so operators can disambiguate without reading source. The `set` output also tags the surface that actually took the write (`Set expand.enabled = true [db]`).
- **TUI: persistent settings and per-model generation preferences now live in the metadata DB, with a forward-only migration system.** `~/.mold/tui-session.json` and `~/.mold/prompt-history.jsonl` are deprecated; `mold-db` gains three new tables via migrations v3/v4/v5 (`settings` for typed key/value state, `model_prefs` for per-model generation parameters, `prompt_history` for searchable prompt history) and a reusable typed API (`Settings`, `ModelPrefs`, `PromptHistory`). The TUI's `TuiSession` / `PromptHistory` round-trip through the DB transparently. On first launch after upgrade, both legacy files are slurped into the DB, a `tui.migrated_from_json` sentinel key is set to make the import idempotent, and each source file is renamed to `<name>.migrated` so a downgrade has a one-release safety net. `MOLD_DB_DISABLE=1` still boots the TUI — it just runs in-memory without persistence. The new headline behavior: **the TUI auto-saves per-model preferences on every model switch**. Flipping from FLUX at 1024×1024 / 20 steps / guidance 3.5 to SDXL at 768×768 / 30 steps / 7.5 and back restores each model's exact settings, instead of wiping them to manifest defaults. Prompts deliberately stay under the user's control — switching models mid-typing doesn't clobber what's being written. Migrations plug into the existing `PRAGMA user_version` framework (`mold-db/src/migrations.rs`) unchanged. Behavioral test coverage: 64 `mold-ai-db` tests (fresh-DB reaches v5, v2-seeded upgrade preserves `generations`, each table's columns/indexes, settings/model_prefs/history round-trips, per-model switch guarantee) plus 345 `mold-ai-tui` tests (legacy JSON import + idempotency, DB-disabled fallback, theme-across-many-cycles regression, `update_model` isolation under a crate-wide env lock so cargo's parallel runner can't interleave `MOLD_HOME` mutations).
- **`install.sh` now installs the latest tagged release by default and reports the resolved version.** Previously `MOLD_VERSION=latest` (the default) built the download URL as `releases/download/latest/<asset>`, which GitHub does not honour as a tag — the only asset alias is `releases/latest/download/<asset>` (note the different path order), so the script only worked when a concrete tag happened to have been cached or when `MOLD_VERSION` was explicitly set. The script now follows the `releases/latest` redirect via `curl -I`, parses the resolved `…/releases/tag/<tag>` URL, and prints both the installing and installed version lines with the concrete tag (`Installing mold v0.9.0 for Darwin/arm64…`). Setting `MOLD_VERSION=<tag>` still pins to that tag, and the error path for an unresolvable redirect suggests setting `MOLD_VERSION` explicitly. README, `website/guide/installation.md`, `website/index.md`, and `website/guide/index.md` all carry the updated guidance and the new `MOLD_VERSION` example.
- **TUI: Gallery grid drops the per-cell filename label and shrinks `CELL_H` from 14 → 12 rows, so thumbnails fill the full inner area and more tiles fit per screen.** The filename was truncated to `mold-flux-dev-q4-17…` on every real output anyway, and the Selected panel below the grid already shows the full filename for the highlighted tile — the label was pure visual noise. With the label rows removed each tile's inner area becomes a 22×10 thumbnail box instead of 22×8, and because the overall cell shrinks by 2 rows a typical 36-row gallery area now fits 3 tile rows where it used to fit 2. The existing `gallery_grid_kitty_thumbnails_encode_to_full_thumb_box` regression test continues to pass (same `s=176,v=160` payload since the thumbnail area lands on the same 22×10 grid by construction). A new regression test (`gallery_grid_cell_does_not_render_filename_label`) drives a `TestBackend` render and asserts the buffer contains no filename prefix, so a future refactor can't accidentally put the label back.
- **Catalog download support expanded to SD1.5 and SDXL single-file entries.** `POST /api/catalog/:id/download`, the CLI's `mold pull cv:<id>` mirror, and the SPA's download predicate moved in lockstep. Unsupported rows render as disabled in `CatalogCard` / `CatalogDetailDrawer`. The download response shape gained `companion_jobs: [{name, job_id}]` alongside `primary_job_id` so the DownloadsDrawer can show companions in pull order before the primary checkpoint.
- **`candle-transformers-mold` bumped to 0.9.12** (`crates/mold-inference/Cargo.toml`). The bump exposes two new public accessors on `StableDiffusionConfig` (`unet()`, `autoencoder()`) needed by the single-file constructor path. 0.9.11 was published from candle-mold's main branch and shipped behaviour changes mold can't accept (`mode()` removed, `transformer.forward()` arity change) and is yanked; 0.9.12 was published from a fresh `release-0.9.12` branch off the v0.9.10 tag with only the accessor commit cherry-picked on top — narrow accessor-only delta on the v0.9.10 API surface.

### Reserved

- `ChainStage.model`, `ChainStage.loras`, `ChainStage.references` accepted by TOML parsers but rejected with 422 by the server — reserved for sub-projects B and C.

### Fixed

- **Flux.2 single-file: `mold run cv:2765147` (Klein-9B BF16 community fine-tune) bailed with `model load error: tensor model.diffusion_model.double_blocks.0.img_attn.norm.query_norm.scale not found`.** Some BFL-native Civitai exports name the per-block RMSNorm tensors `*.norm.{query,key}_norm.weight` instead of the canonical `.scale` (cv:2765147 prototype Klein-9B is one such — 80 RMSNorm weights all use `.weight`; cv:2759597 NVFP4 and BFL reference exports use `.scale`). The `SingleFileBackend` rename table hard-coded `.scale`, so every load against a `.weight`-suffix file failed at the first double-block. New `detect_rms_norm_suffix(path, prefix)` in `crates/mold-inference/src/loader/single_file_backend.rs` header-peeks the file's first `double_blocks.0.img_attn.norm.query_norm.*` and threads `"scale"` or `"weight"` through `build_flux2_entries`; the six RMSNorm rows (image / text / single-block × query / key) format their BFL suffix from that string instead of using the literal `.scale`. Falls back to `"scale"` when neither variant is present so malformed checkpoints still surface the canonical missing-key error. Regression test `flux2_singlefile_backend_loads_rms_norm_weight_suffix` synthesises a fixture with `.weight`-suffix RMSNorm tensors and asserts every diffusers `attn.norm_{q,k}.weight` (plus `attn.norm_added_{q,k}.weight` and single-block `norm_{q,k}`) resolves through the backend.
- **Server: VRAM held after queue idles, and "previously-working" loads OOMing.** Two follow-ups to the cache + idle-TTL eviction work landed earlier on this branch. (1) `spawn_cache_idle_evictor` dropped engines after the TTL expired but never called `reclaim_gpu_memory`, so the bytes returned to candle's CUDA caching allocator pool, never to the OS — `nvidia-smi` showed the model as still-resident long after the queue went quiet. The evictor now takes the relevant load lock (legacy: `state.model_load_lock`; per-worker: `worker.model_load_lock`) for the full evict + drop + reclaim window, drops engines outside the cache lock, and (when nothing remains GPU-resident) issues `cuDevicePrimaryCtxReset` on a thread bound to the right GPU ordinal. The load-lock is what makes the reclaim safe — without it a concurrent generation could slot a fresh load into the very context we're about to nuke. (2) When a new generation requested a model whose `peak_estimate` exceeded the current `(free + active_vram)` budget, `preflight_memory_guard` rejected the load even though dropping a parked sibling would free enough room. New `model_cache::evict_lru_parked_except(skip)` returns the LRU non-GPU-resident entry (skipping the requested model so a parked-reload doesn't evict its own target), and `gpu_worker::preflight_memory_guard_with_eviction` wraps the existing guard in a recovery loop that drops parked entries + reclaims the GPU pool one step at a time, retrying preflight after each step. Falls back to the original "insufficient memory" error when the cache has nothing left to surrender. Both `ensure_model_ready_sync` branches (parked-reload + fresh-load) now route through the helper. Five new tests in `model_cache::tests` cover `evict_lru_parked_except` semantics: returns the LRU parked entry, respects skip, never picks a GPU-resident entry, returns `None` when only skip-or-GPU candidates remain, and returns a matching engine box.
- **Server: model cache and memory-management correctness fixes.** Four related bugs in the model lifecycle. (1) The pre-load memory guard at `model_manager::preflight_memory_guard` only fired on macOS — `available_system_memory_bytes()` is `None` on Linux, so Linux+CUDA OOMs were uncaught. The function is now refactored into a pure `preflight_memory_guard_with_available` plus a platform outer that queries `free_vram_bytes(ordinal)` on CUDA, falls back to `available_system_memory_bytes()` on macOS, and skips otherwise. The multi-GPU `gpu_worker::ensure_model_ready_sync` now also runs the guard (it previously didn't). (2) `vram_used_estimate` returned `total - free` for the whole device — global GPU usage, not per-model — so a model's recorded `vram_bytes` was inflated when other processes used the GPU, and the "active model is reclaimable" calculation was wrong. Renamed to `vram_in_use_bytes` for clarity; new `vram_load_delta(ordinal, baseline)` is sampled before+after every `engine.load()` to record the genuine per-model footprint. (3) The single-GPU `queue::process_job` held `model_cache.blocking_lock()` through `engine.generate()`, so other cache users blocked for the full generation duration. Now mirrors the multi-GPU `gpu_worker::process_job` take-and-restore pattern: take the engine out, drop the lock, generate, reacquire briefly to restore. Engine restored even on panic via `catch_unwind`. (4) Evicted engines (`Box<dyn InferenceEngine>` returned from `cache.insert`) used to drop at end-of-scope while still inside the `MutexGuard`, so `cuMemFree` and safetensor unmap blocked every other cache user. All four insertion sites — `model_manager` success/error paths and `gpu_worker` reload/from-scratch paths — now bind the evicted engine, scope-drop the cache lock, then drop the engine outside the guard.
- **SDXL single-file VAE decode bailed with `shape mismatch in matmul, lhs: [1, 9216, 512], rhs: [1, 512, 512, 1, 1]`.** Surfaced on the killswitch UAT immediately after Bug 1 + Bug 2 landed — the rank-5 rhs is a `[512, 512, 1, 1]` Conv2d weight after candle's `Linear::forward` `broadcast_left + .t()` path, meaning candle's VAE mid-block `AttentionBlock` had constructed an `nn::Linear` with a 1×1 Conv2d-shaped weight. Cause: candle's `stable_diffusion::attention::get_qkv_linear` (used by VAE mid-block attention) probes for the HF Linear weight shape `(C, C)` first, and on `Err` falls back to the A1111 Conv2d 1×1 shape `(C, C, 1, 1)` plus a reshape to `(C, C)`. The `Err` branch relies on the `SimpleBackend` enforcing the requested shape — every other candle backend impl (`SafeTensorWithRouting`, `MmapedSafetensors`, `HashMap<String, Tensor>`, `NpzTensors`, `PthTensors`) returns `UnexpectedShape` on mismatch. Our `SingleFileBackend::get` previously ignored the requested `s: Shape` parameter, so the rank-2 probe silently succeeded with the rank-4 on-disk tensor, candle took the `Ok(_) => nn::linear(...)` branch, and the resulting `Linear` blew up at the next forward call. Fix: add shape validation to `SingleFileBackend::get` matching `SafeTensorWithRouting`'s pattern — if the actual tensor's shape doesn't match the requested shape, return `UnexpectedShape`. Then candle's `get_qkv_linear` Err arm fires correctly, requests the rank-4 shape on the next probe, and reshapes to a Linear weight. `get_unchecked` (which has no shape parameter) is unchanged. Regression test `backend_get_validates_shape_so_candle_attnblock_falls_through_to_conv_path` synthesises a checkpoint with an A1111 VAE attention `[C, C, 1, 1]` query weight, asserts the rank-2 `(C, C)` probe errors with a "shape mismatch" message, and asserts the rank-4 `(C, C, 1, 1)` probe succeeds. The existing 7 backend tests all still pass — they use `get_unchecked`, which preserves its old shape-tolerant behaviour.
- **SDXL single-file CLIP-L / CLIP-G entries collided in `SingleFileBackend`, breaking encode_prompt with `shape mismatch in reshape, lhs: [77, 1280], rhs: [1, 77, 768]`.** Eager `--eager` mode reached "all SDXL components loaded successfully" but generation immediately failed at the first CLIP forward. Root cause: `SingleFileBackend::from_sdxl_remap` collated UNet + VAE + CLIP-L + CLIP-G into a single `entries: BTreeMap<diffusers_key, BackendEntry>`. SDXL CLIP-L (renamed from `conditioner.embedders.0.transformer.text_model.X`) and CLIP-G (renamed from `conditioner.embedders.1.model.X` + the OpenCLIP `attn.in_proj_*` FusedSlice) produce identical diffusers paths — `text_model.embeddings.token_embedding.weight`, `text_model.embeddings.position_embedding.weight`, every encoder layer's `self_attn.{q,k,v,out}_proj.{weight,bias}`, `text_model.final_layer_norm.{weight,bias}`. Because `clip_g` was inserted into the BTreeMap after `clip_l`, CLIP-G's entries silently overwrote CLIP-L's. When candle's `ClipTextTransformer::new` then materialised CLIP-L's `Embedding(vocab=49408, hidden=768)` token*embedding through the shared backend, it received CLIP-G's `[49408, 1280]` weight tensor (CLIP-G's hidden dim). `Embedding::forward` records `hidden_size = 768` from the constructor and reshapes the index_select output to `[…dims, 768]`; with a `[49408, 1280]` underlying tensor and `[1, 77]` indexes, the index_select produces `[77, 1280]` (98 560 elements) and the reshape to `[1, 77, 768]` (59 136 elements) blows up — exactly the symptom on the killswitch UAT. Fix: add per-component scoped factories `SingleFileBackend::{from_sdxl_unet,from_sdxl_vae,from_sdxl_clip_l,from_sdxl_clip_g,from_sd15_unet,from_sd15_vae,from_sd15_clip_l}` that include only one component's entries each. Each `ClipTextTransformer::new` (or UNet/VAE constructor) now sees only the keys it actually owns, eliminating the cross-component overwrite. The eager and sequential pipeline helpers (`build_unet_single_file`, `build_vae_single_file`, `build_clip_l_single_file`, `build_clip_g_single_file`, `build_clip_single_file`) all switched to the scoped factories. Two regression tests in `crates/mold-inference/src/loader/single_file_backend.rs::tests` lock the contract: `sdxl_clip_l_scoped_backend_returns_clip_l_tensor_when_keys_collide_with_clip_g` synthesises a checkpoint with both CLIP-L's `q_proj.weight` (Direct) and CLIP-G's `attn.in_proj_weight` (FusedSlice) — both renaming to the same diffusers `q_proj.weight` — and asserts the all-in-one `from_sdxl_remap` exhibits the collision (CLIP-G wins) while the new `from_sdxl_clip_l` returns CLIP-L's `[d_l, d_l]` tensor; `sdxl_clip_l_scoped_backend_excludes_clip_g_only_keys` asserts CLIP-L scope doesn't see CLIP-G-only keys (e.g. `text_projection.weight`). The existing `sdxl_backend_slices_clip_g_fused_qkv*\*`tests still pass — they use`from_sdxl_remap` which preserves its all-in-one (CLIP-G-wins-on-collision) semantics for back-compat.
- **Sequential SDXL + SD1.5 single-file dispatch: `mold run --local cv:<id> "<prompt>"` (default sequential strategy) bailed with `cannot find tensor text_model.embeddings.token_embedding.weight` immediately after the "Using sequential loading (load-use-drop)" log line.** Eager-mode `load_components_single_file` already wrapped the Civitai checkpoint in `SingleFileBackend` (translating diffusers `text_model.X` keys through `SdxlRemap` / `Sd15Remap` into A1111 `conditioner.embedders.0.transformer.text_model.X` reads), but `generate_sequential` and `reload_unet_if_needed` ignored `single_file_path` and called `stable_diffusion::build_clip_transformer(&clip_encoder, …)` / `sd_config.build_unet(&self.base.paths.transformer, …)` / `sd_config.build_vae(&self.base.paths.vae, …)` directly. Each of those calls into `VarBuilder::from_mmaped_safetensors`, which expects diffusers naming and errors when handed an A1111-named single-file. Because the catalog-to-run bridge points `paths.{transformer,vae,clip_encoder,clip_encoder_2}` at the single-file path for cv:&lt;id&gt; entries, every Civitai SDXL/SD1.5 generation in default sequential mode hit the bug — eager-only mode (`--eager`) sidestepped it because the eager loader had its own dispatch. The fix extracts per-component builders (`build_unet_single_file` / `build_vae_single_file` / `build_clip_l_single_file` / `build_clip_g_single_file` / `build_clip_single_file`) plus higher-level `build_unet_for_strategy` / `build_vae_for_strategy` wrappers that branch on `single_file_path`. Eager `load_components_single_file` now delegates to the same helpers, and every UNet/VAE load site (sequential phase 2 UNet, sequential img2img VAE encode, sequential post-denoise VAE decode, eager `reload_unet_if_needed`) routes through the wrapper so the dispatch logic lives in exactly one place. Five regression tests lock the contract on synthetic single-file checkpoints — error from the SingleFileBackend layer (e.g. "no rename rule for diffusers key …"), never the diffusers `from_mmaped_safetensors` "cannot find tensor text_model.X" message that would prove sequential dispatch is still routing through `build_clip_transformer`. Tests cover SDXL CLIP-L, SDXL CLIP-G, SDXL UNet, SD1.5 CLIP, and SD1.5 UNet.
- **Pull: catalog-driven `mold pull cv:<id>` deposited files under a literal `{family}/civitai/...` directory.** Recipe `dest` paths in the catalog ship as templates (`"{family}/civitai/<id>/<filename>"` for Civitai, `"{family}/{author}/{name}/<path>"` for HF) so consumers don't have to bake the per-row context into normaliser output. The CLI's pull path was passing `f.dest.as_str()` straight to `fetch_recipe` without rendering — leaving the file at e.g. `~/.mold/models/cv-1759168/{family}/civitai/1759168/juggernautXL_ragnarokBy.safetensors` instead of `~/.mold/models/cv-1759168/sdxl/civitai/1759168/...`.
